Headless coverage: new `test/gui/test_navigation_statusbar.py` (7: status bar, stepper skips separators + bounds, reporter discovery, task drives bar + close keeps last message, Cancel fires callback + flag, scoped-logger routing + detach, logged-line-survives-task) and 3 new `burst_analysis` workflow tests (h2mm role wired, h2mm adopts folder via downstream, BVA progress→shell bar); burst suites 79 pass. Commit 2 (Browser + ndX see BVA/2CDE `…4` companions) pending.
* **FCS model UX follow-up (PRD-62): live diffusion-mode re-fold, bounds-toggle-by-default, decade-spaced relaxation defaults, background-corrected brightness/shape outputs.** Same-day polish on the general composable FCS model after first use. (1) **Live re-fold on `diffusion_mode` change**: `chisurf.core.dataspec.ChoiceSection` gained `rebuild_on_change` (opt-in, `ChoiceWidget`-only via `_BoundControlMixin._maybe_rebuild_host`, `QTimer.singleShot(0, form.rebuild)` — generalizes the combo-driven rebuild pattern already used by tool-specific hosts like the PCH detector combo); `AutoForm._collapsed_when` gained `not_equals` alongside `equals` so a panel folds *unless* its bound attribute matches a value. `general.view.json`'s three diffusion panels (MDF/Gauss/Two-focus) each fold via `collapsed_when: {attr: diffusion_mode, not_equals: "<own mode>"}`, so only the active mode's panel stays expanded and it re-folds live on selection, not just at editor-build time — the exact follow-up flagged in PRD-62. Default `diffusion_mode` changed `"mdf"` → `"gauss"` (the common case). (2) **Bounds-toggle-by-default**: `PanelSection.bounds_toggle` default flipped `False` → `True` (was opt-in per-panel, e.g. only TCSPC lifetime); every panel with a parameter table now gets the "bounds" header toggle (columns start hidden, bounds stay editable via the details popup) without needing to opt in — fixes the FCS bunching/anticorr dynamic tables (many paired Lo/Hi/Bounds/Error columns) overflowing narrow docks and needing horizontal scrolling to reach the time-constant column. (3) **Decade-spaced relaxation-term defaults**: `relaxation.py`'s `add_bunching`/`add_anticorr` now default a new term's time constant to the next decade up from the previous one instead of a fixed constant (bunching: 1, 10, 100 µs, ...; anticorrelation: 1, 10, 100 ns, ...) — added terms start usefully spread out instead of stacked duplicates. `AntibunchingTerms` renamed to `AnticorrTerms` (`antibunching`→`anticorr` attr/methods/panel) throughout the general model; the legacy Parse-FCS catalogue's own "antibunching" terminology is untouched (different, older feature). Anticorrelation's time constant now stored/labeled in **ns** (was ms, unreadably small at physical timescales); bunching stays in ms. (4) **Physical/derived-output additions**: baseline offset `b` default changed `0` → `1` (matches the typical normalized-ACF convention, e.g. Kristine-format data, where `G(tau→∞) → 1`, not a background-subtracted 0). Added a `bg` (background count rate, kHz, fixed by default) physical parameter to `MdfPhysical`/`GaussDiffusion`, matching the Parse-FCS catalogue's `Counts`/`BG` convention (`models.yaml`'s "3D diffusion + background" family). Added derived, read-only outputs: `brightness` (`chisurf.core.models.fcs.mdf.compute_brightness`, background-corrected `(CR_total - bg) / N` in kHz, `CR_total` from the data file's `mean_count_rate` metadata e.g. Kristine's header — shared by `MdfOutputs` and `GaussDiffusion`) and `GaussDiffusion.s = w_z / w_r` (the classic structure/aspect-ratio parameter, reported for comparison even though this group still fits absolute `w_r`/`w_z` rather than the dimensionless legacy parametrization). Registry: scoped-merged (same additive methodology as 2026-07-23) picked up 5 new qualified ids and 1 genuinely new bare name (`brightness`; `bg`/`s` already existed from unrelated classes, now correctly flagged `ambiguous` instead of silently sharing a description); added `_flr_chisurf_parameter.brightness` to `modules/mmfdb`'s `mmfdb_flr_ext.dic` (own repo, committed there). Headless coverage: `test/gui/test_fcs_model_editor.py` grew from 7 to 12 tests (default-mode, decade-default, offset, shape/brightness-output, and a live-rebuild re-fold test asserting `CollapsibleBox.is_expanded()` flips on `AutoForm.rebuild()`); `test/fio/test_flrcif_alignment.py` (13 pass). (5) **Compound-equation display**: both `MdfFCSModel` and `GeneralFCSModel` gain `equation_html()` plus a top "Equation" panel (`info` section, `source: "equation_html"`) showing the currently active formula — the selected diffusion term times each active bunching/anticorrelation factor, the latter built from new `BunchingTerms.equation_html()`/`AnticorrTerms.equation_html()` methods on the term groups themselves (mirroring their own `apply()` formula, so the display can't drift from what's computed). (6) **Fixed a real `AutoForm` gap surfaced by (5)**: `_build_dynamic_group`'s add/remove handlers (`on_add`/`on_del`/`on_add_table`/`on_del_table`) only rebuilt their own table/row-grid after dispatching the fit update — no `refresh_plots()` call — so clicking "add"/"del" on bunching/anticorr (or TCSPC lifetimes/anisotropy rotations, same mechanism) never refreshed sibling `AUTOFORM_REFRESH` widgets like the new equation panel. Fixed by adding `self.refresh_plots()` to all four handlers; regression test clicks the real button rather than mutating the model directly. (7) **`hidden_when`: fully hide, not just fold** (explicit follow-up: "really hide the irrelevant groups"): new `PanelSection.hidden_when` (same `{target?, attr, equals|not_equals}` shape as `collapsed_when`), evaluated centrally in `AutoForm._emit_sections` alongside the existing static `visible` field — not per section builder, since a `setVisible(False)` inside `_build_panel` itself was found to be silently clobbered by the `widget.setVisible(bool(section.visible))` line that already runs there for every section. `general.view.json`'s three diffusion panels switched from `collapsed_when` to `hidden_when`: only the active mode's panel is even present now, not merely expanded. (8) **Fixed a real, general precision/range bug in every parameter table**: the value/Lo/Hi columns had no custom editor delegate, so Qt's default double-spinbox editor (2 decimals, 0-99.99 range) was used to edit them — a 0.001 ms bunching time constant displayed/edited as "0.00", and a value above 99.99 (e.g. `w_z[nm] = 2020.1`) could not be typed at all. Added `_FloatEditDelegate` (`chisurf/gui/autoform/sections/parameter_table.py`) wrapping the same `ScientificDoubleSpinBox` (`%g` formatting, unbounded) the standalone parameter widgets already use, wired onto `ParameterGroupTableWidget` and `PairedParameterTableWidget`'s value/bounds_lo/bounds_hi columns (both `__init__` and `set_params`, so it survives a dynamic-group add/remove rebuild). Also fixed the new brightness output's `label_text="&epsiv;[kHz]"` → `"&epsilon;[kHz]"` — Qt's rich-text engine only supports HTML4's Greek-letter entities; `&epsiv;` (HTML5-only) rendered as literal text instead of ε. `test/gui/test_fcs_model_editor.py` grew to 18 tests, `test/gui/test_parameter_table_widget.py` gained a delegate regression test (22 total). See [prds/prd-62.md](/prds/prd-62.md), [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **i18n(de): wired `tttr_time_windows` for translation + full German.** Second rich dock/plot tool made translatable (after `maxent_decay`), applying the audit's rule that heavy dock tools get `i18n.tr`-wrapping rather than an AutoForm rewrite (a form port would lose the dock area + live intensity-trace preview). Wrapped every static user-facing string in `tttr_time_windows/gui/tool.py` — window/help titles, the four dock-tab labels (⚙️ Settings / 📁 Files / 👁️ Preview / 📋 Summary), settings form (Time window / Output folder / Browse… + tooltips), preview controls, the toolbar Process/Clear/Help actions, menu (File/Exit/Help/About), plot axis + title labels, and the static status-bar messages — with `chisurf.core.i18n.tr`. The toolbar `name_map` (object-name styling keyed on `action.text()`) was mirrored through the same `i18n.tr` expressions so the styling still matches under any locale. Diagnostic `_log`/interpolated status lines left English by convention. `i18n-extract` picked up 35 new sources (all this tool's); filled 34 German slots + reused 11 shared chrome terms already seeded, recompiled `de.qm`. Verified offscreen with `gui.language: de`: title *TTTR-Zeitfenster-BIDs*, tabs *Einstellungen/Dateien/Vorschau/Zusammenfassung*, status *Bereit*, menus *Datei/Hilfe* — units (`ms`) correctly fall through. Smoke tests pass (3). See [subsystems/i18n.md](/subsystems/i18n.md).

## 2026-07-23
* **FCS model consolidation (PRD-62): table views, composable diffusion + bunching, parameter-registry scoping.** Four related asks. (1) **Parameter-registry collision fixed + generalized**: FCS MDF's `R0` (emission-side Gauss-Lorentz waist) silently inherited FRET's "Förster radius" description because `chisurf/core/parameter.py`'s registry lookup was an unscoped exact bare-name match into a single flat `parameter_registry.json`, and the generator (`build_tools/dev_utils/export_fitting_parameters.py`) merged any two `FittingParameter(...)` call sites sharing a name regardless of owning class. The generator now also emits a `by_qualified_id` index (`"<ClassName>.<name>"`) and flags a bare-name entry `"ambiguous"` when more than one class contributes to it; `Parameter.__init__` now tries an explicit `registry_id=` → a class-scoped match (derived by inspecting the constructing frame for the owning `self`) → the legacy bare-name entry only when unambiguous — strictly additive, no existing description blanked. Regenerated the registry (`version: 2`) and fixed an unrelated ASCII-mangled "Förster" encoding. (2) **FCS MDF Gauss-Lorentz relocated + migrated to the table view**: moved out of `chisurf/gui/widgets/models/fcs/mdf_widget.py` into a Qt-free `chisurf/core/models/fcs/mdf.py::MdfFCSModel`, deleting the hand-built widget (RICS/PDA/DEER pattern — bare model class + generic `AutoForm`); its 14 parameters regrouped into `physical`/`optics`/`outputs` `FittingParameterGroup` tables (`mdf.view.json`, `parameter_group_table` sections); `R0` renamed to `wem` to remove the registry collision at its source. (3) **Bunching terms added to MDF**: `chisurf/core/models/fcs/relaxation.py::BunchingTerms`, a dynamic add/remove `(ba_i, bt_i)` list (`dynamic_group` table, same mechanism as TCSPC lifetimes/anisotropy rotations), multiplying the diffusion shape by `(1-ba+ba*exp(-tau/bt))` (Parse-FCS catalogue convention). (4) **General composable FCS model**: `chisurf/core/models/fcs/general.py::GeneralFCSModel` — a 3-way `diffusion_mode` selector (`"mdf"` / `"gauss"` single-focus / `"two_focus"`, the classic Dertinger dual-focus technique made a directly selectable preset rather than a parameter buried in a table, per explicit follow-up request) plus dynamic bunching **and** `AntibunchingTerms` (pure sub-Poissonian dip, no plateau shift) lists; every mode supports the same two-focus cross-term via `diam>0` (`GaussDiffusion`, reused for both `"gauss"` and `"two_focus"` with different starting `diam`). Supersedes the diffusion×bunching/antibunching combinatorial subset of `models.yaml` for new work (the catalogue itself is untouched). Also fixed collateral damage from re-running the parameter-registry generator honestly for the first time in a while: it surfaced 8 brand-new FCS parameter names with no `flrcif_item_id` (`Veff`, `conc`, `diam`, `lam_em`, `lam_ex`, `mag`, `pinhole`, `wem`) and a pre-existing dev-tool script (`align_flrcif_parameters.py`) that targets a dead legacy path (`chisurf/core/mmfdb/data/`, pre-MMFDB-extraction) and a stale `_chisurf_schema.*` tag (pre-PRD-44 vendor-neutral rename) — added the 8 missing `_flr_chisurf_parameter.*` item definitions by hand, in the current `_mmfdb_schema.*` convention, directly to `modules/mmfdb`'s `mmfdb_flr_ext.dic` (its own repo, committed there per the mmfdb commit policy). Headless coverage: `test/fitting/test_parameter_registry_scoping.py` (3 pass, scoping regression), `test/gui/test_fcs_models_resolve.py` (10 pass, both models resolve), `test/gui/test_fcs_model_editor.py` (9 pass: table rendering, bunching/antibunching add/remove, 3-way diffusion selector, two-focus G(0) suppression, `wem`-not-Förster-radius guard), `test/fio/test_flrcif_alignment.py` (13 pass, all-parameters-mapped guard restored). `test/fluorescence/test_enderlein_fcs.py` unaffected (6 pass). See [prds/prd-62.md](/prds/prd-62.md), [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md), [subsystems/parameters.md](/subsystems/parameters.md).
* **Fixed micro-time axis unit bug in Histogram-Microtime (ps used as ns).** The detector setup stores the micro-time resolution in **picoseconds** (`micro_time_le` / `effective_micro_time_resolution`, set from `header.micro_time_resolution * 1e12`), but the Decay-Analysis histogram used it directly as the **nanosecond** `dt`/`time_step`, so the decay's Micro-Time (ns) axis and FWHM were 1000× too large (e.g. `dt=3.2958984375 ns` and `FWHM 12082.76 ns` for an SPC-130 file whose real resolution is 3.2958984375 **ps** → `dt≈0.0033 ns`, `FWHM≈12.08 ns`). Added the missing ps→ns (`* 1e-3`) conversion at the three DetectorWizard-driven `dt` sites in `microtime_histogram/wizard.py` (`on_detectors_changed` display + the two `time_step` computes). The non-SPC header path (`* 1e9` from seconds) was already correct and unchanged. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Detector setup: "Polarization resolved" flag (default ON) gating interleaved VV/VH channels.** Added a `polarization_resolved` flag to the detector-setup data model + a checkbox in the Detectors box header (`DetectorWizardPage`): ON = each detector's routing channels are interleaved parallel/perpendicular (VV/VH, the anisotropy case); OFF = one unpolarized stream per detector (no VV/VH split). Serialized in `get_settings()` (defaults True on load for back-compat). The Decay-Analysis `microtime_histogram` plugin consumes it via `_is_polarization_resolved()`: its `parallel_channels`/`perpendicular_channels` (and the inline Compute path) return the interleaved even/odd split when ON, or all channels as `parallel` + empty `perpendicular` when OFF. Headless: `test_channel_definition_lut_box.py::test_polarization_resolved_flag_defaults_on_and_round_trips` + `microtime_histogram/tests::test_polarization_resolved_gates_parallel_perp` — 12 pass; screenshot verified. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **General LUT fix: a process-global active-setup context so every seam read is LUT-aware.** Rather than thread the setup's LUT to each `tttrlib.TTTR` call site (and keep missing some), added `chisurf/core/fio/lut_context.py` (`set_active_setup_lut`/`get_active_setup_lut`/`clear_active_setup_lut`). `staging.open_tttr`'s `apply_lut` now defaults to **None** = "consult the active-setup context"; an explicit `True`/`False` still wins (inspection/editor tools pass `apply_lut=False` to force raw). `DetectorWizardPage._publish_lut_context()` publishes its `channel_luts`/`channel_shifts`/`apply_lut` whenever they change and on `get_settings()`, so once a setup is active every read through the single seam applies its LUT without per-site plumbing — the "route all TTTR through a routine that accounts for setup specifics" ask. The base reader `_open_tttr` passes `apply_lut=None` through when the reader has no explicit gate. Caveat documented: single global correction (last setup wins); process-local (headless RPC passes the correction explicitly). Guarded the double-apply case (the wizard decay preview opens raw + applies its own LUT). Tests: `test_open_tttr_uses_active_setup_context` + autouse context-clearing fixtures — 13 staging + 19 combined pass. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Histogram-Microtime (Decay Analysis) plugin now applies the setup LUT.** The `microtime_histogram` plugin's VV/VH decay Compute opened raw `tttrlib.TTTR` and histogrammed uncorrected micro-times, so a setup with an enabled LUT still produced the raw DNL decay (user-reported). Added `MicrotimeHistogram._apply_setup_lut(tttr)` — reads the embedded Detector-Setup page's `channel_luts`/`channel_shifts`/`apply_lut` and applies them in place via the shared `staging.apply_setup_lut` — called right after each `d = tttrlib.TTTR(...)` in `compute_microtime_histogram`. Encodes the rule **LUT on ⇒ apply on every read** for this analysis path too. Headless: `microtime_histogram/tests/test_widgets.py::test_apply_setup_lut_linearizes_histogram` (histogram changes with the LUT, raw when gated off) — 3 pass. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Visual per-channel micro-time shift adjuster in the detector wizard (LUT-aware).** The LUT-handling box gained an **"Adjust shifts…"** button opening a new `MicrotimeShiftDialog` (`shift_dialog.py`): it opens the read calibration file **LUT-corrected** via `staging.open_tttr` (honoring the rule that shifts align on the linearized axis when the LUT is on), shows the per-routing-channel micro-time histograms with a per-channel shift spinbox, and live-previews the wrapping shift as `np.roll` on each channel's histogram (equivalent to the photon-level shift, so no re-read). Reuses the promoted micro-time-shifter core concept (wrapping shift, mod n_mt). On OK the per-channel shifts are written into the setup's `channel_shifts` (applied non-destructively at read time via the shared shift seam) and the decay preview refreshes. Headless: `test_shift_dialog_loads_lut_corrected_and_round_trips` — 7 pass; offscreen screenshot verified (ch1 visibly offset vs ch0). See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Adding a LUT now auto-enables the master gate (fixes "LUT not applied").** Root cause of the LUT appearing to do nothing: `apply_lut` defaults off, and assigning / pulling LUTs left it off, so the LUT was stored but never applied on reading. New `DetectorWizardPage._enable_apply_lut()` ticks the master checkbox (sets `_apply_lut=True`) whenever a LUT exists — wired into `_pull_luts_from_panel` (Configure LUTs… / Add-to-setup) and `_on_assign_lut_file`. Encodes the general rule **LUT on ⇒ apply the LUT on every read**; `get_settings()` then persists `apply_lut=True` so the reader applies it too. Per-channel graceful behavior is unchanged: a channel with no computed LUT reads raw (`apply_channel_luts` only corrects channels present; `compute_all_channels` skips channels whose auto-detect fails). Headless: `test_channel_definition_lut_box.py::test_assigning_a_lut_auto_enables_apply` — 6 pass. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Shared `apply_setup_lut` helper + reader-control preview LUT fix (prevent the raw-decay-preview class of bug).** Extracted the LUT/shift application out of `staging.open_tttr` into a reusable `chisurf/core/fio/staging.py::apply_setup_lut(tttr, channel_luts, channel_shifts, apply_lut=, lut_seed=)` — the single sanctioned one-liner to LUT-correct a raw `tttrlib.TTTR` you already hold (decay previews that open raw for the header then histogram). The channel-def preview now delegates to it, and the **TCSPC reader-control decay preview** (`tcspc_tttr_reader_control_widget.py::_load_preview_from_file`) — which opened a bare `tttrlib.TTTR` and so showed the raw decay even though the reader carries the LUTs — now opens through `staging.open_tttr` with the reader's `channel_luts`/`apply_lut`. Guardrail: `test/fio/test_staging.py::test_apply_setup_lut_in_place` (no-op when gated off, in-place correction matching `open_tttr` when on) — 12 staging tests pass. **Remaining candidates that produce a micro-time histogram from a raw open and consume a setup** (tracked, fix with `apply_setup_lut`): `microtime_histogram` plugin (`wizard.py:1022/1467`), `tttr_photon_filter` decay preview, `burst_selection` preview (`tool.py:2314`), `burst_mle_analysis`, `sm_image_mle`, `burst/irf_bg`. A blanket `tttrlib.TTTR` monkeypatch is unsafe (no per-file setup context; inspection tools must see raw; SWIG-fragile) — the shared helper + routing analysis reads through `staging.open_tttr` is the systemic fix. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Fixed H2MM dock crash (`wrapped C/C++ object of type PlotWidget has been deleted`) + hardened DockArea.** Re-showing a closed H2MM plot dock via the context menu crashed: the results docks used `DockArea.enable_persistence`, whose **`showEvent`-time restore** fought the embedded burst-analysis navigation lifecycle and left a deleted plot widget registered in `_all_widgets`, so a later `showTab`/menu action called into a dead C++ object. Two fixes. (1) **H2MM** now persists its dock layout the proven **BVA way** — synchronously in `__init__` (`_restore_dock_layout` restores the saved arrangement or applies the default two-column grid with `emit_change=False`; `_save_dock_layout` on `layoutChanged` + `closeEvent`), never on `showEvent`; dropped `enable_persistence`. (2) **DockArea hardening** (helps every tool): a `_is_deleted(widget)` probe (sip/shiboken/last-resort) + `_prune_deleted()` called from `showTab` and `_add_dock_visibility_actions`, so a C/C++-deleted page is skipped/pruned instead of crashing. Headless: reproduced the exact crash (force-delete a registered plot widget, then build menu + `showTab`) and confirmed it now returns cleanly and prunes the entry; clean-first-run default grid + embedded save→rebuild→hide/show lifecycle both verified by offscreen screenshot; `burst_h2mm` + `burst_analysis` suites **66 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Channel-definition micro-time decay preview now applies the LUT (was reading raw).** The editor's decay preview read via a bare `tttrlib.TTTR` (`tttr_channel_definition_tttr_io.py::read_from_tttr_file`), so after configuring a LUT the preview still showed the *raw* decay — it looked like the LUT did nothing. `_update_microtime_preview` now applies the setup's per-channel LUTs/shifts (`_apply_setup_lut_to_tttr`, tttrlib `apply_channel_luts`/`apply_luts_and_shifts` with the same fixed dither seed as the reading seam) before histogramming both the all-channel and per-channel decays. Because users read the calibration file *before* computing/assigning LUTs, added `DetectorWizardPage._refresh_preview_lut()` (re-reads the stored file and redraws with the current LUT) wired to the `apply_lut` toggle, per-channel Assign, and the LUT-Tools pull — so the preview updates live and reverts to raw when the gate is off. Headless: `test/gui/test_channel_definition_lut_box.py` +1 (preview changes with the LUT, reverts off) — 5 pass. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **LUT Tools: one click computes + assigns a LUT for ALL routing channels.** Following up the per-channel LUT work, the default action now computes a LUT for **every** routing channel in the loaded file and assigns them all: `LutComputeViewModel.compute_all_channels()` builds one LUT per used channel (region auto-detected each, honoring the currently previewed channel's hand-tuned region), and **➡ Add all channels to setup** assigns each to its channel (the channel-def editor pulls them on close). The channel dropdown is relabeled **Preview channel** (inspect/tune one) and now **updates the plot** on change — the fix was adding `call: update` to the AutoForm `choice` section and letting `update(*_)` accept the value the hook passes. Header/help/README updated to the all-channels flow. Also fixed a headless test hang: the old bridge test set `current_table` with no data so the new all-channels bridge hit a modal `QMessageBox`; the test now loads a real SPC file and asserts all channels are assigned. `tttr_lut_tools/tests` **14 pass**; tutorial 37 screenshot regenerated. See [subsystems/plugins.md](/subsystems/plugins.md).
* **H2MM results split into per-plot docks + experimental flag removed.** Two H2MM asks. (1) **De-crammed the Results panel** — the seven burstH2MM-style plots were packed into one `GraphicsLayoutWidget` grid in a single "Results" dock, so each was tiny. Each plot is now its **own dock** (`_build_plot_docks`: individual `pg.PlotWidget`s, `_p_*` = their `getPlotItem()`; the state-path plot keeps its ◀/▶ nav bar in its dock) added to the tool's `DockArea`, arriving in a default two-column layout that mirrors the old dashboard (`_apply_default_plot_layout` via `DockArea.set_layout_state`, graceful tab fallback) but every plot is independently resizable / tabbable / maximisable / draggable, and the arrangement persists (`DockArea.enable_persistence("burst_h2mm")`). `_save_plot` now grabs the active plot dock (per-plot filename). (2) **Removed the experimental flag** from the H2MM step: dropped `experimental`/`experimental_message` from `BURST_PANELS` (`burst_analysis`) and `burst_h2mm/manifest.json`, and the "⚠️ experimental" window-title suffix — no more nav ⚠️ / red banner. Headless: offscreen screenshot verified the docked grid; `burst_h2mm/tests/test_gui.py` **6 pass** (updated for per-plot docks), `burst_analysis/tests/test_workflow.py` **15 pass** (experimental test inverted), full burst_h2mm + burst_analysis suites **66 pass**, manifest validation **24 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Burst Background Estimation plugin gained diagnostic plots (inter-photon-time tail fit + rate bars).** The plugin previously showed only a results *table* of background rates. Added a **Diagnostics** tab (pyqtgraph): the per-detector **inter-photon-time distribution** (log–log points) overlaid with the **fitted exponential background tail**, and a **per-detector background-rate bar chart** — the same view the [background tutorial](../../docs/tutorials/15_background_rates.md) illustrates, so the fit can be judged visually. Core: `background.py` refactored to share one Poisson-MLE tail fit (`_fit_exponential_tail`) between the rate estimator and a new `interphoton_time_diagnostics` / `background_diagnostics_from_bursts` returning a `BackgroundDiagnostics` (histogram + model + tail mask + rate) — the estimator's rate is unchanged (bit-identical). Detector colours are semantic (green/red/yellow → their colours) and identity-stable. Headless coverage: `test/plugins/burst/test_background_diagnostics.py` **4 pass** (diagnostics-vs-estimator identity, known-rate recovery, per-detector) and `test/plugins/burst/test_background_gui.py` **2 pass** (plots build/render/clear, screenshot-verified). See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **LUT Tools: per-routing-channel LUTs + "Add to Detector setup" as the default action.** TAC differential non-linearity is *per routing channel*, so a LUT must be computed per channel, not pooled. The api gained per-channel loading (`io.load_microtimes(..., channel=)`, `io.load_micro_and_routing`), `compute.compute_lut_from_files(..., channel=)`, a `channel` CLI option + rpc param. The ① Compute view-model loads all channels once, exposes a **Routing channel** selector (AutoForm `choice` bound to `channels_options`), and re-histograms/re-computes per selected channel (switching channel yields a different LUT). The ①→② bridge auto-assigns the per-channel LUT to its channel (`receive_computed_lut(..., channel=)` sets `channel_luts[ch]`). **UX reframed around the user's confusion**: the primary/default action is now a bold **➡ Add to Detector setup** (assigns each per-channel LUT; the channel-def editor pulls them on close), **saving a LUT/settings file is explicitly optional** (right-aligned "Optional:" buttons), and the second tab is relabeled **"② settings.tttr.json (optional)"** with header/help/README making clear ① is the tool and ② is optional file interchange (README leads with a two-row comparison table). Headless: `tttr_lut_tools/tests` **18 pass** (+ per-channel LUT-differs test); tutorial 37 screenshot regenerated (channel selector). See [subsystems/plugins.md](/subsystems/plugins.md).
* **Burst Analysis workflow fixes: MLE load crash, 2CDE folder hand-off, BVA recompute coalescing + unified progress.** Four issues in the integrated `burst_analysis` shell. (1) **MLE-Lifetime failed to load** with `'PathListWidget' object has no attribute 'count'` — `burst_files_list` migrated to the shared `PathListWidget`-backed factory (which has `paths()`, not `count()`); `_apply_context_to_mle` now guards on `len(file_list.paths()) == 0`. (2) **2CDE never received the analysis folder** — added `_apply_context_to_2cde` (mirrors BVA: `widget.set_folder(burst_folder)`, side-effect-free), registered the `two_cde` role in `_apply_context_to_panel` and the downstream apply loop. (3) **BVA recomputed too many times when switching to it / window busy** — applying context touched the detector table, donor/acceptor combos and folder, each firing a synchronous full read/compute/plot. `BVATool` now coalesces param changes via a zero-delay single-shot `QTimer` (`_on_param_changed` just marks pending + schedules `_flush_recompute`) plus a `suspend_recompute()` batch guard the shell wraps `_apply_context_to_bva` in → a single recompute per entry. (4) **Unified BVA progress bars** — read/compute/write phases now share one `_ProgressDialog` (via `_begin_progress`/`_phase` retargeting) instead of flashing a new dialog per phase. Headless: `burst_analysis/tests/test_workflow.py` **15 pass** (+4 new: MLE paths-not-count contract, MLE prepopulate, 2CDE folder, BVA coalescing); burst_bva + burst_2cde + mle dock suites green. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **LUT Tools ① Compute panel rewritten as AutoForm (`view.json` + view_model + custom sections).** The 773-line hand-built `TACLinearizationPanel` is now a thin `AutoForm` host over a Qt-free `LutComputeViewModel` (`gui/view_model.py`, math delegated to `api`). Layout is declarative `gui/lut_compute.view.json`: a `?` **help** modal, the unified **`path_list`** file loader (bound to `model.files`; `update()` reloads on change), the interactive raw/after TAC plots with the draggable **linear region** + offset/threshold lines as a custom `lut_compute_plot` section (`gui/sections.py`), a live info label, a 2-col **Parameters** panel + a collapsible **Advanced** panel (every field a `value`/`toggle` with a `description` → tooltip, `call:update`), and a `lut_compute_actions` custom section (Auto-detect / Save LUT / Export with file dialogs). A dragged plot item updates the model and re-syncs the value fields via `AutoForm.sync_fields()`. `current_table` stays a property so the ①→② bridge and tests are unaffected. (Dropped the niche manual zero-brush / left-right cut cleanup in the rewrite.) Headless: `tttr_lut_tools/tests/test_gui.py` **14 pass** (+ AutoForm-host + view-model load/region/recompute) and the channel-def transfer test still green; offscreen screenshot verified. This closes the deferred AutoForm item for the plugin. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Tutorial 37 — TAC linearization / microtime LUTs (usage + concepts, screenshots + matplotlib).** Added `docs/tutorials/37_tttr_microtime_lut.md`: part 1 **Usage** walks the ①-compute → ②-assign → setup → LUT-aware-read flow with real **GUI screenshots** (the LUT Tools workspace with a bridged LUT, and the channel-definition LUT-handling box) plus CLI/API snippets; part 2 **Concepts** explains TAC differential non-linearity and the Felekyan cumulative-LUT construction with a **matplotlib** figure (raw-vs-linearized flat-light histogram + the LUT vs the linear ideal). Screenshots are regenerated headlessly from the real widgets via a new `docs/tutorials/make_screenshots.py` (offscreen `QWidget.grab()`); the concept figure via a new `fig_lut()` in `make_figures.py` using the plugin's own `api`. Listed in `index.md` (+ a note on the screenshot script). See [subsystems/plugins.md](/subsystems/plugins.md).
* **LUT Tools GUI: explain the two stages + one-click ①→② transfer.** The two tabs confused users ("what is the difference?"). Added a header toolbar with a one-line **flow explanation** (① Compute from uniform light → ② Assign to channels & export → Configure LUTs… in the Detector setup applies it at read time), numbered/tooltipped tabs (**① Compute LUT** / **② Assign / Export**), and a **`?` help modal** rendering the consolidated README (the crisp "what's the difference / how it transfers" answer). Removed the friction of Save-LUT-then-Load-LUT with a **①→② in-memory bridge**: a "→ Use in ② Assign" button hands the LUT just computed straight to the Assign panel (new `TTTRSettingsPanel.receive_computed_lut`) and switches tabs. `TTRLutToolsWidget` keeps its name + `.settings_panel`/`.tac_panel` attributes so the channel-definition editor's `_pull_luts_from_panel` transfer is unaffected. Headless: `tttr_lut_tools/tests/test_gui.py` **3 pass** + offscreen screenshot verified. (Full AutoForm view.json rewrite of the interactive compute plot remains a follow-up.) See [subsystems/plugins.md](/subsystems/plugins.md).
* **Removed the two deprecated LUT plugin siblings (duplication).** `tttr_microtime_lut/` and `tttr_settings_generator/` were byte-for-byte duplicates of `tttr_lut_tools`' compute and settings logic (both `deprecated`, `menu_hidden`, manifest-less). Consolidated their documentation into a single `tttr_lut_tools/README.md` that crisply explains the **two stages** (① Compute = *make* a LUT from a uniform-illumination measurement; ② Create Settings = *apply* LUTs to channels + export `settings.tttr.json`) and **how a LUT reaches a setup** (the channel-def "Configure LUTs…" button pulls `channel_luts` on close → reader linearizes via `staging.open_tttr`). Repointed the Info dialog (`settings_panel.py`) to the new README and deleted both sibling directories (no remaining references). See [subsystems/plugins.md](/subsystems/plugins.md).
* **`tttr_lut_tools` promoted to a first-class api/cli/rpc plugin (GUI + tutorial follow).** Split the monolithic plugin into layers: **`api/`** — pure, Qt-free logic (`lut.py` the Felekyan math with the old `click`/`tttrlib`/`SystemExit` coupling removed, `io.py` file/tttrlib IO incl. `load_lut_file`/`save_lut`, `compute.py` the load→histogram→autodetect→build orchestration, `settings.py` `settings.tttr.json` build/load + `apply_luts_to_tttr`/`corrected_histogram` reusing the Phase-1 `staging.open_tttr` seam, `contract.py` `METHOD_*` + JSON-Schemas whose `description`s become AutoForm tooltips); **`backend/services.py`** — `register_services(dispatcher)` exposing `lut.autodetect_region`/`compute`/`apply_preview`/`settings_build`/`settings_load` as guarded `{ok,result|error}` handlers; **`cli/main.py`** — a `lut-tools` click group (`compute`/`apply`/`settings`) calling `api/` directly. `manifest.json` gained `entrypoints.cli`/`entrypoints.services` + `rpc_methods[]`. `core/tac_lut.py` is now a thin back-compat shim re-exporting `api` (existing imports, incl. the Phase-1 staging test, unaffected). Headless coverage: `tttr_lut_tools/tests/{test_api,test_services}.py` **9 pass** (compute determinism/monotonicity, settings + LUT-file round-trips, service registration + error envelope + build/load, CLI compute+settings). Remaining in this task: AutoForm GUI + help/tooltips clarifying the two stages, remove the duplicate deprecated siblings, and a usage/concepts tutorial with screenshots. See [subsystems/plugins.md](/subsystems/plugins.md).
* **Single-molecule tutorials with headless-generated figures (`docs/tutorials/`).** Seven self-contained markdown tutorials for the new single-molecule analyses — FRET/ALEX-2CDE burst dynamics, recurrence (RASP), polymer distance distributions (SAW-ν + Ising), FIDA/PCH, Enderlein MDF & two-focus FCS, ns-FCS second-order g⁽³⁾, and RCM detection calibration — each with theory, a runnable ChiSurf snippet, and a real figure. `docs/tutorials/make_figures.py` regenerates all figures headlessly (matplotlib Agg) by running the actual analysis functions on synthetic data (`PYTHONPATH=. python docs/tutorials/make_figures.py`), so the plots are genuine output. `docs/tutorials/index.md` links them. See [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **Ising two-state chain FRET distance distribution (tractable Ising-WLC).** Adds `rdf.ising_chain(r, N, bS, bU, J, h)` — the folded/unfolded heteropolymer P(R) behind Fretica's compiled `FIsingWLCFRET*`, in its tractable Gaussian-segment form: every residue is structured or unstructured under a nearest-neighbour Ising Hamiltonian (cooperativity J, field h), each contributing a Gaussian bond (RMS bS/bU). Because the characteristic function factorises per residue, φ(k) is an **exact 2×2 transfer-matrix product** in Fourier space, inverted by the isotropic transform `P(R)=(2R/π)∫k·sin(kR)·φ(k)dk`; reduces to `gaussian_chain` when bS=bU. Wired as a TCSPC FRET model `IsingChainModel` ("FRET: FD (Ising two-state chain)") + `IsingChainModelWidget`, registered in `experiment_configs.yaml` alongside SAW-ν/WLC/Gaussian. Headless: `test/test_rdf_ising.py` **4 pass** (normalisation, Gaussian-chain limit, field-driven compaction, cooperativity). NB: the residue-resolved full Ising-WLC and the TRBP register correlation remain in Fretica's compiled backend (no portable source) — not reimplemented. See [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **burst_2cde GUI panel + workflow step (completes the 2CDE feature).** The deferred bespoke Qt tool for FRET-2CDE/ALEX-2CDE (`burst_2cde/gui/tool.py`, `BurstTwoCdeTool`): a folder selector, settings form (variant/kernel/τ/donor/acceptor/file-type), a Compute button driving `core.compute_2cde`, and a pyqtgraph E-vs-2CDE scatter that visibly separates static (~10) from dynamic (~40) bursts. Wired into the `burst_analysis` navigation shell as step **4. 2CDE** (`_burst_2cde` factory; MLE/H2MM/Browser renumbered 5/6/7, separator index 6→7) and exposed via the manifest `gui` entrypoint. Headless-verified with an offscreen `QWidget.grab()` screenshot (inspected) + `chisurf/plugins/burst/burst_2cde/tests/test_gui.py` (**2 pass**: build/compute/grab + workflow-factory wired); `burst_analysis` workflow tests updated for the new step (**11 pass**). See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Onboarding wizard: detector/FCS step intros moved from inline boxes into `?` modal help.** The Detector-setup and FCS-channels steps of the boarding wizard rendered their multi-line intro paragraphs as `info` panels that ate vertical space above the embedded editor. Replaced both with the reusable `help` custom section (`{"type":"custom","key":"help","options":{"title":…,"text":…}}`) — a compact right-aligned `?` button that opens the text in a modal — in `boarding.view.json`, and removed the now-dead `detector_intro_html`/`fcs_intro_html` source methods + `_DETECTOR_INTRO_HTML`/`_FCS_INTRO_HTML` constants from `BoardingViewModel`. The embedded `DetectorWizardPage` (with its Phase-4 foldable boxes) now gets the full step height. Offscreen-verified. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Channel-definition editor: foldable boxes + LUT-handling box (Phase 4).** The TTTR-reading / PIE-windows / Detectors sections of `DetectorWizardPage` are now each wrapped in a `CollapsibleBox`, and a new **"LUT handling (TAC linearization)"** box is added, delivering the visible ask. The reorganization detaches the `.ui`'s top-level `gridLayout` sub-layouts (`takeAt` → re-host inside a `CollapsibleBox` container) so all `qtpy_loadUi` wiring is preserved — no `.ui` rewrite. The LUT box carries the master **"Apply TAC linearization (LUT) when reading"** checkbox (bound to `apply_lut`), a per-routing-channel table (Channel | LUT | Shift) auto-populated from the union of detector channels, with a **hover-tooltip LUT plot** (`lut_thumbnail.render_lut_tooltip`, Phase 4a) on each assigned LUT, an "Assign LUT…" file picker (reusing `tttr_lut_tools`' `load_lut_file`), and a **"Configure LUTs…"** button that jumps to the existing `TTRLutToolsWidget` (compute/assign/`settings.tttr.json`) and pulls `channel_luts`/`channel_shifts` back into the setup. Checking the gate with a used channel missing a LUT prompts to open the calculator. Headless coverage: `test/gui/test_channel_definition_lut_box.py` **4 pass** (boxes present, box reflects setup, `get_settings` round-trip, toggle) + offscreen screenshot verified; `test/gui/test_lut_thumbnail.py` **3 pass**. Remaining: the full `detector_wizard_page.ui` → AutoForm `channel_definition.view.json` conversion of the existing tables/preview (deprecating the `.ui`) is a tracked follow-up. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Fretica-gap follow-ups: FIDA multinomial fit + RCM detection-calibration from dye solutions.** Two more pure-Wolfram Fretica ports. (1) `fit_fida` in `chisurf/core/models/pch/fida.py` (port of `FPCHFidaFit`) — Levenberg-Marquardt on the multinomial residuals `(nbins·p-counts)/√(nbins·p(1-p))`, χ²r normed by kmax-nparams; recovers (q,N) from a histogram. (2) `rcm_from_dye_solutions` in `chisurf/core/fluorescence/fret/calibration.py` (port of `FRCMCalibrationFromDyeSolutions`) — solves the routing/detection-correction matrix from donor-only + acceptor-only dye-solution channel rates, absorbance ratio and detector layout (2-channel, or 4-channel polarising/50-50 BS with anisotropy), removing hand-entered correction factors. Headless: `test/models/test_fida.py` **+1** (fit recovery), `test/fluorescence/test_rcm_calibration.py` **4 pass**. See [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **SAW-ν polymer inter-dye distance distribution added as a shared P(R) + FRET/PDA models.** Closes the last Fretica gap (chisurf had only worm-like-chain + Gaussian-chain P(R); no self-avoiding-walk model for disordered/unfolded chains). The single new primitive is `rdf.saw_nu(r, r_rms, nu)` in `chisurf/core/math/functions/rdf.py` — the des Cloizeaux SAW-ν distribution `P(r)∝r^{2+θ}exp[-(r/r0)^δ]`, θ=(γ-1)/ν, δ=1/(1-ν), scaled to a target RMS (reduces to `gaussian_chain` at ν=0.5, γ=1); it reuses, not duplicates, the existing WLC/Gaussian RDFs. Wired into **both** fit stages: a TCSPC FRET decay model `SawNuModel` (`FRET: FD (SAW-ν polymer)`, params Rrms/ν) with `SawNuChainModelWidget`, and a PDA model `PdaSawNuModel` (subclasses `PdaGaussianDistanceModel`, swapping only the distance group `PdaSawNuDistances`, + `saw_nu.view.json`), both registered in `experiment_configs.yaml`. Headless coverage: `test/test_rdf_saw_nu.py` **4 pass** (normalisation, RMS recovery, Gaussian-chain limit, expanded-chain shift), `test/models/test_pda_saw_nu.py` **3 pass** (distances distribution, model resolution, view-spec), TCSPC model resolves in the editor path. See [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **Fan-out: burst / FCS / PCH / RICS reads are now LUT-aware from the selected setup (Phase 3).** Added the single Qt-free adapter `chisurf/core/data_io/detector_setups.py::setup_lut_open_kwargs(setup)` → `{channel_luts, channel_shifts, apply_lut}` (empty/raw for a missing setup) that every setup-consuming reader uses to become LUT-aware in one line. Wired: `burst_selection/api/io.py::load_tttr` now routes through `staging.open_tttr` and accepts the LUT/shift kwargs; the FCS correlator (`fcs_correlator/tool.py`) resolves `setup_lut_open_kwargs(self.workflow_context.detector_settings)` and threads it through `_read_tttr`/`_load_raw_combined`; the PCH and RICS reader-control cascades (`gui/widgets/experiments/pch.py`, `rics.py`) push the selected setup's LUTs/shifts/`apply_lut` onto their models (auto-applied by the base reader's `_open_tttr`, like the TCSPC cascade). No regressions: burst_selection + fcs_correlator suites **184 pass / 2 skip / 1 xfail**. Remaining setup-consuming readers that thread the setup across an RPC boundary (the burst backend `services.py` read, `burst_mle_analysis`, and smaller tttr/fcs inspection plugins) still need the same one-line adapter and are tracked as follow-up. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Channel-definition setups now carry per-channel LUTs and drive LUT-aware reading (Phase 2).** The detector-setup data model gained three inline, JSON/msgpack-safe top-level keys — `apply_lut` (master gate), `channel_luts` `{ch:[NTAC_fract]}`, `channel_shifts` `{ch:int}` (plus optional `channel_lut_sources` provenance) — added to `DetectorWizardPage.get_settings()` and restored in its setup-load path (`tttr_channel_definition.py`), so the setup is self-contained and round-trips through both MMFDB (`setup_data` blob) and the JSON fallback with **no schema change**. The TCSPC reader-control cascade (`tcspc_tttr_reader_control_widget.py::_apply_detector`) now pushes the selected setup's LUTs/shifts/`apply_lut` onto the reader when a detector is chosen — the "user selects a setup, opens a file → LUT-aware read" trigger. Headless coverage: `test/models/test_detector_setups.py` +2 (MMFDB + JSON round-trip of the LUT keys) and a reader-level check that `TCSPCTTTRReader(apply_lut=True, channel_luts=…)._compute_histogram` changes the decay and is reproducible. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **TTTR reading became LUT-aware at the single open seam (Phase 1 of per-channel TAC linearization throughout chisurf).** `chisurf/core/fio/staging.py::open_tttr` — the Qt-free drop-in for `tttrlib.TTTR(path[,routine])` that every reader flows through — now optionally applies **per-routing-channel TAC-linearization LUTs** (`channel_luts={ch:NTAC_fract}`, gated by a master `apply_lut` bool) and **photon-level wrapping micro-time shifts** (`channel_shifts={ch:int}`). With no correction supplied it is byte-identical to a plain open (the "no setup → raw" fallback). LUT application does tttrlib's `apply_channel_luts` + `apply_luts_and_shifts`; a **key reproducibility finding**: that rebinning is stochastic and `seed=-1`/`0` (what the old `tttr_lut_tools` reference used) yields non-reproducible reads — so the seam pins a **fixed positive dither seed** (`LUT_DITHER_SEED=42`), giving reproducible *and* artifact-free (Felekyan-dithered) linearization. The base reader's `_open_tttr` (`core/experiments/core/reader.py`) forwards these, falling back to the reader's own `channel_luts`/`channel_shifts`/`apply_lut` attributes, and `TCSPCTTTRReader` stores them + stamps `lut_linearized` into the read curve's `meta_data`. Photon-shift logic was **deduped**: the `microtime_shifter` plugin's pure `_apply_shifts`/`_compute_effective_shifts` were promoted to a shared `chisurf/core/fio/tttr_shift.py` (the plugin re-exports them), so the plugin and the reading seam share one wrapping-shift code path. Headless coverage: `test/fio/test_staging.py` +4 (raw-identity, apply_lut gating, fixed-seed reproducibility, channel-shift) — 11 pass; `microtime_shifter` suite 12 pass unchanged. Photon-level (wrapping) and histogram-level (`micro_time_shift`/`vh_shift`, padding) shifts remain deliberately distinct. Next phases fold `channel_luts`/`apply_lut` into the channel-definition setup and wire burst/FCS. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **FIDA added as a fit model in the PCH experiment.** Closes the FIDA gap vs Fretica (chisurf had PCH but no Fluorescence-Intensity Distribution Analysis). `chisurf/core/models/pch/fida.py` (filling the empty core/models/pch stub) computes the photon-counting histogram through the FIDA probability *generating function* with an explicit spatial brightness profile (dV/dx): `G(ξ)=exp{Σ_i N_i ∫w(x)(e^{(ξ-1)q_i x}-1)dx + (ξ-1)λ_bg}`, inverted to `P(k)` by evaluating on the complex unit circle + FFT (port of Fretica `FPCHFida`); plus a `dvdx_gaussian` 3-D-Gaussian profile (`w(x)∝√(-ln x)/x`) and the multinomial `fida_residuals` (Fretica `FPCHFidaFit`). Wired as a non-parse `ModelCurve` **`FidaModel`** ("FIDA", up to 3 species q/N + background) added to the PCH experiment's model list in `experiment_configs.yaml` — the reader/histogram (`PCHReader`) and fit stage already exist, so this is an experiment/model/fit addition like TCSPC/PDA. Headless coverage: `test/models/test_fida.py` **7 pass** (normalisation, Poisson background limit, super-Poissonian broadening, brightness/number scaling, species additivity, Mandel-Q ordering, residuals) and `test/gui/test_pch_models_resolve.py` **2 pass** (resolves + appears in the PCH menu as a ModelCurve). See [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **Burst-MLE file list migrated onto the unified AutoForm path list — file-list unification complete.** The last hand-rolled `FileListWidget(QListWidget)` (`burst_mle_analysis/utils.py`, used at three wizard sites: per-detector IRF + background lists and the burst-file list) now delegates to the shared `PathListWidget` (drag-drop + Files/Folder/**Database**/Remove/Clear with first-class MMFDB). `FileListWidget` became a thin **factory** returning a configured, `checkable` `PathListWidget` with the wizard's small API bound on — `add_file`, `get_selected_files` (→ `checked_paths()` as `Path`s), `clear` (inherited), `setAcceptDrops` (routed to the inner drop list so the per-detector "drop burst files first" gating still works) — and the burstwise folder logic extracted to a reusable `expand_mle_folder` (`**/*.bur` → those, else tttrlib-supported files) passed as the `folder_expander` hook. The subtle bit is the **drop-only callback**: the original fired `file_added_callback` only on a user *drop*, never on a programmatic `add_file`/`clear` (the wizard adds a burst file and then calls `load_burst_data` itself, and that loader *appends* — a second implicit call would double-load the bursts). Preserved exactly by wiring the callback to the inner list's `pathsDropped` signal and making the model's `update()` a no-op, so the one-for-all cross-sync (now a single `set_paths`) and `clear_files` can no longer recurse through the callback. No `PathListWidget` change was needed. Verified against the real BH smFRET DNA data: the full MLE suite **33 pass** (unchanged from baseline) plus a new `test_file_list.py` (**5 pass**) covering drop-fires / add_file-silent, checked-file tracking, drop-enable routing, silent clear, and the folder expander; no new lint (utils −1, wizard ±0). This closes the "no custom file-list widgets" mandate — all Tier-A/B plugin file lists now share one widget. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Enderlein Gauss--Lorentz MDF added as a non-parse FCS fit model.** Closes the non-Gaussian molecule-detection-function gap in the FCS model catalogue (another Fretica port). `chisurf/core/fluorescence/fcs/enderlein.py` ports Fretica's `FEnderleinMDF`/`FVeffEnderlein`/`FGdiffEnderlein`: the MDF `U(ρ,z)=κ(z)/w(z)²·exp(-2ρ²/w²)/norm`, the effective volume `Veff=π(∫κ)²/∫(κ²/w²)` (absolute concentrations), and the diffusion ACF from convolving the MDF with the 3-D propagator — lateral integral analytic, axial convolution by Gauss--Hermite quadrature in the difference direction (`z'=z+√(4Dτ)ξ`) so the propagator is resolved at any lag (a fixed z-grid fails once `√(4Dτ)` < grid spacing). Wired as a **non-parse** `ModelCurve`, `MdfFCSWidget` ("FCS MDF (Gauss--Lorentz)"), registered in `experiment_configs.yaml`/`main_helper.py` alongside `ParseFCSWidget`/`DyeShapeFCSWidget`/`MaxEntFCSWidget`; params N, D, w0/R0, fixed optics; outputs Veff/conc/τ_D. Headless coverage: `test/fluorescence/test_enderlein_fcs.py` **6 pass** (MDF shape, Veff scaling, g(0)=1 + g(0)=1/Veff consistency, D-scaling) and `test/gui/test_fcs_models_resolve.py` **2 pass** (resolves + appears in the FCS menu as a ModelCurve). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **`burst_h2mm` per-burst export now lands on ndxplorer's FRET-line plot.** The `h2mm_bursts.csv` summary previously carried only `Mean Macro Time (s)` + H2MM state columns, so an ndX-loaded run had no lifetime/E axes to plot the static/dynamic FRET line against. `core/export.build_tables` now also writes the MFD column names ndX keys that plot on — `Mean Microtime (green)` (per-burst mean donor micro time in ns, an IRF-uncorrected lifetime proxy; ndX's FRET-line X axis), `Proximity ratio` (its default Y axis) and a measured `FRET efficiency` — built from the donor/acceptor **role stream groups** (divisor-aware, `donor_streams`/`acceptor_streams` kwargs) and the run's `MeasDesc_Resolution` (ns/channel, read in `backend/services._micro_resolution` and threaded through the bundle as `micro_time_ns`). `Mean FRET E` is retained (model-derived per-state, Viterbi-weighted) and clarified as distinct from the measured `FRET efficiency`. Headless coverage: `tests/test_export.py` gains column-presence + finite-range assertions and a new `test_write_result_tables_emits_ndx_fret_line_columns` exercising the services writer end-to-end; burst_h2mm export suite **4 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Recurrence Analysis of Single Particles (RASP) added to the burst workflow.** Closes another Fretica gap (chisurf had no recurrence analysis). `chisurf/core/fluorescence/burst/recurrence.py` extracts slow (ms..s) dynamics from freely-diffusing smFRET by correlating a burst with the bursts that recur shortly after (the same molecule returning to the spot): `same_molecule_probability` = `1 − 1/G(τ)` from the burst-arrival-time autocorrelation (log lags + finite-T edge correction), and `recurrence_efficiencies`/`recurrence_histogram` histogram the FRET efficiencies of bursts recurring within a chosen time window after an initial sub-population. Pure per-burst-table operation (arrival time + proximity ratio, no photon access); surfaced as `Bursts.recurrence(...)` → a `Recurrence` result (`.same_molecule_probability()`, `.recurrence_time(threshold)`, `.histogram(e_range, dt_range_s)`, `.plot()`) in `burst_analysis/api/workflow.py`. Headless coverage: `test/plugins/burst/test_recurrence.py` **5 pass** (Poisson-flat vs clustered-recurrence P_same, recurrence histogram recovering an inter-converting state, time-window selectivity, workflow handle). See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Unified every plugin's brand icon onto a coherent per-family emoji set.** Extending the FCS icon pass to the whole plugin tree: the brand icons across all families were an inconsistent mix of raster `icon.png`, **six dangling `icon.png` references** (rendered as grey letter-fallbacks), and many plugins with no icon at all. Assigned each of ~70 previously-inconsistent plugins a single, themed **emoji** — distinct within its family (cross-family repeats are fine; separate menus) — declared in `manifest.json`'s `icon` field where a manifest exists, or a module `icon` attribute for the manifest-less legacy plugins (e.g. burst `💥`/`🎯`/`📈`, traj `📐`/`🔗`/`💾`, tttr `🚦`/`🖼️`/`✂️`, core `📷`/`🌐`/`📂`, games `🎮`/`💣`/`🏓`). Removed **45** now-superseded/dead `icon.png` assets (the resolver order is manifest icon → on-disk image → module attribute, so a leftover png would shadow a module emoji). Also caught the concurrent-stream's brand-new `burst_2cde`/`burst_ebfret`/`burst_h2mm` manifests, which had no icon key (`⚡`/`🪜`/`🔀`). Headless-verified: all assigned plugins resolve through `create_plugin_icon_with_fallback`/`resolve_plugin_icon` to a non-null emoji pixmap, and a full-tree scan finds **no** plugin left on a raster/blank/dangling icon. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **New `burst_2cde` plugin: FRET-2CDE / ALEX-2CDE burst dynamics feature, on a new tttrlib `BurstFeature` framework.** Closes a Fretica/FRETBursts gap (chisurf had no 2CDE). tttrlib gained a **`BurstFeature`** base class for per-burst *features* — named photon streams (channels + inclusive micro-time windows), an optional bit-exact Laplace(5τ)/Gaussian(3τ) KDE evaluated on the full stream, and a parallel per-burst reducer — with **`TwoCDE`** (FRET-2CDE `110−100[(E)_D+(1−E)_A]` and ALEX-2CDE `100−50[BR_Dex−BR_Aex]`, LAPLACE/GAUSSIAN, nbKDE self-correction) as the first feature and **`BVA` migrated onto the same base** (public API preserved; no hard-coded default channels). The C++ is a bit-exact port of the FRETBursts reference, parity-tested against a NumPy reimplementation (rtol 1e-9); tttrlib burst suite 140 pass / 2 skip (committed in the tttrlib repo). ChiSurf side: `burst_2cde/core/computation.py` drives `tttrlib.TwoCDE` (pure-NumPy fallback), a `2cde compute` CLI + `burst_2cde.jobs.compute`/`.workflow.prepare` RPC services, `2cde/<stem>_0.2cde` sidecars, and a `Bursts.two_cde(...)` → `TwoCde` step in the guided burst workflow facade (`.mean_2cde`, `.dynamic_fraction`, `.plot()`). These are *features* — they compute per-burst values; filtering bursts on them is done downstream (ndX). Headless coverage: `chisurf/plugins/burst/burst_2cde/tests/test_2cde.py` **8 pass**, no regression in `burst_analysis` workflow tests (**11 pass**). See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Pixel-wise imaging MLE gained the fit23/24/25 model selector.** The pixel-wise FLIM tool (`img_pixel_mle`) was hardcoded to fit23; it now offers the same fit2x model selector as the burst wizard, driven through the shared **facade** batch kernel (`fit_matrix_threaded` → `Fit2x.fit_many`) — the fast GIL-released `fit_matrix`-per-thread path that is the whole point of pixel fitting. Engine: `fit_matrix_threaded` allocates the result width from the model (`len(PARAMETER_NAMES[model]) + 1`, not a hardcoded 5), so fit24/fit25 (6 columns) work; `core/pixel_mle.py` builds the τ map from `x[0]` for every model (ρ map fit23-only), and `PixelMleSettings` carries `fit_model` + `initial_values`/`fixed_flags` (falling back to the fit23 `tau`/`gamma`/`r0`/`rho` fields when the latter are `None`, for CLI/RPC back-compat). fit23's per-pixel CSV schema is unchanged byte-for-byte; other models write `tau` + one column per free parameter + `2I*` and drop the fit23-only anisotropy columns. Because the pixel path is bound to the facade batch kernel, its free-parameter set is the **facade** `PARAMETER_NAMES` — so **fit25 has no editable `r0`** here (the facade fixes it as a batch input), unlike the burst wizard's raw-estimator editor; the tail fit is not offered (no `fit_matrix` kernel). GUI: `PixelMleViewModel.view_spec()` is model-aware — it loads the JSON as a dict and injects a `fit_model` combo + one value/fix row per free parameter (bound to static `p0…p4` slots proxying that model's start vector/fixed mask); the combo's `call` fires a `"rebuild"` event that the shared `AutoFormMleTool` defers to the next event-loop tick (`QTimer.singleShot(0, …)`) so the mid-commit combo is not deleted under itself. Verified headlessly on the real FLIM PTU (fit24/fit25 produce a τ map + model-specific columns), plus view-model tests (selector rebuilds the rows, per-model edits stay independent, run() forwards the chosen model) — `img_pixel_mle` **16 pass**, shared MLE + `sm_image_mle` **24 pass**. See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **Standardised the FCS plugin family onto a coherent emoji icon set.** The FCS group's brand icons were an inconsistent mix — raster `icon.png` (`fcs_calculator`, `fcs_filter_calculator`, `fcs_merger`, `flc_2d`, two of them dangling references to a non-existent file), one emoji (`fcs_channel_preset` = `📡`), and several with no icon at all — so the family read as visually incoherent in the menu/ribbon. Gave each plugin a distinct, correlation/diffusion-themed emoji: FCS umbrella `〰️`, Correlator `⚛️`, Calculator `🧮`, fFCS Filter Calculator `🎚️`, Converter `🔁`, Merger `🔀`, Lifetime-FCS Simulator `🎲`, 2D-FLCS `🌈`, Channel Preset `📡` (kept). Set via each plugin's `manifest.json` `icon` field where a manifest exists, or a module `icon` attribute for the three manifest-less legacy plugins; the superseded `icon.png` assets were deleted (the resolver prefers manifest icon → on-disk image → module attribute, so a stale png would otherwise shadow a module-level emoji). Verified headlessly: all nine resolve through `create_plugin_icon_with_fallback`/`resolve_plugin_icon` to a non-null emoji pixmap. This complements the app-wide glyph unification (icons *in* widgets) by making the plugin *brand* icons consistent too. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **FRET-docking PDB rigid-body list migrated onto the unified AutoForm file list; `PathListWidget` gained `allow_duplicates` + `set_paths`.** The `modelling/fret` docking tool's PDB list was the last fully hand-rolled `QListWidget` in the file-list sweep, and a genuinely awkward one: it stored the full path in `Qt.UserRole` while showing basenames, its order encodes `body_id` (0,1,2…), and the **same PDB may legitimately appear twice** (a homodimer reusing one structure for two rigid bodies) — which the shared list's de-duplication would have collapsed. Rather than a special widget, `PathListWidget` grew two general capabilities: **`allow_duplicates`** (keep repeated paths; removal becomes **row-position-based** so a duplicate drops only the selected occurrence — a strict improvement even for non-duplicate lists, which map 1:1 to their rows; mutually exclusive with `checkable`, which keys tick state on the path text) and **`set_paths(paths)`** (replace the list *verbatim* — no folder expansion, no existence filtering — so loading a saved project's file set round-trips order and repeats even if a referenced file is momentarily missing, which `add_paths`'s `_expand` would silently drop). The tool binds the widget through a tiny `_PdbListModel` adapter whose `update()` re-joins the ordered paths into the engine's comma-joined `pdb_paths` string; the redundant toolbar **Add PDB / Remove PDB** actions are gone (the list's own ➕ Files / ➖ Remove / 🗑 Clear replace them), selecting a row previews that structure via the existing ChiMol section, and the PDB list gains drag-drop + MMFDB (`kinds=["structure"]`) for free. Deleted `_pick_pdbs`/`_add_pdb_item`/`_pdb_paths_from_list`/`_remove_pdb`; `_set_pdb_list` (project load) now calls `set_paths`. Tests: three new `test_path_list_section.py` cases (duplicates kept + row-remove, `allow_duplicates`+`checkable` rejected, `set_paths` verbatim incl. a missing file) and a `test_dock_gui.py` case exercising the homodimer/round-trip through the real tool; path_list + fret dock suites **14 pass** (the 8 pre-existing `test_examples`/`test_gui` FPS-editor + olga-data failures are unrelated and fail identically at HEAD). Tenth and final Tier-A plugin; the remaining custom list, burst-MLE's shared `FileListWidget`, is deferred while that plugin is under concurrent edit. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Migrated inline emoji literals to `Glyphs.*` registry references across production GUI code (86 files).** After the canonical `chisurf/gui/glyphs.py` registry landed and all UI glyphs were normalised to canonical literals, the remaining hard-coded emoji literals in Python GUI files were mechanically rewritten to reference the registry: whole-string literals became attribute refs (`icon = "ℹ️"` → `icon = Glyphs.INFO`) and mixed strings became f-strings (`setText("💾 Save")` → `setText(f"{Glyphs.SAVE} Save")`), so a concept's glyph now has exactly one authored source. The rewrite was done by a deterministic, verified converter (each rewrite proven value-equal to the original; the whole tree re-checked so every changed line is glyph-literal- or import-only) and skips docstrings, multi-line help blocks, `.format` templates, tests (kept as literal regression anchors), games, and — deliberately — **directional arrows** `→`/`←` (prose/structural, dominant in sentences, plugin names and log messages) which stay literal, matching the earlier normalization policy. Plugin **brand** icons in `manifest.json`/module `icon` attributes are left as canonical literals. Import blocks re-sorted by ruff; `test/test_glyphs.py` **13 pass**, converted plugins import headlessly with `icon` resolving correctly. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Burst-MLE batch Run export generalised beyond fit23 to every fit2x model (fit24/fit25).** The per-burst batch export (**Run** → `process_bursts` → the multiprocessing `_mp_worker.process_one_file_worker`) was fit23-only, guarded with a warning; it now runs fit23/24/25 through the same worker. The worker builds the **raw tttrlib estimator named by the registry** (`cfg['method']`) instead of the `Fit2x` facade — matching what `wizard.create_fit_instance` builds for the live preview, so batch and preview use the same estimator and the same free-parameter layout (fit25 keeps its trailing `r0` input, which the facade's batch layout drops); the background is area-normalised in a new `_build_fitter` (the same rule `Fit2xSettings.__post_init__` applies). The start vector stays per-detector for fit23 (its authored rows differ per detector) but is a single registry-editor vector for the other models (the editor is not per-detector), read once via the model-aware `fit_parameters` property. Output columns follow the model (`_burst_result_columns` + `_mp_worker._record`): fit23 keeps its historical `.b?4` layout byte-for-byte (tau/gamma/r0/rho + the two anisotropy columns), other models write `Tau` (best lifetime, `x[0]`) plus one column per registry free parameter and drop the fit23-only anisotropy columns; `_save_burst_results_fast` now reindexes so a model that did not emit a column writes NaN rather than raising. The **tail fit** stays preview-only in batch (a different estimator family, under-determined per burst) with a specific warning. Verified by running the real per-file worker headlessly on a synthetic mono-exponential burst for fit24 and fit25 (`test_batch_worker_fits_non_fit23_models`) plus a column-structure unit test (`test_burst_result_columns_are_model_specific`); MLE end-to-end suite **20 pass**, worker net -3 ruff findings. See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **`burst_h2mm` gains profile-likelihood uncertainty (burstH2MM `ll_*_scatter`), complementing the bootstrap.** `core/analysis.profile_likelihood` sweeps each state's E (and S, with an Aex stream) over a ±window with all other parameters held fixed and recomputes the fixed-model forward log-likelihood at each point (`fixed_loglik` = `optimize(..., max_iter=1, tol=0.0).loglik`, the same fixed-model loglik the A/B-vs-H2MM_C tests use); `_model_with_state_e`/`_model_with_state_s` rebuild the emission row preserving untouched mass, and `_ci_from_scan` interpolates the CI where deviance `2·(logLmax−logL)` crosses χ²₁ (3.84). A "📈 LL scan" toolbar action runs it on a Worker and opens `LikelihoodScanDialog` (deviance-vs-parameter curves per state, MLE + threshold markers, likelihood CI shaded, bootstrap CI overlaid dotted). Where bootstrap resamples the *data*, this profiles the *likelihood surface*, so a **flat profile / window-wide CI flags an unidentifiable state** the bootstrap can miss. Tests: `test_profile_likelihood_peaks_at_mle_with_ordered_ci` + a dialog-build assertion; headless-rendered. Plugin suite: 51 pass. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **`burst_h2mm` writes a per-dwell table so dwell filtering can happen at the ndxplorer level.** Dwell filtering (min photons/dwell, drop burst-edge dwells, select by state/duration) is done downstream in ndX — but ndX filters *rows* and the plugin only wrote per-photon + per-burst tables, so a dwell wasn't a filterable unit. Added `core/export.build_dwell_table` → `h2mm_dwells.csv`, one row per Viterbi dwell (`State`, `Number of Photons`, `Dwell Time (ms)`, measured `FRET efficiency`/`Proximity ratio`/`Stoichiometry`, per-colour `Mean Microtime (<name>)` — each dwell is a single state so a per-dwell FRET-line lands on the static line — `Mean Macro Time (s)`, and an `Is Edge` flag = 1 when the dwell touches its burst's first/last photon), built straight from `H2mmAnalysis.dwells`; `write_result_tables` emits it. New tests `test_build_dwell_table_per_dwell_rows_and_edge_flag` + dwells-CSV assertion in the end-to-end writer test. Plugin suite: 50 pass. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **`burst_h2mm` now surfaces its transition kinetics: a rate-matrix panel + rate arrows.** The per-fit transition rates (`H2mmAnalysis.trans_rates`, 1/s) were computed but never shown. Added a "Transition rates (1/s)" heatmap panel (`_plot_rates`: row-major from→to, state-labelled axes, per-cell value labels via `_fmt_rate`) and burstH2MM-style **rate arrows** between state nodes on the dwell FRET panel (`_overlay_trans_arrows`, `trans_arrow_ES`): on the E–S scatter the arrows connect the (E,S) nodes; in 2-colour E-histogram mode a kinetic scheme is drawn above the histogram, line width ∝ rate, the two directions perpendicular-offset. Per-state fluorescence-decay panel returns to half width. Headless render-verified (2-state ALEX + 2-colour); GUI test asserts the rate-matrix labels + arrow items. Plugin suite: 49 pass. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Three more burstH2MM capabilities ported to `burst_h2mm`: bootstrap uncertainty, nanotime-divisor (lifetime-resolved) H2MM, and an ndxplorer FRET-line per-burst export.** (1) **Bootstrap uncertainty** — an on-demand "± Uncertainty" toolbar action resamples bursts with replacement (`_subset_bursts` rebuilds a `BurstPhotons` from the CSR arrays), refits, and overlays per-state E (and S) percentile error bars on the E–S scatter (`bootstrap_uncertainty`, label-aligned by sorting states on E; background Worker, cancelable). (2) **Nanotime divisors** — `bursts_from_dataframe(..., divisors=N)` splits each base stream into N micro-time quantile bins (contiguous per-base blocks, `stream_index // N` = role) so H2MM can separate same-E/different-τ states; the FRET/stoichiometry/measured-E-S helpers and `bootstrap_uncertainty` are now **role-group aware** (a role is a set of stream indices) and `analyze(..., divisors)` derives + stores the donor/acceptor/Aex blocks on `H2mmAnalysis`; `H2mmSettings.divisors` + a "Nanotime divisors" GUI spinbox. (3) **ndX FRET-line export** — instead of an in-plugin E–τ panel (redundant with ndxplorer's static/dynamic FRET-line plot; the panel was added then removed), the per-burst export table (`core/export.py::build_tables`) now carries the MFD column names ndX recognises — `Mean Microtime (green)` (mean donor micro time in ns, IRF-uncorrected donor-lifetime proxy; ndX's FRET-line X axis), `FRET efficiency` and `Proximity ratio` (measured apparent E; its default Y axis) — plus the existing `Dominant State` / `Number of Transitions`, so H2MM results open straight onto that plot (like BVA augmenting the MFD burst table). `services._micro_resolution` reads the TCSPC resolution from the TTTR header. Headless-verified (uncertainty overlays, divisors=2 render, FRET-line column round-trip); new tests `test_bootstrap_uncertainty_brackets_state_fret`, `test_nanotime_divisors_expand_streams_and_recover_fret`, `test_write_result_tables_emits_ndx_fret_line_columns` + GUI overlay assertions. Plugin suite: 49 pass. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **H2MM results panel rebuilt into the full burstH2MM-style plot set, plus optional µsALEX/PIE stoichiometry; matching tttrlib example added.** The `burst_h2mm` GUI grew from four pyqtgraph plots to a six-panel burstH2MM-style dashboard: a dwell **E histogram** (or a dwell **E–S scatter** when an acceptor-excitation stream is present), the **transition-density plot**, **BIC/ICL model selection**, per-state **dwell-time distributions**, per-state **fluorescence-decay** (nanotime) histograms, and an interactive per-burst **Viterbi state-path** viewer (◀/▶ + spinbox, "dynamic bursts only" filter; photons coloured by state, shape by stream). To feed them, `core/analysis.py` now decodes the Viterbi path once and returns it on `H2mmAnalysis.path` alongside a `dwells: list[Dwell]` carrying each dwell's *measured* E/S (photon-count based, not the model emission matrix) — the quantities burstH2MM histograms per state; the legacy `dwell_times`/`transitions`/`populations` are unchanged. A new `state_stoichiometry()` and an **optional third "Acceptor (Aex)" stream** (channels tab combo, default the PIE "yellow" detector) yield per-state `S = (D+A)/(D+A+A_ex)`; with only two streams the analysis degrades cleanly to FRET-E only (`S = NaN`, `H2mmResult.has_alex=False`). All panels except the nanotime decay work without per-photon metadata, so results loaded without `bundle.meta` still render. Headless-verified via offscreen `QWidget.grab()` PNGs (FRET-only + ALEX) and new tests: `test_services.py::{test_analyze_populates_dwells_path_and_measured_es,test_analyze_stoichiometry_with_alex_stream}` and `test_gui.py::test_h2mm_gui_alex_es_and_nanotime`; plugin suite **46 pass**. The companion **tttrlib** tutorial `examples/single_molecule/plot_h2mm_analysis.py` reproduces the same figures on its own C++ `tttrlib.H2MM` engine (`simulate_bursts` → BIC state-count scan → `viterbi_path` → dwell/transition extraction), smoke-tested by `test/python/h2mm/test_examples.py` (recovers the simulated 3-state ground truth). See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).

## 2026-07-22
* **Wired the tttrlib tail fit into the burst-MLE wizard as a selectable model.** The burst-MLE model combo now offers a **Tail fit (multi-exp)** entry alongside the registry-driven fit2x estimators. It is a different fitter family — `tttrlib.DecayFitNExp` with `DecayFitNExpOptions.tail_start` (pure exponentials from a start channel, **no IRF deconvolution**, prompt/rise excluded from the likelihood) — the standard treatment for FRET sensitised-emission decays. Its parameters (a `tail_start` channel + N `tauN` lifetimes) come from a local `_tail_schema()` rather than the fit2x registry but flow through the **same** schema-driven editor (`_rebuild_dyn_params`/`_fit_param_names`, now factored behind a `_model_schema(model)` helper that returns the registry schema for fit2x models and the local schema for `"tail"`). `_run_tail_fit` builds the options from the header `dt`/`period`, calls `DecayFitNExp.fit` (IRF array ignored, background = the detector's), and exposes the result through the fit2x plotting interface via a `SimpleNamespace(data, model)` on `self._fit`, so `plot_fit_result`/`update_fit_ui` are unchanged; the IRF overlay and the IRF-required guard (`_fit_blocked_reason`, the plot early-return) are skipped in tail mode. Batch **Run** export stays fit23-only (the existing non-fit23 guard covers tail). Verified end-to-end through the real wizard on the BH smFRET DNA data: `test_tail_fit_recovers_a_plausible_lifetime` selects the tail model, refits the green decay and asserts a physical recovered τ + a drawn model curve; MLE end-to-end suite **17 pass**. Completes the "surface it in the burst-MLE UI" follow-up from the tttrlib tail-fit entry below. See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **tttr_microtime_lut migrated onto the unified file list (drop-triggers-processing).** The TAC-linearization tool used a bare `QListWidget` + an `installEventFilter` drag-drop hook that showed *basenames* while the real paths lived in `self.files`, and a drop called `_process_files` — which loaded micro-times and refreshed the plots (i.e. the list *is* the processing trigger). Replaced it with the shared `PathListWidget` (drag-drop replaces the list via `replace_on_drop`, + Files/Folder/Database/Remove/Clear + MMFDB) bound through a `_FileListModel` adapter over `self.files`; the model's `update()` calls a new `_on_files_changed` that dispatches to `_process_files` (non-empty) or a new `_reset_state` (empty, extracted from the old `_clear_files`). Removed the `eventFilter`, the Load/Clear buttons and `_load_files`; `_process_files` no longer manages storage/display (the list owns both) and just loads + plots. Display now shows full paths instead of basenames (consistent with the other migrated tools). The widget can't be constructed under pytest (pre-existing pyqtgraph/tttrlib abort, no prior tests) so behaviour was verified via `python -c` smoke (build, add→load-and-graceful-fail on a non-TTTR file, clear→reset); new `tests/test_widgets.py` unit-tests the pure `_FileListModel` adapter (**2 pass**). No new lint. Ninth plugin. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **microtime_histogram migrated onto the unified file list; AutoForm file drop gained `replace_on_drop` + a rejected-drop signal.** The plugin had two hand-rolled checkable `FileListWidget(QListWidget)` lists (main TTTR + BID) with clear-on-drop, burstwise-folder BUR/BST globbing on the BID list, and a `.spc`-needs-manual-type warning. To migrate without a special widget, two general capabilities were added: (1) `PathDropListWidget` now emits **`pathsRejected`** alongside `pathsDropped` (paths the `path_filter` rejected, so a host can warn instead of silently dropping), re-exposed on `PathListWidget` as **`rejectedPaths`**; and (2) `PathListWidget` gained a **`replace_on_drop`** option (a drop replaces the list instead of appending). Both lists are now `checkable` `PathListWidget`s bound via a `_FileListModel` adapter whose `update()` fires the host load-callback **only when non-empty** — so a programmatic `clear()` no longer re-triggers the callbacks (matching the old `QListWidget.clear`) and avoids a clear-cascade between the two lists. The BID list uses `folder_expander` (`_expand_bid_folder`: `**/*.bur` + `**/*.bst`); the `.spc` warning rides `rejectedPaths` → `_warn_needs_type_selection` (faithfully preserving that `tttr_filetype` returns `None` for "Auto", so the original check was already inert). Deleted the custom `FileListWidget`; `get_selected_files()` → `checked_paths()`, per-file `add_file` loops → `add_paths(list)`. Verified via manual headless smoke (build, add, clear-no-cascade, BID expand, checked, `lifetime_analysis` embed still builds) since the widget can't be constructed under pytest (pre-existing — detector-wizard/tttrlib abort the interpreter headlessly); new `test_widgets.py` covers the pure `_expand_bid_folder`, and `test_path_list_section.py` gained `replace_on_drop` + `rejectedPaths` cases. path_list + dock_tool + microtime suites **19 pass**; wizard shed 8 ruff findings (removed dead Qt imports). Eighth plugin. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Unified the "emoticon" glyph icons app-wide behind a canonical registry.** The same UI concept was drawn with several different glyphs (delete as `🗑️`/`➖`/`❌`/`✕`, refresh as `🔄`/`🔁`/`♻️`/`↻`, search as `🔎`/`🔍`, plus inconsistent emoji **variation selectors** so text-default symbols like `⚙`/`✏` rendered monochrome in some places and colour in others). Added `chisurf/gui/glyphs.py` as the single source of truth — named canonical constants grouped by concept (`Glyphs.SAVE/DELETE/REFRESH/SEARCH/…`) plus an idempotent, Qt-free `normalize(text)` that rewrites the *unambiguous* variants (synonyms `🔎→🔍`, `✎→✏️`, `✖→✕`; VS16 completion `⚙→⚙️`, `🗑→🗑️`, …) while leaving directional arrows, painter-drawn tree/timeline markers and plugin **brand** icons alone. Ran `normalize` across the tree (**34 files**, incl. the tests that assert UI strings, kept in sync), excluding `glyphs.py` (its map keys are the bare variants) and `node_editor/timeline_widget.py` (an intentional monochrome symbol set `+ ⇄ ✖ ↔ ✎ ≡ …`). Then fixed the genuine **semantic** collisions `normalize` deliberately skips: interactive Cancel/Close `❌→✕` (4 sites; `❌` reserved for error status) and refresh/reload `↻→🔄` (5 sites). Wired `setup_selector.py` to `Glyphs.REFRESH` as the reference usage; new/edited GUI code should reference `Glyphs.*`, JSON view/manifest files use the same canonical literals.
