Icon *rendering* stays in `icon_utils.py`. Tests: `test/test_glyphs.py` (13 pass) covers rewrite/idempotency/no-double-selector. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **More emoticon harmonization: import/export arrows → tray glyphs, folder-browse → open-folder.** Extended the glyph unification to two more collision families: Import/Export actions drawn with directional arrows `⬇️`/`⬆️` now use the canonical tray glyphs `📥`/`📤` (MMFDB admin File menu + toolbar, ai_settings "Fetch models"); genuine *download* arrows (updater, spectra-downloader brand icon) and folder-*destination* labels are left alone. Browse/Choose buttons drawn with the closed-folder `📁` now use the open-folder `📂` (MMFDB admin Browse, plugin-manager Choose Image), matching `Glyphs.OPEN` vs `Glyphs.FOLDER`. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Generalised the PTU Header Editor into a format-agnostic TTTR Header Editor.** The tool (`chisurf/plugins/tttr/ptu_header_edit` → renamed `tttr_header_edit`) was hard-wired to PicoQuant PTU on both ends despite `tttrlib` normalising *every* container it reads (PTU, HT3, Becker&Hickl SPC, Photon-HDF5) into the identical `{name,type,value,idx}` tag list — verified by reading the repo's HT3/SPC/PTU fixtures, which all expose the same `header.json` tag structure. So reading generalises for free: `load_ptu` → `load_file` (auto-detects the container via `tttrlib.inferTTTRContainerTypeFromExtension`; `load_ptu` kept as an alias), the Open dialog + drag-drop now accept `*.ptu *.ht3 *.spc *.hdf5 *.h5` (new `OPEN_FILTER`), and a status line shows the detected format + tag count (`source_container`/`source_summary`). The **write** side stays PTU on purpose: probing `TTTR.write` across containers showed only the PTU writer persists arbitrary edited tags — the HT3/SPC writers regenerate a fixed binary header and silently drop custom tags — so a header edited from any source is saved as a new PTU with the source photon events copied verbatim (labels/messages say "Save as PTU"). Renamed the plugin dir + all references (`tttr_toolbox` panels.json/manifest/docs/tests, entrypoint role `ptu_header_editor`→`tttr_header_editor`, persist key, `name = "TTTR:Editor:TTTR Header editor"`, window title, README). Tests: parametrised read test over PTU/HT3/SPC + a "save-from-HT3 writes PTU with edited tags" round-trip; the stale PTU round-trip `== n_tags` assertion (tttrlib 0.27's PTU writer now adds a derived `MeasDesc_NumberMicrotimes` tag) relaxed to a tag-name superset check; header-editor + toolbox suites **13 pass**. See [plugins/tttr.md](/plugins/tttr.md).
* **TTTR Time-Windows migrated onto the unified file list; AutoForm file drop gained a `path_filter` hook.** `tttr_time_windows` was Tier-B — it used the bare `PathDropListWidget` plus a hand-rolled `_add_paths`/`_refresh_file_list` (which also mirrored the list into a preview combo) and *separate* toolbar 📂 Add / 🗄 Database actions + a File-menu Add. Replaced the list with the shared `PathListWidget` bound via a `_FileListModel` adapter over the tool's canonical `_file_paths`; the combo-mirror + preview logic moved to an `_on_files_changed` handler driven by the model's `update()`. The redundant toolbar Add/Database actions and the File-menu Add are removed (the list's own ➕ Files / 📁 Folder / 🗄 Database buttons replace them); 🕐 Process / 🗑️ Clear (reset-all: now `file_list.clear()` + result/plot/log reset) / ℹ️ Help stay. To preserve acceptance of compressed `*.ptu.gz` TTTR files (which a plain suffix test misses), `PathListWidget` gained a general **`path_filter`** option — a `callable(str)->bool` predicate that overrides the extension check in both drop-acceptance and folder expansion — and the tool passes its existing `_is_supported_path`. Removed `_add_paths`/`_refresh_file_list`/`_add_files_dialog`/`_add_from_mmfdb`; obsolete tests rewritten to the unified API + a new `path_filter` case in `test_path_list_section.py`; time-windows + path_list suites **11 pass**. Seventh plugin (after shifter, vv_vh ×2, fcs ×2, tttr_lut_tools). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **TTTR LUT Tools file list migrated onto the unified AutoForm file list.** `tttr_lut_tools`' settings panel (`gui/settings_panel.py`) dropped its hand-rolled `FileListWidget(QListWidget)` (own drag/drop + `add_files`/`current_paths`/`clear_files` + `filesChanged` signal) and the separate 📂 Add / 🧹 Clear buttons for the shared `PathListWidget` (drag-drop + Files/Folder/**Database** MMFDB/Remove/Clear), bound via a small `_FileListModel` adapter whose `update()` drives the existing `_files_changed` reload. The custom widget class is deleted; consumers now read `file_list.paths()`. Its `LUTListWidget` is left as-is — a *single*-LUT drop target (emits `lutDropped`, `break` after the first file), which is the single-file-drop pattern, not a file list. The near-identical `tttr_settings_generator` was intentionally **not** migrated: it is `deprecated=True` / menu-hidden ("Use TTTR:LUT Tools instead"). Panel builds and the list works (MMFDB button present); no plugin tests exist there; lint unchanged. Sixth plugin in the file-list unification (after shifter, vv_vh ×2, fcs ×2). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **AutoForm file list gained checkable + folder-expander support; FCS + burst-FCS correlators migrated (no special file-list widgets left there).** To migrate the two correlators without a bespoke widget, `PathListWidget` (the `path_list` section) grew two general capabilities: (1) **`checkable`** — per-entry tick boxes (default *checked*, state tracked as an `_unchecked` set so it survives the widget's full-list refreshes), ☑ All / ☐ None buttons, a `checked_paths()` accessor and a `checkChanged(list[str])` signal; and (2) a **`folder_expander`** hook — an optional `callable(Path)->list[str]` that replaces the default recursive extension-filtered scan when a folder is added. With these, `fcs_correlator`'s wizard page dropped its hand-rolled checkable `FileListWidget` (drag-drop + per-item checkboxes + context menu + `all_files`/`checked_files`) for a `checkable` `PathListWidget` bound via a `_FileListModel`; its BST-aware step-availability logic now rides the `checkChanged` signal. `burst_fcs_correlator` dropped its `BurstFileListWidget(FileListWidget)` subclass entirely — the burstwise-folder→BUR/BST resolution is now a plain `expand_burst_folder()` function passed as the `folder_expander`, via a thin `make_burst_file_list()` factory used by both its wizard and dock tool. Both custom `QListWidget` subclasses are deleted; both correlators gain MMFDB file selection for free. Tests: new checkable-mode case in `test_path_list_section.py`, fcs test updated to `add_paths`; path_list + fcs + burst-FCS suites **22 pass**; fcs wizard shed 9 ruff findings (removed unused Qt/typing imports). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Rebuilt the ChiMOL `cmd` layer as a declarative, signature-driven command language (PyMOL-parity architecture).** Ported PyMOL's four-piece design to Python introspection: a `@command` decorator + `collect_commands` registry with a minimal-unique-prefix `Shortcut` index (`registry.py`), and a central comma/keyword/bracket/quote-aware tokenizer + `inspect.signature` binder with annotation-driven coercion and `raw1`/`raw2` verbatim-tail modes (`argparse2.py`, `selection_types.Selection`). Migrated **every** command mixin (loader/rendering/selection/measurements/editing/animation/exporting/lifecycle + base `help`/`quit`/`exit`) from the old `_cmd_x(args: List[str])` + `_mixin_commands()` handlers to typed `@command` methods, so adding a command is now writing one annotated method and parsing/keyword-args/coercion/Python-API come free (`align a, b, cutoff=3, cycles=10` binds natively). Removed the dual-dispatch adapter — `base.py:do()` resolves solely through the registry — and deleted the dead `cmd/core.py` (2556-line legacy monolith) and never-registered `cmd/rmf.py`. External callers (`cli.py`, `command_dock.py`) now read the command list via the new `Cmd.command_names()`. Added `count_atoms` and `set_name` as the payoff. **Fixed a latent Python-3.11+ bug** in `sele_parser.py`: the per-token patterns carried inline `(?i)` flags that, once joined with `|`, landed mid-expression and made `TOKEN_REGEX` fail to compile — silently breaking every selection evaluation (color/select/alter/remove/align on real structures); hoisted to a single `re.IGNORECASE`. Full chimol suite now green with **nothing excluded** (136 passed), where alignment/editing/selection-grammar previously had to be skipped. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **Parameter tables can hide their Lo/Hi/Bounds columns behind a per-panel "bounds" toggle.** To keep the Lifetime editor's tables narrow (bounds are also editable in the parameter details popup), both table widgets — `ParameterGroupTableWidget` and the paired `PairedParameterTableWidget` — gained `has_bounds_columns()` + `set_bounds_visible()` that show/hide the `bounds_lo`/`bounds_hi`/`bounds_on` columns (respecting the section's `columns` whitelist; the paired widget re-applies the choice across add/remove rebuilds). A `panel` view-spec section with `bounds_toggle:true` (new `PanelSection` field) puts a small checkable "bounds" `QToolButton` in its fold-box header that toggles every bounds-capable table in the panel at once, starting **hidden**. `CollapsibleBox` gained `add_header_widget()` (header restructured into an HBox) so the toggle sits on the existing header row at **zero extra vertical cost**; wired in `auto_form.py`'s `_build_panel` via `_add_bounds_toggle`. `lifetime.view.json` sets `bounds_toggle:true` on all five panels. Verified headlessly (bounds hidden by default; toggling all five reveals Lo/Hi/Bounds in every table); extended the integration test to assert the toggle exists, columns start hidden, and `set_bounds_visible` flips them. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Added tail-fitting to tttrlib's multi-exponential decay fitter (for FRET sensitised emission).** `DecayFitNExp` (the bounded multi-exponential Poisson reconvolution fit, `FitNExp`) gained a `tail_start` option in `DecayFitNExpOptions` (`~/dev/tttrlib`). When `tail_start >= 0` the fit becomes a **tail fit**: each exponential component is a pure decay `exp(-(i - tail_start)·dt/tau)` from that channel (no IRF reconvolution — the IRF shape is bypassed), and the pre-tail channels (the rise/prompt) are excluded from the likelihood by zeroing their pooled counts (the EM/NLL already skip `counts <= 0`). This is the standard treatment for sensitised-emission decays, whose rise is not a simple instrument response. Implemented as a branch in `convolved_component` (pure exponential from `tail_start`) plus a count mask in `DecayFitNExp::fit`; `-1` (default) keeps the normal reconvolution fit, so existing callers are unchanged. Verified on a synthetic decay (a non-IRF prompt bump + a τ=3 ns tail): the tail fit recovers **2.99 ns** while the un-masked fit is pulled to **6.76 ns** by the prompt. Test `test_tail_fit_recovers_lifetime_ignoring_the_prompt` in `test_DecayFitNExp.py`; full decayfit suite **57 pass / 1 skip** (no regression). Surfacing it in the burst-MLE UI is a separate step — `DecayFitNExp` is a different fitter family from the fit2x estimators (different construction/call), so it needs its own path rather than the fit2x selector.
* **Burst-analysis Data Selection migrated onto the unified AutoForm `path_list`; dead standalone-Channels code removed.** The workflow's step-1 `BurstDataSelectionWidget` had a hand-rolled panel — a `PathDropListWidget` plus its own Import files… / Import folder… / Select from MMFDB… / Clear buttons, a custom `add_paths` (folder expansion + dedup), and a bespoke `MmfdbDatasetPickerDialog` path (`_select_mmfdb_dataset` / `_open_mmfdb_dataset`). Replaced with the shared `PathListWidget` (`path_list` section: drag-drop + ➕ Files / 📁 Folder / 🗄 Database / ➖ Remove / 🗑 Clear + first-class MMFDB), bound via a nested `_FileListModel` adapter (`paths: list[str]` + `update()` — kept a separate object so `update` doesn't shadow `QWidget.update`). The adapter's `_on_paths_committed` rebuilds the canonical `list[Path]`, imports newly-added local files to MMFDB (`_import_path_to_mmfdb`, preserved) and notifies the workflow; `paths()` / `mmfdb_payload()` / `add_paths()` / `clear()` stay as the stable external API. MMFDB dataset resolution now lives in the shared picker (`picker.pick_local_paths`), so the per-panel `_open_mmfdb_dataset` and the `selections` provenance dict are dropped (the old code re-imported picked files anyway). Separately, the standalone **Channels step** — already gone from `BURST_PANELS` (channels come from the detector setup the Burst Selection step picks) — had its dead plumbing removed: the `_channel_selection` factory, the `role == "channels"` bind branch, `_bind_channel_panel`, `_on_channel_definitions_changed`, and the channels-panel path in `_sync_channel_context` (now sources only from `_channel_settings_from_selection`). Tests: `test_data_selection_uses_the_shared_path_list_widget` (+ updated import test), the panel-order test already asserts no `channels` role; all 8 workflow panels load; burst_analysis + selection suites **42 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **VV/VH anisotropy + G-factor batch windows migrated onto the unified AutoForm file list (file-list unification, batch 2).** Both plugins carried a near-identical hand-rolled `FileDropList(QListWidget)` (own dragEnter/dragMove/drop + `add_files`) plus Add…/Remove/Clear buttons and `_on_add`/`_on_remove`/`_on_clear` handlers. Replaced each with the shared `PathListWidget` (`path_list` section — drag-drop + ➕ Files / 📁 Folder / 🗄 Database / ➖ Remove / 🗑 Clear + first-class MMFDB), bound via a tiny `_FileListModel` adapter (`files: list[str]` + an `on_change` that clears stale results when the list empties). The batch runners now read `self.file_list.paths()` instead of iterating `QListWidget` items; the redundant Add/Remove/Clear buttons and their handlers are gone, and the now-unused `QListWidget`/`QListWidgetItem`/`QAbstractItemView`/`QLabel` imports were dropped (net −5 ruff findings each). Both plugins gain MMFDB file selection for free. vv_vh suites **9 pass**; follows the micro-time-shifter reference (`ad9756b7`). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Lifetime & rotation components now render as paired tables in the TCSPC Lifetime editor.** The Lifetimes (xₗ/τₗ) and Anisotropy rotation (bᵢ/ρᵢ) groups were the last verbose spin-box grids in the otherwise table-based Lifetime editor (Generic, Corrections, Anisotropy r0/g/l1/l2 already used `parameter_group_table`). Added a `style` field to the `dynamic_group` view-spec section (`chisurf/core/dataspec/__init__.py`): the default `"grid"` keeps the standalone spin-box rows, while the new `"table"` renders each `row_width`-parameter component as **one row** of a paired `QTableView` — a `#` index column then the compact `value`/`fixed`/`bounds_lo`/`bounds_hi`/`bounds_on`/`error` columns repeated per slot, so the amplitude block sits beside its lifetime block (author-approved layout). New `PairedParameterTableModel`/`PairedParameterTableWidget` + a rich-text `QHeaderView` (renders `x<sub>l</sub>`→xₗ and the bare entity `&rho;`→ρ with the per-component index stripped) in `chisurf/gui/autoform/sections/parameter_table.py`; the widget reuses the existing table's cell logic (factored a shared `_set_param_value`), `FittingParameterProxyController` link/detail actions, and copy/paste, and rebuilds on add/remove. Wired in `auto_form.py`'s `_build_dynamic_group`; `lifetime.view.json` sets `style:"table"` on both groups. Verified headlessly (offscreen editor grab shows both paired tables; ρ header renders correctly); extended `test/gui/test_model_editor_integration.py` to assert the paired tables render (width 2, 13 columns), existing parameter-table suites **38 pass**, renderer + view-spec suites green. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Fixed fit25 in the registry (missing r0) and excluded pattern-only fit26 from the burst selector.** Selecting fit25 in the burst-MLE panel crashed — `Fit25.__call__` needs a length-6 `initial_values` (`x[:6] = …`), but the tttrlib `fit` registry advertised only 5 (`[tau1,tau2,tau3,tau4,gamma]`), omitting **r0**, so the schema-built vector was one short (`could not broadcast (5,) into (6,)`). Root-caused to the registry being out of step with the estimator; fixed it at the source (`~/dev/tttrlib/src/FitRegistry.cpp`): fit25's `params_schema` now lists `r0` (fixed-by-default, in `initial_values` order after gamma) and the description matches. chisurf's `_fit_param_names` now uses the schema `properties` order (the full `initial_values` layout the registry guarantees) rather than `required`, so the built vector is always the length the fit expects. Also fit26 (pattern fractioning) takes two reference decays, not an IRF — its `Fit26.__init__(pattern_1, pattern_2, …)` rejects the burst workflow's dt/IRF/background construction — so the selector now offers only estimators whose class is fit2x-constructible (`_is_fit2x_constructible`, derived from the class signature, not a hardcoded name): fit23/24/25 are offered, fit26 is skipped. Verified all offered models fit a synthetic decay with schema-derived params (fit25 x0 length 6, class Fit25) and the four-model switch is crash-free; tttrlib rebuilt, decayfit cross-language/batch suites unchanged (8), chisurf MLE + fit-model-form + fit2x suites **19 pass**. NB two follow-ups the user asked for remain: a "load reference pattern" option so fit26 becomes usable, and a tail-fit estimator in tttrlib (for FRET sensitised emission).
* **Burst-MLE actions consolidated into a full-width toolbar across the top of the panel.** The action controls were scattered down the Burst-MLE controls column — the green Run button at the very bottom, "to default" save wedged next to Min photons, "Optimize target" inside the fit-parameter grid, and the one-click Auto-optimize / Auto IRF-BG / IRF-model / IRF&BG actions in their own row. Consolidated all eight (Run, Auto-optimize, Auto IRF/BG, IRF-model combo, IRF & Background…, Optimize + iteration count, Save) into a single action bar at the **top of the whole `tab_parameters` page, above the controls|plots splitter** (`_build_parameters_tab`: the page is now a QVBoxLayout of [toolbar, splitter]), so the bar spans the full Burst-MLE panel width. It is a plain `QFrame` hosted as a widget — NOT `addToolBar()`, which would not render in the embedded workflow (only the central widget is embedded) — using the reusable `FlowLayout` (`gui/widgets/dock_area/dock_stacked_tab_bar.py`) so the buttons **wrap to another row when narrow** instead of hiding overflow behind a `»` menu. A first cut placed the bar inside the narrow left controls column, where the embedded dock clipped its wrapped rows and covered the buttons; moving it above the splitter (full width) fixed that. Labels shortened for the bar (🎯 Optimize, 📁 IRF/BG…, 💾 Save). Verified headlessly in both the standalone window and the embedded `burst_analysis` dock (all eight visible in one row, controls + plots intact below). Regression test `test_action_buttons_live_in_the_top_toolbar`; MLE + workflow suites **40 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Micro-time shifter migrated onto the unified AutoForm file list — the reference migration for the "no custom file lists" mandate.** The shifter had a hand-rolled file panel: a bare `PathDropListWidget` plus ~150 lines of custom `_add_paths` / `_refresh_file_list` / `_on_file_selected` / context-menu / remove / clear, and a *bespoke* MMFDB loader (a toolbar "📂 Load…" opening `MmfdbDatasetPickerDialog` itself). Replaced the whole panel with the shared `PathListWidget` (the `path_list` AutoForm section, which already carries drag-drop + ➕ Files / 📁 Folder / 🗄 Database / ➖ Remove / 🗑 Clear and first-class MMFDB selection), bound via a tiny `_FileListModel` adapter to the tool's canonical `_file_paths` (kept as the source of truth for batch processing; the adapter avoids giving the `QWidget` tool an `update()` that would collide with `QWidget.update`). The bespoke toolbar Load action is gone — local + database loading now come from the list's own buttons. To let a hand-built host preview the highlighted file, `PathListWidget` gained a general public API: a `selectionChanged(list[str])` signal, a `select_first` option (auto-select row 0 after a refresh), and `add_paths` / `paths` / `clear` / `selected_paths` / `select_index`. The shifter preview now hangs off `selectionChanged`. Net −~150 lines of duplicated widget code from this one plugin. Tests: shifter suite updated to the new API (**29 pass / 2 skip**); new `test_path_list_section.py` cases for the public API + selection signal (**6 pass**); no new lint. This is the pattern the remaining ~15 custom-file-list plugins (tttr settings/LUT/histogram, vv_vh pair, fcs/burst lineage, …) follow. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Burst-MLE fit page now fits fit24/25/26 interactively — parameter editor + estimator class both driven by the tttrlib fit registry (no hardcoding).** Building on the fit-model combo, the interactive fit is generalised: `create_fit_instance` takes the estimator **class from the registry entry's `method` field** (`_fit_class()`, not a model→class table); a `_rebuild_dyn_params(model)` builds the parameter rows (label/initial/fix/result) **from the schema** — `title`, `default`, `minimum`/`maximum`, and `fixed_default` per parameter — so switching to fit24 shows "Lifetime 1 tau1 (ns)/Scatter fraction/Lifetime 2 tau2 (ns)/Fraction of tau2/Constant offset (offset fixed by default)", fit25 shows its four-lifetime selector, etc.; `fit_parameters` reads that editor in schema order and `update_fit_ui` writes the results back the same way (`twoIstar` → a Score field). fit23 keeps its authored rows untouched (they carry the anisotropy r_scatter/r_exp extras) and the two panels swap visibility, so fit23 has zero regression risk. All four estimators share the `Fit23` constructor + `__call__(data, initial_values, fixed, include_model)` interface, so only the class/params differ. The batch **Run** export stays fit23 (per-burst columns + multiprocessing worker) and now warns for other models rather than mis-exporting; the live fit + data/model/residual plot are model-generic. Verified by offscreen render (fit24 editor built entirely from the schema) and headless model-switching across fit23→24→25→26→23; suites (MLE sim, fit-model form, workflow) **21 pass**. See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **Burst-analysis navigation streamlined + fit-model selector added to the MLE page.** Per the requested workflow shape (`burst_analysis/gui/tool.py` `BURST_PANELS`): **removed the standalone "2. Channels" step** — channels now come from the detector setup the Burst Selection step already selects (which carries `show_channel_selection=True`); the coordinator's `_sync_channel_context` gained a `_channel_settings_from_selection()` fallback that reads the selected saved setup (`load_detector_setups`) and publishes it to the shared `DetectorSetupClient`, so downstream MLE/BVA/H2MM still receive the definition. **Moved "IRF & Background" below the separator** (next to Background, unnumbered — it is auto-button driven), and renumbered the pipeline to 1 Data Selection · 2 Burst Selection · 3 BVA · 4 MLE-Lifetime · 5 H2MM · 6 Browser. Updated `test_burst_workflow_panel_order` (asserts the new order + `channels` role absent); burst-analysis workflow suite **11 pass**. On the MLE-Lifetime page (`burst_mle_analysis/wizard.py`): added a **fit-model combo** at the top of the Fit-parameters section, populated from tttrlib's `fit` registry (fit23/24/25/26 via `FitModelForm`'s data source); only fit23 is wired end-to-end today, so selecting another model surfaces a status note and reverts to fit23 rather than silently mis-fitting (`fit_model` property + `_on_fit_model_changed`). This lands alongside the earlier same-page layout work (controls↔plot `QSplitter`, compact τ/γ/r₀/ρ labels + tooltips). Full multi-model execution (reflowing the parameter editor from the schema and generalising the fit23-coupled create_fit_instance/plot/export) remains the follow-up. Verified by offscreen render + construction; affected suites (workflow, MLE sim, fit-model form, IRF background) **28 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Merged the retired `_dev/fluorophore_db` into `spectra_downloader` — one plugin now owns the whole scrape→stage→push pipeline.** The two were not code-duplicated so much as split: `spectra_downloader` (the registered scraper/curation plugin) *imported* its backbone — the `mmfdb_adapter` (`FluorophoreDatabase(MFDatabase)` + the `COMPONENT_KINDS` taxonomy + `DEFAULT_DATABASE_PATH`) — from `_dev/fluorophore_db`, which also still shipped a **standalone curation table UI** (`db_manager_widget.py` + `editor.py`) that `spectra_downloader/browser.py` had already superseded, plus the 39 MB staging `spectra.db`, and was still menu-visible under Plugins→Dev. Moved `mmfdb_adapter.py` and the tests into `spectra_downloader/`, relocated the staging DB (+ WAL sidecars, `.bak`s, `backups/`) beside it (`DEFAULT_DATABASE_PATH` is `Path(__file__).with_name("spectra.db")`, so it follows the module automatically), repointed all ~14 adapter imports (`chisurf.plugins._dev.fluorophore_db.mmfdb_adapter` → `chisurf.plugins.spectra_downloader.mmfdb_adapter`), and deleted the duplicate standalone UI + the `_dev/fluorophore_db` package (removing the Dev-menu entry). Added `.gitignore` rules for the DB at its new home (the previous `chisurf/plugins/_dev*` blanket rule no longer covers it). Verified: adapter + `get_db()` + every downloader import (except `thorlabs`, which needs the optional `openpyxl`, unrelated), `DEFAULT_DATABASE_PATH` resolves to the relocated existing DB, moved tests + the dedupe suite **36 pass / 1 skip**. See [plugins/sample-spectra.md](/plugins/sample-spectra.md).
* **Synthetic-decay generator gained a lifetime-spectrum Load button, and the TCSPC experiment simulator now shares its canonical generator.** Two linked changes. (1) The interactive Synthetic Decay tool (`fluorescence_decay/synthetic_decay`) had Add/Remove/Generate/Save but no way to load a spectrum; added a **📂 Load** button (`SyntheticDecayViewModel.load_spectrum`) that reads a CSV/text file as either a flat interleaved `a1,τ1,a2,τ2,…` list or a 2-column `(amplitude, lifetime)` table — the same convention as the TCSPC simulator experiment setup — validates it via `validate_lifetime_spectrum`, and fills the table. (2) Per "the generator should also back experiment", `core.experiments.tcspc.simulator.TCSPCSimulatorSetup.read()` was converged onto the canonical `core.fluorescence.decay.synthetic_decay` instead of calling the low-level `calculate_fluorescence_decay` directly. To keep the acquisition simulator's ability to model **rise terms (negative amplitudes)** and **zero-lifetime components**, `synthetic_decay` gained an `allow_rise_terms` flag that relaxes the spectrum validation to *finite-only* (the strict default — used by the interactive tool — still requires positive lifetimes and non-negative, not-all-zero amplitudes). The reader calls it with `normalize=False` and amplitude-normalises (÷Σamp) outside, which reproduces the old builder's `normalize=True` **exactly by linearity** (verified max|Δ| ≈ 1e-16 across normal, rise-term and zero-lifetime spectra) while no longer mutating the spectrum in place. New tests in `synthetic_decay/test/test_core.py` (**4**: rise-term parity, reader-uses-canonical-generator, interleaved + 2-column load); synthetic-decay + editor suites **31 pass**, no new lint. See [subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md](/subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md).
* **New Converter hub aggregates the file-conversion tools into one menu entry.** Added `chisurf/plugins/tttr/converter` (`Tools:Converter`, visible), a navigation-panel shell that embeds `tttr_splitter` (TTTR Split/Convert), `tttr_time_windows` (TTTR→Time-Window BIDs) and `bid_to_analysis` (BID→Analysis) as lazily-loaded panels from a data-driven `gui/panels.json`. The three sub-tools are now `menu_hidden` (manifest flag for `tttr_time_windows`; module-level `menu_hidden` attr for the legacy `bid_to_analysis`; `tttr_splitter` was already hidden) so they appear only inside the hub — mirroring how the Games and TTTR-Tools hubs hide their members. Refactor: promoted the previously-duplicated `_embed_mainwindow` QMainWindow-flattening helper and the `panels.json` loader into shared `chisurf/gui/widgets/navigation.py` as `embed_mainwindow()` and `load_panels_json()`; `tttr_toolbox` and `structure_tools` now consume the shared helpers instead of local copies. Tests: 4 converter widget/manifest tests (shared shell, data-driven panels, sub-tools hidden, hub visible); headless screenshot verified all three panels load. See [plugins/tttr.md](/plugins/tttr.md).
* **Burst-MLE fit page: resizable controls↔plot splitter + compact labels; plus a registry-driven fit-model form ready to embed.** The MLE-Lifetime page packed the controls and the two plots into one grid where horizontal space was scarce. Reworked the page (`burst_mle_analysis/wizard.py`) so the controls (left) and the plots (right) live in a horizontal `QSplitter` (`_mle_splitter`, non-collapsible, plots stretch) — the user can now give either side more room. Shortened the space-hungry labels and moved the detail to tooltips: "Fit Start/Stop"→"Range", "Minimum Photons:"→"Min photons", "Shift (VV/VH)"→"Shift", "Scatter Countrate [Hz]"→"Scatter [Hz]", "G factor"→"G", and the fit-parameter rows to the Greek symbols "τ [ns]/γ/r₀/ρ [ns]". Verified by an offscreen render (controls left, residual+intensity plots right, labels compact). Separately, the fit-model selection the burst- and pixel-MLE panels lacked is now available as a reusable registry-driven widget: `chisurf/gui/widgets/fit_model_form.py` `FitModelForm` renders tttrlib's `fit` registry (fit23/24/25/26) into a model chooser + AutoForm, mirroring `BurstSearchForm` (see the earlier registry log entry). Embedding it into the wizard's fit-parameter panel — replacing the hardcoded τ/γ/r₀/ρ rows and generalising the fit23-coupled execution — is the remaining step. See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **AutoForm image-browser now fills its panel vertically.** `ImageBrowserWidget` (the `image_browser` section) set only `AUTOFORM_REFRESH` and no size policy, so when nested inside a `panel` (the TTTR image browser, `tttr_image_browser/gui/browser.view.json`) AutoForm's panel builder found no expanding child and appended a trailing `addStretch(1)` that ate the leftover height, pinning the browser to its size hint (the "unused vertical space" the user saw). It looked fine only where it is a direct `dock_area` child (clsm_generator, sm_image_mle), which the dock masks. Fix (`chisurf/gui/autoform/sections/image_browser_section.py`): mark the widget `_autoform_expanding = True` and give it an `Expanding/Expanding` size policy, mirroring the `waterfall`/`phasor`/`path_list` sections — the panel loop now assigns it `setStretch(row, 1)` instead of the space-eating trailing stretch. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Light-path simulator: node view auto-fits on load, and Easy Mode no longer disappears when the spectra catalogue fails to load.** Two fixes in `chisurf/plugins/core/lightpath_simulator/gui/tool.py`. (1) Every graph-load path (`_on_load_graph`, `_on_load_from_mmfdb`, `load_graph_from_dict`, and the saved-graph restore in `_setup_default_path`) now calls a new `_fit_view()` that defers `NodeView.fit_all()` (zoom-to-fit-all-items, already used by the scene's right-click "Fit") to the next event-loop tick, replacing the hardcoded `centerOn(550, 200)` — so a freshly loaded scene is framed to the viewport instead of appearing off-screen or half-visible. (2) The "Easy Mode" tab was built only in `_on_probes_loaded`; the failure path `_on_probe_load_failed` (hit when MMFDB probe metadata can't load) never called `_build_easy_mode_tab`, so Easy Mode silently vanished — it now builds the tab too (with an empty probe list), so Easy Mode is always present. See [plugins/core-tools.md](/plugins/core-tools.md).
* **ChiMOL `cmd` gained PyMOL's core viewing/transform commands.** Added `turn`, `move`, `clip`, `rotate` and `translate` (RenderingMixin), the most-used PyMOL commands that were missing and that complement the new trackball navigation. Camera commands go cmd → `MolView` → `qtgl` renderer, all built on the rotation-matrix camera: `turn <x|y|z>, <angle>` left-multiplies the view rotation about a screen axis (Rodrigues); `move <x|y|z>, <dist>` pans (x/y) or dollies (z); `clip <near|far|slab|move>, <dist>` shifts the clip planes. `rotate <x|y|z>, <angle> [, sel]` and `translate [x,y,z] [, sel]` build a rigid transform and call `apply_transform_to_object` — verified `rotate x, 90` gives PyMOL's +90° ((0,1,0)→(0,0,1)) on the real `arr @ R^T` convention; fixed `MockViewer.apply_transform_to_object` (was `arr @ R`, missing the transpose) to match and gave it `turn`/`move`/`clip` stubs so the commands are testable headless. New `test_cmd_viewing.py` (**4**): registration, rotate/translate math, and camera-command dispatch. Chimol suite **89 passed**; lint net down (`cmd/rendering.py` 34→0 via cleanup, `qtgl`/`view` unchanged). More of PyMOL's surface (`create`, `set_name`, `dss`, `origin`, `count_atoms`, `save`, `run`) remains for follow-up. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **Burst-MLE fit page now displays the per-detector G factor / l1 / l2 — and a latent l1/l2-swap corruption is fixed.** The polarisation corrections (`g_factor`/`l1`/`l2`) are per-detector calibration constants on the detector definition and feed the fit's anisotropy, but the MLE fit page never showed them, and inside the embedded `burst_analysis` workflow the Detector Definition tab is hidden — so there was no way to see what the fit used. Added a read-only **G factor / l1 / l2** row to the fit page's "Shift · scatter · G/l1/l2" section (`wizard.py::_build_ui`), refreshed on every detector switch by `_sync_polarization_widgets` (and kept in step by `_set_polarization_display` on any programmatic change). While wiring this up, found the values were **corrupting on detector switches**: `_ensure_channel_state(det)` seeded the transient per-detector cache from `self.g_factor` (the *current* detector, not `det`), and `_apply_ui_state` then wrote g/l1/l2 back through setters that poke the definition's table cells by row/column — which mis-targeted and **swapped one detector's l1/l2 onto another** on repeated switches, so the fit silently ran with the wrong corrections. Fixes: seed `_ensure_channel_state` from the specific detector's own definition (`channel_definer.detectors[det]`), and stop `_apply_ui_state` restoring g/l1/l2 at all — they are owned by the definition and read live via the getters, so nothing needs restoring (the display fields are read-only; edit in the setup/Channels step). Verified headlessly: green/red display their own g/l1/l2 stably across repeated switches with the definition intact. Regression test `test_fit_page_displays_per_detector_g_factor_l1_l2`; MLE + workflow suites **39 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Games consolidated into the Games hub; Minesweeper playfield now configurable.** Moved the standalone `misc/pong_game`, `misc/tetris_game` and `misc/breakout_game` plugins under the `misc/games/` hub as hidden sub-plugins (`games/pong`, `games/tetris`, `games/breakout` — `menu_hidden: true`, display names under `Tools:Miscellaneous:Games:*`), so the navigation-panel Games hub is the single menu entry offering Number Quest, Minesweeper, Tetris, Pong and Breakout. The three arcade windows now accept a `parent` and no longer self-`show()`, so they embed cleanly in the hub shell (hub enlarged to fit their fixed 800×650 boards). Minesweeper gained playfield selection: core `DIFFICULTIES` presets (Beginner 9×9/10, Intermediate 16×16/40, Expert 16×30/99) plus a `MinesweeperGame.configure()` API and a GUI row with a preset combo and custom rows/cols/mines spin boxes (mine count clamped to cells−1; board rebuilt in a scroll area). Tests: minesweeper core configure/preset tests, hidden-manifest tests for pong/tetris/breakout; legacy macro-test paths updated. 17 games-plugin tests pass; headless hub screenshot verified.
* **MLE fit models are now populated from tttrlib's registry (fit23/24/25/26), mirroring the burst-search picker.** tttrlib already advertises its lifetime estimators in its machine-readable registry — a `fit` category (from `FitRegistry.cpp`, surfaced by `tttrlib.registry("fit")`) where each of fit23/24/25/26 carries a label, summary, description and a JSON-Schema `params_schema` giving every optimisable parameter's type, default, range, unit and `fixed_default`, plus a `setup` link to the shared `fit_setup`/`fit2x` construction inputs. Wired the **chisurf** side: added `FIT_MODEL = "fit"` / `FIT_SETUP = "fit_setup"` category constants to `chisurf/core/tttrlib_registry.py`; a thin `chisurf/core/fluorescence/mle/registry.py` (`fit_models()`/`is_available()`) view onto that category (mirrors `burst.tttrlib_search.algorithms()`); and a new registry-driven widget `chisurf/gui/widgets/fit_model_form.py` `FitModelForm` — a model chooser + an `AutoForm` generated from the schema via the existing `tttrlib_registry.entry_form_view_auto`, exactly as `BurstSearchForm` does for burst searches. Switching the model reflows the whole parameter set from the registry (fit23 → tau/gamma/r0/rho; fit24 → tau1/gamma/tau2/A2/offset; fit25 → the four-lifetime selector), so a fit model added in tttrlib appears in the GUI on upgrade with no chisurf change. Tests: `test/gui/test_fit_model_form.py` (5) pin that the combo offers every registry model, defaults come from the schema, switching reflows params, saved state restores, and the change signal fires; lint clean. Remaining: embed `FitModelForm` in the burst-MLE wizard's fit-parameter panel (whose fit execution currently hardcodes fit23's four parameters) so the user can actually pick fit24/25 there. See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **Delta-macro-time interval is now a real photon-stream pre-filter on the burst search (it had no effect before).** In `burst_selection`, the min/max dMT interval (`DeltaMacroTimeFilterSettings`) was AND-ed onto the burst-search *result* inside `apply_photon_filters` — but burst photons already have a small inter-photon gap, so the interval never removed any of them and the control did nothing (a documented-`xfail` "Unresolved" test captured exactly this: the bound "moves the wrong way / selects ~94%"). Reworked `api/selection.py` so the interval gates the photons the search *sees*: extracted the per-mode search dispatch into `_run_burst_search(tttr, …)`, added `_delta_macro_time_mask` and `_search_on_delta_filtered_stream`, which builds the reduced stream with **tttrlib** (`tttr.get_tttr_by_selection(idx.astype(np.int32))`), runs the search on it, and maps the detected photons back to the original index space. Semantics are **exclude-from-search-only**: a photon the interval drops can no longer seed or extend a burst, but a burst that spans it still counts it (bursts are found over the original photons, so the returned mask stays length = n photons and downstream `.bur`/summary are unchanged). Both the live GUI preview (`gui/client.py`) and the backend service (`backend/services.py`) go through `apply_photon_filters`, so the wizard's dMT controls now move the selection. When no search runs (`filter_active=False`) the interval is the selection directly, byte-identical to before; channels/micro-time pre-filters are unchanged. Verified on real BH SPC-130: with a count-rate search the selected fraction now tracks `dT_max` (0.042 @ 0.02 ms → 0.076 @ 0.05 → 0.086 @ 0.15) where before it was a fixed no-op. Removed the now-passing stale `xfail`; added `test_delta_macro_time_prefilters_the_burst_search` + `_keeps_bursts_in_original_index_space`; burst_selection suite **178 pass**. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Burst-MLE auto IRF: Gaussian is now *fitted* to the extracted prompt, with a Gaussian/Skewed/Experimental switch.** The one-click auto-IRF built a Gaussian by crude peak + half-max-width placement — deterministic, never fitted — and there was no way to fall back to the measured prompt. Reworked `chisurf/core/fluorescence/burst/irf_bg.py`: `irf_model="gaussian"` now **least-squares fits** a Gaussian to the baseline-subtracted non-burst prompt over a window around the peak (Poisson counting weights, `scipy.optimize.curve_fit`, analytic peak/FWHM pulse as fallback). Because a Gaussian cannot follow the slow *fluorescent* tail the non-burst prompt rides on, the fit locks onto the sharp scatter peak and sheds that tail — the artifact that biased recovered lifetimes ~2× short — while still tracking the measured prompt (verified on real BH SPC-130: far-past-peak IRF mass drops from 0.39 raw to 0.043 fitted). Added `irf_model="skewed"` (a skew-normal `erf`-tail fit for an asymmetric detector response) and `"experimental"`/`"raw"` (the measured prompt); the symmetric Gaussian formerly inlined here now goes through the shared `tcspc.irf.synthetic_irf`. New `irf_shape` arg controls skewness. UI: the Burst-MLE fit page gained an **IRF-model combo** (`comboBox_irf_model`: Gaussian (fitted) / Skewed Gaussian / Experimental) next to the ✨ Auto IRF/BG button; changing it re-runs the extraction, and `auto_extract_irf_bg` passes the choice through. Test: `test_mle_irf_model_gaussian_fit_suppresses_fluorescent_tail` in `test/plugins/burst/test_irf_background.py` pins that the fitted Gaussian carries <½ the far-tail mass of the experimental prompt and that the three modes differ (suite **7 pass**), lint clean. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **ChiMOL navigation now matches PyMOL: virtual trackball + PyMOL button mapping.** The camera was a turntable (fixed azimuth/elevation about world Z/X), so it couldn't roll or rotate about an arbitrary screen axis and felt unlike PyMOL. Extracted PyMOL's `SceneMouse.cpp` behaviour via subagent and reimplemented in `qtgl.py`: the camera orientation is now a 3×3 world→camera rotation matrix; **left-drag is a virtual trackball** (project prev/cur cursor onto a sphere of radius `0.45·min(W,H)`, rotate about `cross(n_prev,n_cur)` with `mouse_scale`=1.3 and roll-damping `1/(1+|axis.z|)`, left-multiply the rotation, SVD re-orthonormalise to prevent drift); **middle-drag pans** (unchanged, already the correct `depth·2tan(fov/2)/H` pixel→world scale); **right-drag dollies** (`Δ = dy/400·max(5,distance)`) while a right-*click* still opens the context menu. Camera right/up/forward now come straight from the rotation rows, and the 18-float view state stores the rotation in slots 0–8 (PyMOL's convention) with a legacy fallback that reads the old elevation/azimuth in slots 10–11; `raytracer._camera_from_view_state` decodes the same, so the offscreen raytrace and the GL view stay consistent (verified: GL camera origin/forward derivation matches the raytracer's exactly, and a default-view raytrace of 148L renders upright). `reset_view(distance,elevation,azimuth)` keeps its signature (builds the matrix internally) so callers are unchanged. Trackball math unit-checked (orthonormal, ~0.4°/px, no drift over 200 drags); chimol suite **85 passed**; no new lint (`qtgl.py` 56→56, `raytracer.py` 23→23). The GL feel (rotation direction) can't be verified headlessly; it follows PyMOL's formula. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **Fluorophore staging DB: spectra quality assessed and duplicate probes merged (2288 → 2141 live probes).** Added `chisurf/plugins/spectra_downloader/download/dedupe_spectra.py`, a headless curation utility for the scraper staging `spectra.db`. It (1) **assesses spectra quality** — decodes every live `spectra` BLOB (float64) and flags empty, length-mismatched, non-monotonic, non-finite, all-zero or negative-intensity curves — and (2) **merges duplicate probes**, reusing MMFDB's exact grouping model (`mmfdb.admin.backend.duplicate_grouping.group_duplicates`) and the same union-merge as `fluorophores.merge_probes` (copy each duplicate's missing spectra/optical-properties into the richest probe via `INSERT OR IGNORE` on the `UNIQUE` constraints, keep the longest name, soft-delete the duplicates). Because MMFDB compares probes only *within* a category, a `--cross-category` pass merges exact-normalized-name matches across categories too (the same fluorophore ingested as an fpbase `protein` and a chroma `organic_dye`, carrying complementary absorption/excitation spectra), guarded to **skip optical filters** (any `transmission` spectrum or `filter` category) so filter sets named after a dye are never fused into the dye. It also (3) **highlights potential duplicates** with a self-contained **Bayesian classifier** (`duplicate_probability` — a naive-Bayes posterior over normalized-name similarity + absorption/emission maxima, with same-source curated pairs down-weighted): `--candidates` reports borderline pairs the automatic merge does *not* touch, ranked by posterior, split into a `strong` and a human-review band (report-only, never writes). Runs read-only by default; `--apply` makes a timestamped, non-overwriting `.backup` first. Assessment on the live DB: 2868 spectra, 121 flagged (114 negative-intensity — almost all benign `transmission` filter-baseline noise — and 7 non-monotonic wavelengths). Applied: 116 within-category + 31 cross-category duplicate probes merged (147 total, all fpbase/atto kept over chroma), e.g. EGFP now carries the union `absorption,emission,excitation`. Post-merge `PRAGMA integrity_check` = ok, `foreign_key_check` empty, 0 orphaned live spectra, no duplicate names remain, and the Bayesian classifier flags **0** remaining pairs above the 0.5 review threshold (the top residual near-name-twins — mono/di/tri-carbocyanines, Star 635 vs 635p, rsEGFP vs rsEGFP2 — are correctly scored ~0.09 because their maxima disagree); three incremental backups retained. The `spectra.db` is gitignored (staging data, not committed); new `test_dedupe_spectra.py` (**4**) covers the quality flags, the union-merge/soft-delete semantics and the Bayesian classifier ranking on an in-memory DB. See [plugins/sample-spectra.md](/plugins/sample-spectra.md).
* **The fit2x batch kernel is now general (fit23/24/25/26), and `Fit2x.fit_many` follows.** The batch fit (`fit_matrix` — fit a whole matrix of decays in one GIL-released C++ call, the path that makes threaded fitting scale) existed **only** for `DecayFit23`; fit24/25/26 could only be fit one row at a time from Python. tttrlib (`~/dev/tttrlib`, uncommitted for the author): added a model-agnostic engine `DecayFit::fit_matrix_generic(fitfn, …)` in `DecayFit.cpp`/`.h` — it threads over row-chunks (shared `decay_batch_threads` heuristic), gives each thread a private `DecayFitData`, copies the caller's full packed start vector per row, calls the model's scalar `fit`, and writes `[params…, 2I*]`; thin `DecayFit24/25/26::fit_matrix` wrappers pass their `fit` fn + parameter count (5/5/1). SWIG bindings (`DecayFit24/25/26.i`) expose them with the same numpy typemaps + GIL-release exception as fit23. Verified **bit-exact** parity: batch `fit_matrix` == per-row scalar `fit` (max|Δ| = 0.0) for fit24/25/26 (`test/python/decayfit/test_batch_fit_matrix.py`, 3 tests); cross-language + DecayFit23 suites unchanged (13). chisurf: `Fit2x.fit_many` (`core/fluorescence/mle/fit2x.py`) dropped its `NotImplementedError` fit23-only guard — it now dispatches per model, packing the free params + estimator-specific input slots (fit25's r0) + the BIFL-scatter flag into the full start vector via a small `_BATCH_LAYOUT` table, and returns `[free params…, 2I*]`. Test `test_fit_many_is_general_and_matches_scalar_fits` (fit2x suite **9 pass**). NB the imaging/burst callers still use fit23; this generalises the engine so fit24/25 batch fits are available. Registry-driven fit-type population (advertise the estimators + parameter schemas from tttrlib, like the burst-search registry) is the next piece.
* **ChiMOL cartoon no longer twists — PyMOL-style ribbon orientation.** The cartoon rendered helices as jagged "twisted tape" because the ribbon orientation used the raw per-residue carbonyl direction (`C - O`), which is noisy and, in a helix, spins around the axis with each residue. Rebuilt the orientation pipeline against PyMOL's `RepCartoon.cpp` (extracted via subagent): (1) `_build_trace_ups` now uses the **peptide-plane normal** `normalize((N−C)×(N−O))` (PyMOL's `RepCartoonGeneratePASS1`) instead of `C−O`; (2) a new `_refine_orientations` applies PyMOL's three anti-twist passes before spline sampling — **round helices** (helix orientation = `normalize(axis × tangent)` with `axis` a running CA-difference sum, so the oval circles a smooth axis), **flat sheets** (4-cycle 3-point box average of orientations across each β-strand so it shares one up-vector), and **refine-normals** (force ⊥ tangent, propagate sign forward to kill 180° flips). Verified by offscreen raytraced renders of 148L (helices go from broken twisted tape to smooth spiralling ribbons) and 1rtd (proper mixed α/β). Cross-section dimensions already matched PyMOL (oval 0.25×1.35, rect 0.4×1.4, loop r=0.2). `test_geometry_vectorized` + fallback-render suites **12 passed**; `cartoon.py` lint unchanged (43→43). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **F-test tool rebuilt on AutoForm — the 500-line hand-built widget compressed to a declarative model + view.** `chisurf/plugins/core/f_test` was a legacy `f_calculator.py`: a `FTestWidget(QWidget)` that hand-assembled two `QGridLayout` sections, wired eight spin boxes with per-widget slots, exposed ~25 boilerplate property getter/setters (`n1`/`n2`/`chi2_1`/…), and built three separate `>`-button menus to load values from open fits. Replaced with (1) a tiny `_FTestModel` holding the ten fields (`chi2_1`, `n1`, `chi2_2`, `n2`, `conf_level`; `chi2_min`, `npars`, `dof`, `conf_level_2`, `chi2_max`) plus three `recompute_*` methods carrying the exact legacy F-distribution formulas (`F.cdf`/`F.isf` and `chi2_max`), (2) `gui/ftest.view.json` — two AutoForm panels binding those fields (χ² max `read_only`) with short labels + tooltips and a `?` help modal, and (3) `FTestTool(QMainWindow)` that binds each `ValueWidget.editor` to a single `_on_field_edited(attr)` dispatcher (edited-attr → which recompute) and collapses the three fit-loader menus into one compact **📊 From fit ▾** toolbar button (one submenu per open fit → model 1 / model 2 / χ²-min). Added a `manifest.json` (id `f_test`, v2.0.0, categories Tools/Statistics) so the tool is manifest-discovered like the other calculators, registered it as a sixth entry in the calculators hub, and deleted `f_calculator.py`. `FTestWidget` is kept as an alias of `FTestTool`; `__init__`/`__main__` repointed. Verified headlessly: view spec binds exactly the ten fields, the recompute methods reproduce the reference numbers (χ²max = 1.464759 matches the documented example), the tool builds and a confidence edit recomputes the χ²(2) threshold; new `f_test/test/test_widgets.py` (**3**) + hub-registry suite pass, all files lint-clean. See [plugins/calculator.md](/plugins/calculator.md).
* **κ²-distribution calculator moved under the calculators group and embedded in the Calculators hub.** `chisurf/plugins/kappa2_dist` → `chisurf/plugins/calculator/kappa2_dist` (`git mv`), so the FRET orientation-factor calculator lives beside the FRET/homoFRET, phasor and FCS calculators rather than at the plugin root. All dotted paths `chisurf.plugins.kappa2_dist` → `chisurf.plugins.calculator.kappa2_dist` were rewritten across the manifest entrypoints/rpc, the plugin's own back-compat shims (`k2dfun`/`k2dgui`), and every external importer (`structure_tools`, `kappa2_helpers`, four GUI/fitting tests). Registered a new `kappa2_dist` `CalculatorEntry` in `calculator/hub/core/registry.py` (icon 🎯) so the hub now embeds it as the fifth calculator; `Kappa2Dist(kappa2=0.667)` already constructs with no required args, so it is hub-embeddable as-is. Also removed a core→plugin coupling: `chisurf/core/models/anisotropy_to_kappa.py` now imports `kappasq`/`s2delta` directly from `chisurf.core.fluorescence.anisotropy.kappa2` (the shim's own source) instead of via the plugin. Verified: all five hub widget paths resolve, the plugin/shim/model import clean, and kappa2 + hub-registry + anisotropy-to-kappa suites **5 pass**. See [plugins/calculator.md](/plugins/calculator.md).
* **Embedded Burst-MLE wizard no longer crashes ("wrapped C/C++ object of type MLELifetimeAnalysisWizard has been deleted") when its optimise buttons are clicked.** The burst-analysis workflow embeds the MLE panel by `takeCentralWidget()`-ing the `MLELifetimeAnalysisWizard` (a QMainWindow) and hosting only that central widget — the wizard object is kept alive as the workflow's `"mle"` logic panel (macOS swallows clicks on an embedded QMainWindow, so only its content is embedded). But the factory left the *wizard* parented to the workflow window while `central` — which holds every fit button, each wired to a `wizard` slot — lived in the navigation stack's branch: two branches, two lifetimes. A window/child cleanup pass could destroy the zombie QMainWindow while `central` and its buttons survived, so a later `Start hyperparameter optimization` / `⚡ Auto-optimize` click fired a slot on the deleted C++ object (the crash the user hit; the `except` then double-faulted trying to `QMessageBox.critical(self, …)` on the same dead wizard). Root-cause fix in `_burst_mle` (`chisurf/plugins/burst/burst_analysis/gui/tool.py`): after taking the central widget, `wizard.setParent(central, Qt.Widget)` + `wizard.hide()` — the wizard is now a **hidden child of the widget that is actually embedded**, so it shares exactly one lifetime with its own content (can neither outlive nor predecease it, and no button can outlive it). It must be reparented as `Qt.Widget`, **not** `Qt.Window`: a first cut used `Qt.Window`, which stays a top-level widget that macOS actually shows — an empty little traffic-light window floating over the panel, whose close deletes the wizard and re-triggers the crash. A hidden non-window child never renders (no QMainWindow-as-child click-swallow) and is not a closeable window. Verified headlessly: the wizard is a descendant of `central`, is not a stray workflow child, `isWindow()`/`isVisible()` are False and it is absent from `topLevelWidgets()`, and `sip.delete(central)` takes the wizard and its buttons with it. Regression test `test_mle_wizard_shares_lifetime_with_embedded_widget` in `burst_analysis/tests/test_workflow.py` pins these invariants; burst_analysis workflow (**11**) + MLE dock-shell/end-to-end suites pass. See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Deduplicated the two imaging MLE plugins onto a shared base (`mle_common`).** `img_pixel_mle` and `sm_image_mle` were near-identical copies around the shared `Fit2x` fit; extracted the common scaffolding into `chisurf/plugins/microscopy/mle_common/`: `AutoFormMleTool` (the AutoForm host + background-run-thread shell + `apply_setup_settings`/`apply_pipeline_context`/`apply_calibration` forwarders; each plugin's `gui/tool.py` shrank to a ~15-line subclass — the molecule tool overrides a `handle_event` hook for its segmentation-preview/table-export events); `contract.py` (`service_success`/`service_error` envelopes, `build_contract_descriptor`, `register_analyze_and_contract` — each plugin's `api/contract.py` keeps only its id/version/method names); and `view_model.py` (a module-level `scalar()` settings-descriptor factory and an `MleObserverMixin` observer hook, both previously copied verbatim into each view-model). These join the earlier shared `parse_detector_setup` and `interpolate_shift`. Deliberately **not** folded into `imaging_common.ImagingMapTool`: that base is shaped for single-file per-pixel *map* tools (`compute()`/`needs_recompute`/HDF5/ndxplorer/single-file drops), whereas the MLE tools take a file *list*, run a per-observation loop, and carry calibration — a different shape. Verified both tools construct via the base, view-models inherit the mixin, `scalar` bindings and observer/notify work; imaging + fit2x suites **40 pass**, all changed files lint-clean. Remaining per-plugin: the genuinely different cores (pixel cube + threaded `fit_matrix` vs molecule watershed segmentation + per-molecule loop) and result views (τ-map vs molecule browser). See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **ChiMOL is now entirely scipy-free (zero scipy/scikit-image imports) — and faster for it.** Completed the dependency migration by removing the last `scipy.spatial.cKDTree` and `scipy.ndimage.distance_transform_edt` uses. New shared numba cell list `geometry/neighbors.py` (cell = query radius, so all in-range points sit in the query cell + 26 neighbours) provides `shade_from_atoms` (Gaussian-weighted colour + density-gradient normal per surface/metaball vertex), `count_within_radius` (surface-exposed-atom mask) and `within_distance_mask` (`sele_parser` distance selection). Each is **bit-exact** vs a brute-force reference (counts array-equal, mask array-equal, shading ≤3e-16). The SES distance transform became a numba **separable exact EDT** (`surface._distance_transform_edt`, Felzenszwalb-Huttenlocher) — **max|diff| 0.00** vs `scipy.ndimage.distance_transform_edt`. Bonus: the cell-list colouring is **faster than the KD-tree k-nearest** it replaced (metaball build **806 → 391 ms**, surface **427 → 157 ms** on 1rtd) — a radius query fits this dense, local access pattern better than a tree. Surface-colour parity vs the old scipy path is exact (0.0000). New `test_neighbors.py` (**4**, scipy-free) checks all three kernels + the EDT against brute force; chimol suite **81 passed**; no new lint (`view.py` 44→44, `surface.py` 15→15, `sele_parser.py` 84→81). `grep 'from scipy\|from skimage' chisurf/plugins/chimol` is now empty. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **ChiMOL surface stack is now scipy/scikit-image-free — only IMP + NumPy/numba.** Per the directive that scipy is not a dependency and "only IMP allowed", removed both external libs from the surface/AV path. (1) `scipy.ndimage.binary_dilation`/`gaussian_filter` in `_generate_surface_mesh_from_points` → NumPy `_binary_dilate_6` (6 shifted ORs; **array-equal** to scipy's default element) and `_gaussian_blur_3d` (separable 1-D conv; matches `gaussian_filter` to **4e-16**). (2) `skimage.measure.marching_cubes` (used by AV, metaball and gaussian/EDT surface) → a self-contained numba marching cubes: `geometry/marching_cubes.py` (Lorensen–Cline kernel, two-pass count-then-fill with exact allocation, per-grid-edge vertex **welding**, trilinear gradient normals) + the canonical `geometry/_mc_tri_table.py`. Output is **bit-identical to skimage** — verified 3612 verts / 7220 tris vs skimage's 3612 / 7220 on a real AV, bbox diff 0, outward unit normals, matching winding (a drop-in: surface.py's existing `-norms`/`[0,2,1]` post-processing is unchanged) — and **4–9 ms** for an AV-sized grid, ≈skimage. **IMP's own isosurface** (`IMP.display.IsosurfaceGeometry`, CGAL) was implemented and rejected: geometrically correct (sphere r=14.99) but **~1.7 s** for a 60³ grid (CGAL Delaunay refinement, not fast MC), needing an `em.DensityMap` numpy round-trip because grids have no bulk setter, and `em.get_grid` throws on negative origins. Metaball/surface build times unchanged (MC was never their bottleneck — AO/gaussian accumulation is); metaball vertex count stays 64k (welding prevents the 3× loose-vertex blow-up). Tests: new `test_marching_cubes.py` (**5**, skimage-free) validates the triangle table against the edge table across all **256** cases, all non-trivial cases producing a surface, sphere geometry + outward normals, spacing scaling, and empty-when-out-of-range; chimol suite **81 passed**. Lint clean (`surface.py` 15→15, new files pass). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **Imaging MLE: G-factor/l1/l2 now flow from the shared setup into the fit, plus a first round of pixel↔molecule deduplication.** The shared detector setup already carries per-detector `g_factor`/`l1`/`l2` (the `DetectorWizardPage` rows), but both imaging tools' `apply_setup_settings` read only `chs`/`mtr` and dropped the polarisation corrections, so the pixel- and molecule-wise fits silently ran at `g=1, l1=l2=0`; the molecule tool additionally *overwrote* any G-factor with an IRF-tail estimate. Fixes: new shared `chisurf/core/fluorescence/mle/setup.py` `parse_detector_setup()` returns channels (+ even/odd ∥/⊥ split), fit window **and** g/l1/l2 from one parser both view-models now call; `img_pixel_mle` gained `g_factor`/`l1`/`l2` on its transport `PixelMleSettings` and threads them through the backend + GUI into the core fit; `sm_image_mle` gained an `auto_g_factor` flag so a setup-supplied G-factor is authoritative and the IRF-tail `compute_g_factor` is only a fallback. Dedup: the sub-bin IRF `interpolate_shift` was copied three times (burst `interpolate.py`, `img_pixel_mle/api/pixel_mle.py`, `sm_image_mle/core/molecule_mle.py`); consolidated into `chisurf/core/fluorescence/mle/irf.py` (the robust burst variant, handling `None`/empty/`n<2`), with all three now importing it — verified they resolve to one function object. Tests: `test_parse_detector_setup_*` + `test_settings_area_normalises_the_background` in `test/fluorescence/test_mle_fit2x.py`, and `test_apply_setup_settings_carries_polarisation_corrections` in each imaging plugin's widget suite (**40 pass** across fit2x + both imaging suites + burst sim). Further dedup (a shared `AutoFormMleTool`/`MleViewModelBase` base and shared api/CLI boilerplate — the bulk of the remaining ~90% overlap in `gui/tool.py` and `api/contract.py`) is scoped as a follow-up; the anisotropy-calibration plugin (g/l1/l2 + IRF/scatter shift from fast-rotating-dye reference data, shown only for polarisation-flagged setups) follows after. See [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md).
* **MLE-lifetime input contract centralised and transferred from burst to imaging; new OKF concept.** The burst-MLE fixes are properties of the *shared* tttrlib `Fit23` engine, so they apply equally to the pixel-wise (`img_pixel_mle`) and molecule-wise (`sm_image_mle`) imaging fits, which reach it through `chisurf/core/fluorescence/mle/`. Moved the background area-normalisation into `Fit2xSettings.__post_init__` so **every** facade consumer (burst batch run + both imaging plugins) gets a true 0..1 `gamma` in one place, rather than each caller normalising ad hoc (the burst interactive-preview path, which builds `Fit23` directly, still normalises in `create_fit_instance`). Confirmed the other two lessons were already honoured in imaging: `dt` and `period` are derived from the TTTR header (`img_pixel_mle/backend/services.py`, `gui/view_model.py`, same `n_chan·micro_res·1e9` formula the burst wizard now uses), and the tttrlib soft-bound fix propagates automatically because the batch kernel `DecayFit23::fit_matrix` calls the scalar `fit()` per row. Documented the whole contract as a durable concept, [subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md](/subsystems/mle-lifetime-fitting.md) (dt/period units, background-as-fraction, soft bounds vs hard clamp, IRF-quality caveat, defence-in-depth), linked from the subsystems index. tttrlib side (`~/dev/tttrlib`, uncommitted for the author): `include/i_lbfgs.h` gained optional per-parameter **soft bounds** (`set_bounds`, smooth exterior penalty, zero inside the box) and `DecayFit23::fit` bounds `gamma` to `[0, 0.999]` for the free pass; regression `test_free_gamma_uses_soft_bounds_and_does_not_stall_at_the_cap` plus unchanged cross-language references. Test: `test_settings_area_normalises_the_background` in `test/fluorescence/test_mle_fit2x.py` (7 pass); imaging core suites green.
* **Burst-MLE wizard: one-click Auto IRF/BG now yields a physical lifetime instead of a railed fit.** Beyond the already-logged header `dt`/`period` fix and the decay legend, the burst-MLE plugin gained the robustness machinery that makes the auto-extracted-IRF path usable. `chisurf/core/fluorescence/burst/irf_bg.py` grew an `irf_model` arg: the default `"gaussian"` replaces the baseline-subtracted non-burst histogram (whose prompt rides a fluorescence tail that biased the recovered lifetime ~2× short — ~2.2 ns read as ~1.1 ns on real BH smFRET) with a tail-free Gaussian at the prompt's measured peak/core-FWHM; `"raw"` keeps the old behaviour. `wizard.py`: the fit's background is now area-normalised so Fit23's `bg·gamma` makes gamma a true 0..1 fraction (a raw histogram made gamma an enormous multiplier → free-gamma diverged to ≈1); robust "quick lifetime" defaults leave only `tau` free (gamma/rho/r0 fixed at physical nominals — unidentifiable against a scatter-shaped background); `_auto_select_binning`/`_irf_fwhm_channels` coarsen the sparse micro-time axis to well-populated bins (a MLE on ~1 count/bin rails tau to the period), `_auto_select_fit_range` trims to the filled region, `_fit_diverged` flags a gamma-railed result (2I* < 0 or runaway amplitude) and the plot clips the displayed model so it stays readable. UI: the fit-parameter groups became collapsible boxes and an `✨ Auto IRF/BG` + `IRF & Background…` action row was added, with a "measured IRF gives better lifetimes" hint. Also a guard against the transient empty-detector `detectorsChanged`. Tests: new headless `test_mle_fit_simulation.py` (5) pins the Fit23 machinery against tttrlib's own forward model at known lifetimes, and `test_mle_gui_end_to_end.py` gained auto-extract coverage (coarsening, window, per-colour physical lifetime, model-free cross-check); **27 pass** (5 + 22). See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **CI now smoke-tests that each built installer actually starts.** The `Build and Release` workflow (`.github/workflows/pixi-ci.yml`) previously produced the macOS DMG/`.app`, the Linux AppImage and the Windows Inno Setup `.exe` but never launched them — a bundle that assembled fine yet crashed on startup (missing lib, broken shebang, bad relocation) shipped green. Added a headless self-test to `chisurf/gui/__init__.py`: when `CHISURF_SMOKE_TEST` is truthy, `get_app()` skips the interactive MMFDB login (which would otherwise block on the login dialog forever), lets the main window paint, then after `CHISURF_SMOKE_DELAY_MS` (default 5000 ms) optionally writes a `CHISURF_SMOKE_SENTINEL` file and quits so the process exits 0 (helpers `_smoke_test_enabled`/`_schedule_smoke_exit`). Wired one launch step per runner OS after the installer build: **macOS** launches the `.app` bundle with `open -W` and confirms via the sentinel (macOS `open` does not propagate the child exit code); **Linux** runs the AppImage under `xvfb-run` and checks the exit code; **Windows** silently installs the `.exe` (`/VERYSILENT /DIR=…`) then runs the installed `python.exe -m chisurf`, checking both sentinel and exit code. Each step has a watchdog (pkill / `timeout` / `WaitForExit`) so a hung launch fails the job fast rather than hitting the job timeout. Verified locally on macOS: `CHISURF_SMOKE_TEST=1 … python -m chisurf` exits 0 and writes the sentinel. See [workflows/build-and-env.md](/workflows/build-and-env.md).
* **ChiMOL AVs now render as a transparent surface envelope instead of a transparent point cloud.** The FRET `AVViewer3D.show_av` drew each accessible volume via `add_point_overlay` — a dense cloud (tens of thousands of points) as transparent sphere sprites. That is fragment-overdraw bound: every sprite runs the full lighting fragment shader and they stack many-deep under alpha blending, so rotation crawls even though the CPU build is cheap. Per the user's direction ("determine the surface and render that"), `show_av` now calls `add_surface_overlay` → `_generate_surface_mesh_from_points` (voxelise → binary-dilate → Gaussian-smooth → marching cubes), producing **one transparent closed mesh**: a 60k-point AV becomes **~3.6k verts / ~7.2k triangles**, `render_mode="transparent"`, ~22× fewer primitives and vastly less overdraw for the same volumetric read. Meshing (~9 ms for 60k points) is all C (`scipy.ndimage` + marching cubes); the overlay auto-falls-back to points only when meshing fails (too few points). Separately hardened `add_point_overlay` (still used for genuine point data) to cap/subsample above `overlay.max_points` (new config, default 30k) with a uniform random draw, keeping alpha untouched so the cloud stays see-through. Tests: new `fret/test/test_av_viewer_3d.py` (2) — `show_av` yields a transparent mesh with far fewer triangles than input points, and the point path caps dense clouds; chimol overlay suite **7 passed**. No new lint (`view.py` 44→44, `config.py` 2→2, `av_viewer_3d.py` 10→10). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **ChiMOL Gaussian surface ~5× faster; clarified the AV rendering path.** Profiling `_update_surface` on 1rtd showed 979 ms in `_build_surface_mesh_scene` — a per-vertex/per-atom Python double loop (754k `math.exp`) colouring vertices and computing analytical normals from scipy-KD-tree `query_ball_point` neighbours. Replaced it with the vectorised fixed-k approach the **metaball** path already used (one `tree.query(verts, k=32)`, then array-wise Gaussian weights → weighted colour + gradient normals). **Bit-exact vs the reference loop** on real structures (verified 0.0000 max colour diff on 148l — the 32 nearest capture every atom inside the 2.5σ cutoff; distant atoms weigh ≈0). Surface build **2.1 s → 0.41 s (~5×)**. Prompted by the note that **AVs are rendered as surfaces**: traced the AV paths — `av_viewer_3d.show_av` uses `add_point_overlay` (transparent point cloud) and `fps_json_editor` uses `add_surface_overlay` → `_generate_surface_mesh_from_points` (voxel + `scipy.ndimage` + marching cubes, **~9 ms for 60k points — already fast**). Neither hits `_build_surface_mesh_scene`; the real AV-workflow cost is that every AV show/update triggers a full `_update_view` structure rebuild (cartoon/atoms/surface), which the session's builder speedups cut ~8×. Removed the now-unused `math` import (`view.py` lint 44→44). Chimol suite **76 passed**. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **ChiMOL atoms (balls) ~5× faster: the sphere glyph was 16×32 = 512 verts/atom.** Profiling `_update_atoms` on 1rtd (17,784 atoms) showed 183 ms, all in `_build_balls_mesh` — it merges one sphere per atom, and the shared glyph was a 16×32 UV sphere (**512 vertices / 960 faces per atom → ~9 M vertices** for the model), far more than small on-screen balls need, inflating both the CPU merge (`np.repeat` of normals/colours) and every GPU frame. Made the sphere tessellation config-tunable (`balls.sphere_lat`/`sphere_lon`, default **10×16 = 160 verts**) through a new `MolView._balls_sphere_segments()`, applied at both the fallback and structured ball paths, and parameterised `_build_sphere_mesh(radius, lat, lon)` (gaussian/selection glyphs keep the smooth default). Also swapped the merge's `np.repeat` for broadcasting. Atom build **183 → 39 ms (~4.7×)** and the per-frame triangle count dropped ~3×, which is what the user actually feels while rotating. Combined with the earlier geometry+AO work, a full cartoon+atoms+sticks build for this model is now **~0.33 s (was 2.7 s, ~8×)**. Updated the fallback-render test to derive verts-per-atom from the coarse resolution; chimol suite **76 passed**; no new lint (`primitives.py` 13→13, `view.py` 44→44, `config.py` 2→2). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **ChiMOL scene-build ~7× faster by vectorising the hot cartoon/stick geometry kernels.** Profiling a full cartoon+atoms+sticks build for a ~18k-atom model (1rtd) put the cost at **2.7 s**, all in per-element Python loops whose dominant single cost was `numpy.cross` (208k calls, 8.6 s cumulative across the run — its `moveaxis`/axis-normalisation overhead swamps the actual arithmetic when called per point). Confirmed first that camera rotation does **not** rebuild the scene (`qtgl.py` mouse handlers only bump azimuth/elevation and call `update()`), so the win is in the geometry kernels, not the paint loop. Rewrote four builders from loops to batched NumPy, each pinned by a parity test against a reference loop (`test_geometry_vectorized.py`, **5** tests, exact to ≤1e-9/1e-5): `primitives._build_stick_mesh` — a new batched `_rotations_from_z` (Rodrigues over all bond directions) + einsum instead of a per-bond loop (**9.8×**, 937→95 ms for ~18k bonds); `cartoon._extrude_shape` — broadcast the cross-section over all path points and the quad-index pattern over all rings (**21×**); `cartoon._sample_path` — vectorised Catmull–Rom via the Hermite basis (**113×**); `cartoon._build_frames` — batched cross products with only the genuinely-sequential sign-continuity flip left as a scalar loop (**29×**). Added `cartoon._batch_cross` (explicit component form) to replace `numpy.cross` in the hot paths. Net full-build **2.7 s → 0.39 s (~7×)**; cartoon 1302→220 ms, sticks 1444→152 ms. Marching-cubes metaball/surface remain heavy but are opt-in and untouched. Chimol suite **74 passed** (69 + 5 new); no new lint (`primitives.py` 13→13, `cartoon.py` 44→43). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **ChiMOL metaball/surface ~7× faster: the cost was ambient occlusion, not marching cubes.** Profiling `_update_metaballs` on 1rtd showed **18.4 s of 21.7 s in `_estimate_ambient_occlusion`** (marching cubes was 0.2 s) — its default path was a numba **O(n²)** double loop over the *mesh vertices* (tens of thousands from marching cubes). Replaced it with an O(n) numba **cell list** (uniform grid, cell = radius; every point within the radius lies in the query cell or one of the 26 neighbours), **bit-identical** to the retained O(n²) reference (0.00 diff across sparse/dense clouds) and far faster: 80k pts **84 ms** (was ~18 s extrapolated), 131k pts 359 ms. Metaball build **5.7 s → 0.83 s**; AO is shared by balls/cartoon/surface/metaball so all gain. On the user's steer that **scipy is not a declared dependency but IMP is**, evaluated IMP first and rejected it: `IMP.algebra.NearestNeighbor3D.get_in_ball` is an *approximate* search that silently drops interior neighbours even at `epsilon=0` (verified undercounting vs brute force — numba matched exactly, IMP did not), and `IMP.core.GridClosePairsFinder` materialises every close pair (~10 M on a dense mesh → 13 s, slower than the O(n²) loop). scipy's `cKDTree` was correct and fast but is not a dependency, so the final choice is the numba cell list (numba already accelerates this file). Tests: `test_geometry_vectorized.py` gained cell-list↔O(n²) exact-parity + edge-case tests (**7 geometry tests total**); chimol suite **76 passed**; `ambient.py` lint unchanged (3→3). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **Burst-MLE free-gamma fit no longer diverges: background is area-normalised and tttrlib's optimiser gained soft bounds.** Freeing gamma (the scattered-light fraction) made the fit "go crazy" — the model amplitude blew up to ~1e9, 2I* went negative, and the plot exploded to ±1e6. Root cause (found by reproducing on the real BH SPC-130 data, not guessed): tttrlib's `Fit23` model adds background as `bg[i]·gamma`, i.e. gamma is the *fraction* of the model that is background and only means that if the background pattern has unit area — but the burst-MLE wizard passed the extracted background as raw photon counts (sum ≈ 35 000), so any gamma > 0 multiplied it by that factor and the model ran away. Fix has two parts. (1) chisurf: `MLELifetimeAnalysisWizard.create_fit_instance` now area-normalises the background before constructing the fit, so gamma is a true 0..1 fraction. This is done caller-side rather than in tttrlib's `DecayFit23::modelf` on purpose — that model is the cross-language Python/R/Java reference contract (`test_decayfit_cross_language`), so changing it would break parity; gamma weighting a caller-normalised background is correct and leaves the contract intact. (2) tttrlib (`~/dev/tttrlib`, separate repo, left uncommitted for the author): even with a normalised background the free-gamma optimiser used to walk gamma onto its hard 0.999 clamp and stall at a spurious "all background" corner, because the clamp makes the objective flat (zero gradient) beyond the bound. The header-only L-BFGS (`include/i_lbfgs.h`) gained optional per-parameter **soft bounds** — a smooth exterior penalty (`set_bounds(i, lo, hi)`) that is identically zero inside the box (true objective untouched, unconstrained path unchanged) but rises with a real gradient outside, pushing the iterate back — and `DecayFit23::fit` sets `[0, 0.999]` on gamma for the free-gamma pass. Result on the real data: gamma-free now converges to the same interior optimum (tau≈2.75 ns, gamma≈0.58, 2I*≈1.4) from every starting value instead of pinning at 0.999 with a negative 2I*. Defence in depth on the chisurf side: `plot_fit_result` detects a non-physical result (`_fit_diverged`: 2I* < 0, non-finite model, or model amplitude ≫ data) and clips the displayed model + shows a status warning so a pathological fit can never blow the plot up again. Tests: tttrlib `test_free_gamma_uses_soft_bounds_and_does_not_stall_at_the_cap` (reproducible interior gamma from 3 starts, recovers an injected 0.30 scatter fraction) — decayfit suite **13 pass**, cross-language references (fit23/24/25/26) unchanged.
* **Burst-MLE lifetime `dt`/`period` now come from the file header, so the reported lifetime is in real nanoseconds.** `Fit23` measures `tau` (and `rho`) in units of its `dt` and convolves over one `period`; both must be nanoseconds to match the reported lifetime. The Burst-MLE wizard was taking `dt` from `channel_definer.effective_micro_time_resolution` — **picoseconds** (it feeds the g-factor calculator as `effective_micro_time_resolution_ps`) — and `period` from `channel_definer.excitation_period`, which reads the macro-time field; **neither field is populated from the loaded file's header** (the header-derived excitation period computed in `update_burst_files` at ~line 2220 was assigned to a local and discarded), and both defaulted to `50`. So `Fit23` was handed `dt`/`period` that were defaulted and in mismatched units: the fit is scale-covariant in `dt`, so the model still fit the data *shape*, but the lifetime label was in arbitrary units — a decay visibly falling in ~1 ns was displayed as "5 ns" (the user's "the model in the plot doesn't correspond to the 5 ns parameter").
