Fix: added `MLELifetimeAnalysisWizard._header_time_ns()`, which derives `(dt_ns, period_ns)` from the current file's TTTR header — channel width `micro_time_resolution·1e9·binning`, and one excitation period as the full TAC range `number_of_micro_time_channels·micro_time_resolution·1e9` (binning-invariant, `n_binned·dt == period`). `dt_effective` and `excitation_period` now return the header values when a file is loaded and fall back to the page fields otherwise; the shared channel-definition widget (and its picosecond g-factor path) is untouched. Verified on the real BH SPC-130 data: header gives `dt=0.003296 ns`, `period=13.5 ns` vs the buggy `50/50`, and the same real decay/IRF fit reports `tau≈2000` at `dt=50` vs a physical value at the header `dt` (identical 2I*, confirming pure mislabeling). NB: the poor residuals on that data are a *separate* issue — the auto-extracted IRF is decay-shaped, which the plugin already warns about. Test: `test_fit_dt_and_period_come_from_the_file_header` in `test_mle_gui_end_to_end.py` exercises `_header_time_ns` on the real header across binnings (no Qt) — passes.
* **Burst-MLE decay plot now carries a legend.** The "All selections" decay panel in the Burst-MLE wizard (`chisurf/plugins/burst/burst_mle_analysis/wizard.py`) overlays four curves — data points, the green model, the red IRF and the blue background — but had no legend, so the colours were unlabelled. `setup_plots` now creates `self.combined_legend = self.combined_plot.addLegend(...)` once; `plot_fit_result` empties it (`legend.clear()`) alongside the existing `combined_plot.clear()` so the rows don't stack a fresh set every fit, and the data/model `plot()` calls gained `name='Data (VV|VH)'` / `name='Model (fit)'` (IRF and Background already passed `name=`). Test: `test_decay_plot_has_a_labelled_legend` in `test_mle_gui_end_to_end.py` asserts the four named rows exist and do not duplicate across a refit — MLE suite **24 pass**.
* **Dragging the delta-macro-time region now updates the min/max dMT fields.** In the photon-filter wizard the macro-time bounds are edited by two paths — the `min dMT` / `max dMT` fields of the generated filter-settings `AutoForm` and the horizontal `LinearRegionItem` on the "Delta macro-time" plot. A finished region drag (`onRegionUpdate` in `tttr_photon_filter_plots.py`) writes the new bounds straight into `FilterSettingsModel.settings.dt_min/dt_max`, which fires the model's `on_change`; but `on_change` only mirrored the values into the hidden Designer spin boxes and the region itself, never back into the `AutoForm`'s own field widgets (AutoForm binds widget→model on edit and does not observe programmatic model writes). So the numbers stayed frozen while the region and the analysis moved. Fix: `on_change` in `filter_settings_form.py` now calls a new `_sync_form_fields(page)` helper that invokes `AutoForm.sync_fields()` on `page._filter_settings_form_widget`; `sync_fields` re-reads every field with signals blocked, so the visible controls follow the model without a rebuild and without recursing through `on_change` (editing a field just re-writes its own value). Test: `test_region_drag_updates_the_dmt_fields` in `test_filter_settings_form.py` emulates a guarded region drag and asserts both the model and the `AutoForm` editors move — filter-settings suite **passes**. See [subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md](/subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md).
* **Trace Browser now accepts fractional / sub-millisecond bin widths.** The bin-width control (`window_ms_spin` in `chisurf/plugins/tttr/trace_browser/__init__.py`) was an integer `QSpinBox` clamped to `1–10000 ms`, so binnings below 1 ms were impossible even though the whole downstream path (core `trace.py`, the RPC client, the CLI `--window-ms`, and `tttrlib.get_intensity_trace`) already carries `window_ms` as a float. Swapped it for a `QDoubleSpinBox` (3 decimals, range `0.001–10000 ms`, `0.1` step, keyboard-tracking off so a re-plot fires only on commit). Fixed the three GUI-side sites that still truncated to `int` and would have collapsed distinct fractional bins onto the same value: `_trace_signature` (`'win_ms': int(...)` → `float(...)`, which otherwise made e.g. `0.25 ms` and `0.75 ms` share a cache entry), the CSV-export `window_ms` read, and the CSV filename tag (now `%g` with `.`→`p`, e.g. `_bin0p25ms.csv`); updated the `window_ms: int` annotations on the cache helpers to `float`. The two transfer targets (`intensity_trace.window_spin`, `tttr_time_windows.tws_spin`) were already `QDoubleSpinBox`, so hand-off is unaffected. Test: `test_trace_browser_supports_fractional_binning` in `test/test_widgets.py` pins the widget type/decimals/min and asserts `0.25`/`0.75 ms` produce distinct cache signatures — trace_browser widget suite **3 pass**. See [plugins/profiles/trace-browser.md](/plugins/profiles/trace-browser.md).
* **Unified the burst-search picker on a single `BurstSearchForm` widget.** The photon-filter wizard had its own inline registry picker (`tttr_photon_filter_tttrlib.py`) duplicating — and out-capability-ing — the standalone `chisurf/gui/widgets/burst_search_form.py`, which used `RpcMethodView` directly (no composite `coincident` nesting, no foldable groups) and was unused. Routed `BurstSearchForm` through `tttrlib_registry.entry_form_view_auto` (gaining composite nested-panel + grouping + deferred nested rebuild), gave it an optional `combo=` parameter so a host can hand it an existing selector, then made the wizard embed one `BurstSearchForm(combo=page.comboBox_burst_filter)` in the filter-settings right column and **deleted `tttr_photon_filter_tttrlib.py`**. `tttr_photon_filter_mode.install_filter_mode_visibility` shrank to wiring the widget in (retaining `page._tttrlib_algorithms`, `tttrlib_algorithm`/`tttrlib_parameters`, and a no-op `update_parameter_visibility` for back-compat); `used_filter` is now always `'tttrlib'`; project restore calls `burst_search_form.set_state(...)`. Tests: extended `test_burst_search_form.py` (composite/grouping), rewrote `test_tttrlib_wizard_modes.py` onto the external-combo seam, retargeted `test_filter_ui_elements.py`/`test_filter_settings_form.py` at `page.burst_search_form`. Single source of truth for "pick a burst search, edit its parameters." See [subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md](/subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md).
* **Migrated `fret_trajectory` (Structure2Transfer) off its hand-built `.ui` onto AutoForm — the last traj `.ui`.** The largest of the batch: a Qt-free `FretTrajectoryViewModel` owns a `CalculateTransfer` engine and proxies its parameters (donor/acceptor dipole atom-index pairs, `t_step`, `stride`, `forster_radius`, `tau0`, `dipoles`), loads the first-frame topology into a structured array that drives the atom pickers, and `calc(output_file=…)` runs the per-frame RDA/kappa2/FRET-rate computation over the trajectory (chunked `md.iterload`) writing a CSV — taking the path as an argument so it is Qt-free. Three custom sections (`fret_traj_io` picker, `fret_atom_pairs` hosting the four `PDBSelector` donor/acceptor widgets, `fret_run` Process button) plus built-in `value`/`toggle` settings and an `info` log are laid out from `structure2transfer.view.json`; `gui.py` is a thin `AutoForm` wrapper preserving the entrypoint (`gui:Structure2Transfer`), `name`, `@persist_plugin_state` and the public parameter properties. **Fixed two real bugs surfaced by the functional tests**: (1) `traj2fret.py` accessed `chisurf.core.fluorescence.anisotropy.kappa2` without importing the submodule (it is not auto-imported) → `AttributeError` on every dipole compute; added the explicit import. (2) The atom-pair section was marked `AUTOFORM_REFRESH`, so a log line emitted *during* processing triggered a form refresh that reloaded the structure and reset the donor/acceptor combos to atom 0 mid-compute — both donor atoms collapsing to one index gave a zero-length dipole and per-frame kappa2 "calculation error" (non-finite output); the section now reloads only on the `loaded` event, preserving the selection. Verified before/after with headless PNGs. New `test/`: Qt-free `test_view_model.py` (6, incl. a real end-to-end `calc()` writing a finite (n_frames, 6) transfer table) + `test_gui.py` (4, incl. a **functional qtbot interaction test** that loads the trajectory, sets donor `(0,1)`/acceptor `(2,3)` through the real `PDBSelector` section and clicks Process, asserting a finite transfer series was computed and written) — **10 pass**. **This completes the traj `.ui`→AutoForm migration (all eight single-tool widgets).** See [plugins/trajectory.md](/plugins/trajectory.md).
* **Migrated `potential_energy` (Potential-Energy calculator) off its hand-built `.ui` onto AutoForm.** The dynamic case: a Qt-free `PotentialEnergyViewModel` holds `trajectory_file`, `stride`, a core `Universe`, the added-potential table records, and a running log; `process(csv_path, progress_cb)` ports `onProcessTrajectory` faithfully (per-frame `xyz×10` → `update_dist` → `Universe.getEnergies` → CSV) but takes the path as an argument so it is Qt-free and testable. The irreducibly-dynamic Qt lives in two custom sections: `potential_energy_setup` (trajectory picker + potential-type combo + the **dynamic `potentialDict` parameter editor** rebuilt on combo change + weight + Add button) and `potential_energy_run` (Process button + progress + save dialog); the added-potentials `table` uses `activated_call` for double-click removal, and `stride`/log are built-in sections — all laid out from `calculate_potential.view.json`. Kept the entrypoint (`widget:PotentialEnergyWidget`), `name`, `@persist_plugin_state`, and the `stride`/`potential_number`/`potential_name`/`energy` properties (now delegating). Verified before/after with headless PNGs. New `test/`: Qt-free `test_view_model.py` (7, incl. a real end-to-end `process()` writing a per-frame energy CSV for a radius-of-gyration potential on a synthesized 3-residue ALA peptide) + `test_gui.py` (4, incl. a **functional qtbot interaction test** that selects a potential in the real combo and clicks the real **Add** button, asserting the `Universe`/table grew) — **11 pass**, lint/format clean. See [plugins/trajectory.md](/plugins/trajectory.md).

* **Migrated `traj_remove_clashes` off its hand-built `.ui` onto AutoForm.** A Qt-free `RemoveClashesViewModel` (trajectory path, mdtraj `atom_selection` expression, `stride`, `min_distance` + running log; `min_distance_nm()` applies the historic `/10.0`; `save_clash_free` streams chunked `mdtraj` frames, drops any frame with an atom pair closer than the threshold, and writes the survivors to a fresh HDF5) drives an `AutoForm` from `remove_clashes.view.json` — a `traj_remove_clashes_io` custom picker section (`💾 Save clash-free…`), a `value` text `atom_selection`, `value` int `stride`, `value` float `min_distance`, over an `info` log. Added `set_rotation_matrix`/`set_translation_vector` notify methods so the widget's programmatic setters re-sync the editor grid (the `"fields"` event). Fixed the `Trajecotry` typo. Fixed the `Trajecotry` label typo. See [plugins/trajectory.md](/plugins/trajectory.md).

Fixed the old `Trajecotry` label typo. Kept the entrypoint (`widget:SaveTopology`) and `@persist_plugin_state` so the manifest and `__init__`/`__main__` are unchanged. Verified before/after with headless offscreen `QWidget.grab()` PNGs. New Qt-free `test_view_model.py` (**5**: log-ready, set-trajectory notify, no-op without a trajectory, real first-frame save to PDB via a synthesized 2-frame `.h5`, view-spec loads) and rewritten `test_gui.py` (**2**: AutoForm construction, filename round-trip through the model) — all **7 pass**; the new files are lint- and format-clean. See [plugins/trajectory.md](/plugins/trajectory.md).
* **ChiMOL's non-lossy PDB fallback silently broke sticks and hid most atoms — the atoms/sticks renderers assumed a structured `_atoms` array the fallback never has.** Making the fallback carry a separate CA trace (previous fix) split the viewer's coordinates into `_coords` (trace, drives cartoon/trace) and `_all_atom_coords` (every atom, drives atoms/sticks/surface). Two interactive scene builders keyed off the *structured* `_atoms` array rather than `_all_atom_coords`, so a raw-coordinate object fell through both: **(1)** `_update_atoms`' all-atom ball path is gated on `_atoms is not None`, so the fallback dropped to a branch that sparse-samples ~50 points from `_coords` — and `_coords` is now the CA trace, so "not all atoms show"; **(2)** `set_coordinates` never computed `_bond_pairs`, so `_update_sticks` returned `None` immediately — "sticks stopped working". Crucially the **raytracer path (`get_atom_sphere_data`) already read `_all_atom_coords` directly**, so the offscreen-raytrace verification used for the previous fix could not have caught either gap — they only exist in the interactive GL builders. Fix: `set_coordinates` now computes `_bond_pairs` from the **raw** (unscaled) coordinates exactly as `set_structure` does, and sizes `_ball_mask`/`_sticks_mask` to the atom count; `_update_atoms` gained a raw-coordinate branch that renders **every** atom straight from `_all_atom_coords` (via `get_atom_sphere_data` + a new shared `_build_balls_mesh` merge helper), honouring an explicit per-atom mask only when its length matches. Verified headlessly on 148l through the fallback path: `_bond_pairs` = 1439 bonds → a `sticks` scene object, and `atoms_mesh` draws **all 1385 atoms** (not ~50). New `test_chimol_fallback_render.py` (**2**) drives the real `_update_atoms`/`_update_sticks` builders (not the raytracer) and pins both. Chimol suite **66 passed** (excluding the pre-existing `test_chimol_alignment`/`test_chimol_editing` failures and the `test_selection_grammar` collection error); no new lint in `renderer/view.py` (44 → 44). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **The same fallback split then broke dots and metaballs — a per-atom colour path that gated on `_residue_ids` but read `_all_atom_res_ids`.** Follow-up to the entry above. Because the fallback now populates `_residue_ids` (per-CA) while `_all_atom_res_ids` stays `None`, `_update_dots` entered its atom→residue colour block (`len(colors_per_ca) == len(_residue_ids)` is true) and then iterated `np.asarray(self._all_atom_res_ids)` — a 0-d `asarray(None)` — raising `TypeError: iteration over a 0-d array`. Since `_update_dots` runs inside the shared `_build_scene_for_current_object`, that exception aborted the **entire** scene build, so dots *and* metaballs (and cartoon, atoms, everything) disappeared the moment dots was enabled — hence "dots and metaball broken", though the metaballs code itself was correct. Fix: `_update_dots` now computes `atom_res` only when `_all_atom_res_ids is not None` and guards both colour blocks on it, matching the already-correct `_update_metaballs`/`_update_surface`/sticks paths; a raw-coordinate object keeps the base colour. Verified on 148l through the fallback: `dots` draws all 1385 points, `metaballs` renders, and a full `_update_view()` with both enabled no longer raises. `test_chimol_fallback_render.py` grew to **4** (added a dots-count test and a full-scene-build test — the latter being the shape that actually catches a builder that aborts the whole scene, which an isolated `_update_dots` call would not). Chimol suite **68 passed**; `renderer/view.py` lint unchanged (44 → 44). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **Metaballs then rendered but looked broken — a unit mismatch: density sigmas sized for unscaled Ångström against coordinates scaled 10×.** Third follow-up. `_all_atom_coords` is scaled by `_scale_factor` (default 10) and `set_structure` scales the real per-atom `radius` field to match; `_update_metaballs`/`_update_surface` derive their Gaussian sigmas from `_all_atom_radii`. The fallback left `_all_atom_radii = None`, so both fell back to a **constant sigma ~1.5–1.8 in the scaled frame** — ~10× too small, so the density barely overlapped between atoms and marching cubes returned a handful of disconnected specks (148l metaballs: **888 verts**) rather than an envelope. Rather than patch each renderer's default, fixed it at the source: `set_coordinates` now seeds `_all_atom_radii = _DEFAULT_ATOM_RADIUS_A (1.5 Å) × scale`, chosen just below the real vdW radii the reader assigns (measured on 148l: min 1.515, mean 1.775, max 2.027 Å), so balls, surface and metaballs all render in the right units and match the structured path. Verified end-to-end: 148l metaballs **888 → 131 525 verts** spanning the full molecular extent; surface 29 198 verts. `test_chimol_fallback_render.py` grew to **5** (added a metaball-density test asserting the vertex count and that the mesh bounding box spans the atom cloud — not merely that a mesh exists, which the broken 888-vert case also satisfied). Chimol suite **69 passed**; `renderer/view.py` lint unchanged (44 → 44). See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **Metaballs then rendered but looked wrong — too knobbly and glassy — and the tuning fix surfaced a config-loading footgun.** With the surface finally connected, the ask was a softer, blobbier, matte, AO-shaded look. Tuned the `metaball` display config: `iso_value` 0.15→0.1 and a new `sigma_factor` knob (2.2) replacing a **hardcoded ×1.5 sigma multiplier** in `_update_metaballs` fuse atoms into rounder blobs (148l metaball mesh 131 525→64 326 verts — half the high-frequency detail, i.e. smoother); `alpha` 0.6→1.0 (opaque) so the baked AO reads instead of a glassy blend; `ao_strength` 0.5/0.6→0.9 and `ao_radius`→7.0 deepen crevice shadows; `specular_strength`→0.12 and `shininess`→22 kill the wet-plastic gloss (traced to `qtgl.py`'s fragment shader: the gloss is a fake-MatCap env-reflection + sun highlight gated by `specStrength`, so matte = drop specular, not shininess). **The footgun:** editing only `chimol_display.json` had no effect for the author, because `config.py:load_display_config` keeps a *second* hardcoded `default` dict that is shallow-merged to fill any section the loaded file omits — and the loader prefers a stale legacy `~/.chisurf/protview_display.json` over the shipped package file, so the package JSON was shadowed entirely and `metaball` (absent from the legacy file) came from the code dict. Fixed by updating **both** copies to agree; verified through `load_display_config()` (what the GUI reads) that the live `metaball` now carries the tuned values, and raytraced 148l before/after offscreen to confirm the blobbier, smoother envelope. The legacy-file-outranks-package precedence is flagged in the profile as worth simplifying. Chimol suite **5** fallback-render tests still pass; `config.py`/`view.py` lint unchanged. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).

## 2026-07-21
* **Shipped macOS/Linux bundles carried the CI runner's path in every console script.** `build_installer.py` copies `dist/osx/bin` into `ChiSurf.app/Contents/bin` verbatim, but micromamba/pip bake the build-time prefix into the shebang of every entry point they generate — on GitHub Actions that is `/Users/runner/work/chisurf/chisurf/dist/osx/bin/python3.12`, a path that exists on no user machine. So `chisurf`, `csc` and all ~24 `csg_*` scripts inside the bundle died with "bad interpreter" the moment the `.app` was moved. **The main window still opened**, which is why this went unnoticed: the `.app` launcher execs `Contents/bin/python -m chisurf` directly and never touches a script — only the entry points reached through `PATH` (which the launcher prepends) were broken. New `relocate_scripts(bin_dir, build_prefix)` rewrites both shebang forms pip emits — the plain `#!<prefix>/bin/python3.12` and setuptools' long-shebang workaround (`#!/bin/sh` + `'''exec' "<prefix>/bin/python3.12" "$0" "$@" #'''`, used once the path exceeds 127 chars) — into a self-locating preamble that resolves the interpreter as a sibling of the script, and is itself both valid `sh` and a valid Python string literal so the payload below is untouched. Only shebangs pointing **into the build prefix** are touched (`#!/bin/sh` activation scripts are left alone), and a non-Python in-prefix interpreter is reported rather than wrapped, since the `sh`-exec trick is only valid inside a Python file. Also applied to the **AppImage**, which mounts at a fresh `/tmp/.mount_*` on every launch and therefore has the same defect. Windows is unfixed and out of scope here: Inno Setup installs to a user-chosen directory and pip's `.exe` launchers embed the path in a binary header, so it needs a different mechanism. Tests: `test/test_build_installer_relocation.py` (5) build a fake prefix, rewrite it, **move the tree**, and execute the scripts from the new location — both shebang forms, resolution through `PATH`, the leave-alone cases, and idempotency. See [workflows/build-and-env.md](/workflows/build-and-env.md).
* **ChiMOL's PDB fallback is no longer lossy — a reader failure now costs accuracy, not a working picture.** The fix below removed the *likely* trigger for the raw-coordinate fallback but left the fallback itself as the original bare `ATOM`/`HETATM` array, so any remaining path into it still rendered spaghetti — which is what "still broken" turned out to mean. Reproduced exactly by forcing `_ChiSurfStructure = None` in an offscreen window (the state the screenshot showed) and confirming the tangle returned. `_simple_load_pdb_coords` is replaced by `_parse_pdb_backbone`, returning a `PdbBackbone` dataclass: all coordinates **plus** the CA trace with residue numbers, names and chain ids. `set_coordinates`/`add_coordinates` accept those as keyword arguments and, when a trace is supplied, mirror `set_structure` — CA trace into `_coords`, all atoms into `_all_atom_coords` — so the cartoon/trace builders find their segment boundaries. **Two parsing constraints are deliberate and load-bearing**: the trace is built from `ATOM` records of the **first model only**, skipping non-first altlocs, because including `HETATM` puts ligands and waters on the backbone and including further `MODEL`s makes the trace jump between conformers — both produce exactly the streaks this fixes. The coordinates branch of the info panel now also computes Rg (from the unscaled coordinates at load time, since the viewer keeps only a centred/scaled copy) instead of hardcoding `?`. Verified end-to-end on 1dg3 with the factory forced off: **540 residues, real sequence (`HMTGPMCLIENTNGRLMANPEALKILSAITQPMVVVAIVG…`), Rg 38.62 Å, 5 correctly-segmented cartoon meshes**; raytraced offscreen, the fallback render is visually indistinguishable from the `Structure` path. New `test_pdb_fallback_backbone.py` (**8**) pins the metadata alignment, both segmentation directions (a synthetic gap/chain-change yields 3 segments; a gapless chain stays 1, so the check cannot pass by splitting everything), HETATM/multi-model exclusion, and the no-backbone and empty-file paths. A first draft asserted a chain break in `hGBP1_closed.pdb` and failed — that fixture is a **complete model with no gaps** (max CA–CA step 3.89 Å), unlike the RCSB 1dg3 download; the test was rewritten rather than the assertion loosened. Chimol suite **81 passed**; the 6 failures in `test_chimol_alignment`/`test_chimol_editing` and the `test_selection_grammar` collection error (`re.error: global flags not at the start`) are **pre-existing, verified by stashing**. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).
* **ChiMOL rendered loaded structures as spaghetti, and `radius_gyration` was not a radius of gyration.** One report — a `fetch 1dg3` showing a tangle of long blue streaks, `Residues: ?`, `Radius of gyration: ?` and `(no sequence information)` — turned out to be **one root cause plus one independent bug**. The tell was the atom count: the panel read `Atoms: 4698`, which is exactly `ATOM`(4357) + `HETATM`(341), i.e. the raw-coordinate fallback in `io/structure.py`, not the 4357 the `Structure` reader returns. That fallback carries no residue/chain arrays, and `renderer/view.py:_update_trace` only computes segment boundaries `if res_ids is not None or chain_ids is not None` — otherwise it splines **a single polyline through all 4698 atoms in file order**, scattered waters included. So the missing metadata and the ugly render were the same failure, not two. With the real reader, 1dg3 splits into **5 trace segments**, suppressing 4 spurious CA–CA connections, the longest spanning **22.2 Å** against a real bond of ~3.8 Å. Why the fallback was taken: `Structure` was imported in the *same* `try/except` as `chisurf.gui.widgets.general.open_files`, which pulls in the whole Qt stack — so any GUI-side import failure set `_ChiSurfStructure = None` and silently downgraded every load. The three imports are now independent, and both the factory-missing and reader-failed paths log a warning naming the consequence (the old `except Exception: structure = None` is why this was undiagnosable from the symptom). Separately, `Structure.radius_gyration` computed `sqrt((r-rM)**2).sum(axis=1).mean()` — per-axis *absolute* deviations summed, i.e. a mean Manhattan distance from the centroid, **not** `sqrt(mean(|r-rM|²))`; it over-reported by ~32% (1dg3 **51.35 → 38.96 Å**, 148L **23.44 → 16.30**, hGBP1 **50.32 → 39.24**). The two fixtures that pinned the wrong value (`test/fluorescence/test_structure.py` and the class doctest) were updated — both reproduced the old number exactly, confirming they encoded the bug rather than an independent measurement. `test/fluorescence/test_structure.py` 6 passed; the one failure, `test_labeled_structure` (an `@unittest.expectedFailure` on AV distance distributions), is **pre-existing and unrelated — verified by stashing**. No new lint findings in the touched files. See [plugins/profiles/chimol.md](/plugins/profiles/chimol.md).

## 2026-07-20
* **FRET auto-fit now fits real distances, closing the last gap in the model migration.** The FRET kind fitted N lifetimes and then *derived* `E = 1 − τ/τ_D0`, taking the longest fitted lifetime as unquenched donor — a weak inference that produced **no FRET parameters at all**, so there was nothing to link and nothing to constrain. `build_fret_fit`/`fit_fret_model` (in `decay_fit_model.py`) drive a real `GaussianModel`: each state is a Gaussian donor–acceptor distance whose **mean, width and species fraction are `FittingParameter`s**, alongside the donor-only fraction `xDOnly`, with efficiencies following from the fitted distances and R₀ (read from the Instrument dock). **R₀ and τ_D0 are held fixed** — they are calibration, not data, and fitting them against a distance distribution is badly conditioned because R₀ and R trade off through the same `(R/R₀)⁶`; `fit_donor_lifetime` frees τ_D0 for callers that want it. The lifetime→E route is kept for callers that only have lifetimes, with a docstring that now states which inference it makes. Shared wiring (data curve, fit window, convolution, nuisance terms — identical for every TCSPC model) was extracted to `_make_fit`/`_configure_lifetimes`. Validated against simulated decays at known distances (40/65 Å, R₀ 52 Å): distances recovered within **15%**, efficiencies consistent with them, and the donor-only fraction recovered to **0.25 ± 0.15** from a 0.1 start. `test/test_decay_fit_model.py` **21**, filter-calculator **64**, `test_decay_fit.py` + `test_tcspc_fit_convergence.py` green. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md) and [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **FCS Filter Calculator auto-fit now runs through a real ChiSurf `Fit`, and its parameters are linkable.** Completes the migration begun with `decay_fit_model.py`: `_auto_fit_components` calls `fit_lifetime_model` instead of the standalone scipy fitter, and the **Auto-fit dock renders the fitted `LifetimeModel`'s parameters in the standard AutoForm parameter table** (`ParameterGroupTableWidget`), whose context menu offers the ordinary **Link…** targets — which is what the auto-fit was asked for. Three reconciliations at the seam: (1) **amplitude convention** — the model reports pre-exponential amplitudes where the scipy fitter reported photon fractions, and everything downstream means fractions, so the seam converts (`f_i = a_i·τ_i / Σ a_j·τ_j`) rather than relabelling; (2) the **full decay + range** goes to the model, which masks the window itself, retiring the `fit_lo`-relative arithmetic the sliced-window call needed; (3) **IRF write-back** converts the model's σ (`iw`) to the detector table's FWHM exactly through `FWHM_TO_SIGMA` (the skew `ik` is the *same* generalized-normal shape parameter `synthetic_irf` takes, so it transfers unchanged) and reads the shift off the model's own processed-IRF peak, avoiding any dependence on the units of the internal timeshift. The table hides convolution/acquisition plumbing (`dt`/`rep`/`start`/`stop`/`irf_start`/`irf_stop`/`n0`/…), which the auto-fit configures rather than reports. Also extended the core: **`fit_irf` now fits the generated prompt's shape through the model's own `iw`/`ik` parameters** (`Convolve` synthesises a generalized-normal IRF whenever no IRF *curve* is stored), reaching parity with the scipy path's joint IRF fit — measured with **no** measured IRF: lifetimes 1.212/4.001 against a truth of 1.2/4.0, width |iw| 0.2506 against σ 0.25. Two traps recorded: the IRF **width is magnitude-only** (`_process_irf` takes `abs(iw)`; the asymmetry's sign lives in `ik`), and the IRF **timeshift must stay free** — an interim version pinned it whenever `fit_irf` was off, which biased the lifetimes and broke two passing tests, because a measured IRF is recorded separately from the data and its timing genuinely drifts. **Fixed a pre-existing bug this surfaced:** `Parameter.link`'s setter calls `controller.set_linked()`, which `FittingParameterProxyController` never implemented — so right-click → **Link…** raised `AttributeError` in *every* AutoForm parameter table, not just the new one; the proxy owns no editors, so it absorbs the notification and repaints its view. Filter-calculator suite **61 green** (18 core + 43 widget, run per-test around the known teardown abort); `test_decay_fit_model.py` 15. Unrelated pre-existing failure, verified by stashing: `test_parameter_prior_widget.py::test_focus_moving_to_a_child_keeps_the_popup_open`. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md) and [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **Decay fitting through the real model stack (`decay_fit_model.py`), step 1 of moving the FCS auto-fit onto `FittingParameter`s.** `chisurf/core/fluorescence/decay_fit.py` fits multi-exponentials with a standalone scipy optimiser, so its results are plain floats — not linkable to other fits, no covariance-derived errors, and a second forward model beside `LifetimeModel`'s. The new Qt-free `chisurf/core/fluorescence/decay_fit_model.py` fits the *same* problem through the real stack: `build_lifetime_fit(decay, bin_width=…, irf=…, …)` returns a runnable `Fit`, `fit_lifetime_model(...)` runs it and returns the keys `fit_lifetime_components` returns (so it can stand in) **plus** the live `fit`/`model` and per-parameter error estimates. It centralises the settings that make a `LifetimeModel` compute from an in-memory array — all of which **fail silently**, leaving a flat background (see [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md)). Two findings pinned by tests: (1) **the two fitters report amplitudes in different conventions** — `decay_fit`'s basis is unit-sum decay columns, so its amplitudes are **photon fractions**, while `Lifetime.amplitudes` are **pre-exponential** (`f_i = a_i·τ_i / Σ a_j·τ_j`; a 0.3/0.7 mixture at 1.2/4.0 ns reads as 0.11/0.89, so reading one as the other makes a real component look negligible); (2) an **unmodelled background biases both lifetimes upward** (1.2/4.0 → 1.48/4.17 on the fixture, χ²ᵣ 1.54 → 0.99 once fitted), which is what `fit_background` is for. Cross-fit linking is asserted end-to-end (`follower.link = target` propagates a value change). Also removed dead state in the filter calculator: `_auto_fit_settings["periodic"]/["period_ns"]` were dropped by `_sync_autofit_settings` on the first dock edit and read by nothing — periodicity comes from the Instrument dock via `_fit_period_ns`. `test/test_decay_fit_model.py` (12); `test_decay_fit.py` (9) and `test_tcspc_fit_convergence.py` (8) still green. **Still to do:** wiring the Filter Calculator's `_auto_fit_components` onto this, and the FRET (`FRETModel`) path.
* **Fit summary now reports priors and parameter intervals.** The fit-info panel showed only `chi2r`, the range and a Value/Error/Source/Link table, with no indication of which parameters carried a prior or what their uncertainty actually means. Added `Fit.prior_summary()` and `Fit.posterior_summary()`, rendered by `Fit.__str__` (which the info panel prints first, so every consumer of `str(fit)` gets them). The prior section separates *informative* priors, shown in full, from the uniform/box ones that merely restate bounds and contribute nothing to the objective — those are collapsed onto one line, since a typical TCSPC model reports six of them and they drowned out the one prior that mattered. The interval section prefers a chi² scan's profile crossings (possibly asymmetric) over the covariance matrix's symmetric Laplace `value ± error`, and names which one each row carries rather than presenting them as interchangeable. **The heading is conditional on purpose**: it reads *posterior (MAP)* only when an informative prior is attached, because with bounds alone the posterior is proportional to the likelihood inside the box and the interval is an ordinary confidence interval — calling it credible would be a claim the fit does not support. Verified against a real two-exponential TCSPC fit, with and without a Gaussian prior plus an adaptive scan. Tests: 6 in `test/fitting/test_priors.py` (47 total), including the wording distinction. No regressions: the related suites fail identically before and after (8 pre-existing failures).
* **Parameter popup still dismissed itself — the prior drop-down was the second cause.** The previous fix handled focus moving to a *child widget*, but clicking the prior combo still closed the popup. Reproduced headlessly by clicking each control in turn (`sb_value`, `cb_fixed`, `cb_bounds_on` fine; `cb_prior_family` closed it). Root cause: a combo's drop-down is its **own top-level window**, and `QWidget.isAncestorOf` is documented as confined to a single window, so the ownership test reported the drop-down's `QListView` as foreign even though its parent chain is `QListView -> QFrame -> QComboBox -> popup`. Ownership is now decided by walking `parentWidget()`, which does cross the window boundary. Opening the drop-down also *deactivates* the popup's window, so the two dismissal paths were split rather than merged: `FocusOut` hides unless focus is on a widget the popup owns, `WindowDeactivate` hides unless one of the popup's own windows is visible — deliberately **not** consulting the focus widget, because Qt keeps that pointing into the popup while the application is in the background, which would leave it open after the user switched away. Both checks are deferred to the event loop, since neither the new focus owner nor the drop-down's window exists until the transition settles. Tests: 8 more in `test_parameter_prior_widget.py` (33 total) — every control click parametrised, the drop-down opened *and* used, the deactivation/child-window distinction, and `_owns` across window boundaries. Two of them had to close the drop-down in a `finally`: leaving that window open **segfaults** qtbot's teardown.
* **FCS Filter Calculator auto-fit: fit range, IRF shift/skew, nuisance fractions, stacked filters, Instrument + Info docks.** Second pass over the auto-fit landed with the PAM-referenced fFCS work ([references/fcs-pam-port.md](/references/fcs-pam-port.md)). **Core** (`chisurf/core/fluorescence/decay_fit.py`, `fit_lifetime_components`): the synthetic IRF's **width, center/shift and skew** are now all free parameters (previously width only — the shift and skew were pinned at `2·FWHM` and 0, which left an antisymmetric residual at the rising edge); `include_scatter` (IRF-shaped column) and `include_background` (constant afterpulse column) fit the nuisance **fractions jointly** with the lifetimes so the lifetimes no longer have to absorb the prompt/baseline; and a `period` argument threads periodic convolution through `_basis`/`synthetic_decay` so the previous-pulse tail wraps into the window instead of showing up as a residual ramp. **GUI** (`fcs_filter_calculator`): the fit window is a draggable `LinearRegionItem` synced to spinboxes and overridable **per detector** (Info-dock table); fFCS filters are solved over that window (`_ranged_filters`) and a range change re-zeros from a cached un-zeroed copy, so dragging replots **without a refit**; residuals are masked to the range; a per-detector **Shift** column (ns) applies to measured *and* synthetic IRFs and persists in projects; a **Global (stacked)** multi-detector mode concatenates detectors onto one axis so the FRET-species inter-detector brightness ratios constrain the unmix; new **Instrument** dock (AutoForm: α/β/γ/δ, G, l₁/l₂, R₀, laser period) and **Info** dock. Scatter now reuses the detector's own IRF (`_detector_irf`) rather than an independent coarse re-fit, so scatter, component convolution and auto-fit share one IRF — `_synthetic_scatter_fits` survives only as the no-usable-IRF fallback (and is now initialised in `__init__`; it was created lazily, so two tests hit `AttributeError` on the attribute never existing). Validated on real `bh_spc132_sm_dna/m00*.spc` green (ch 0/1, 599–3274): **χ²ᵣ 1.48 → 1.05**. Tests: `test/test_decay_fit.py` (9, +3 for periodic convolution, joint width/shift/skew, and scatter/background fractions) and `fcs_filter_calculator/test/` (56 = 18 core + 38 widget), all green. **Known pre-existing defect, not from this change:** running the widget suite as one process aborts in Qt teardown (`libc++abi: Pure virtual function called!`); every test passes individually, and the abort reproduces identically with this change stashed. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Parameter popup closed on the interactions it exists for; prior selector de-duplicated.** Three fixes to `FittingParameterDetailPopup`. (1) **Dismissal**: both `focusOutEvent` and the event filter hid the popup on *any* `FocusOut` — but a widget receives one whenever a **child** takes focus, so clicking the value spin box or a radio button dismissed the popup mid-edit. Verified empirically: focusing `sb_value` sends exactly one `FocusOut` to the popup. The check is now deferred to the event loop (the incoming focus widget is only settled after the transition) and hides only when `QApplication.focusWidget()` is neither the popup nor a descendant; `WindowDeactivate` still hides immediately, and a new `mousePressEvent` dismisses on clicks outside the rectangle (`Qt.Popup` grabs the mouse, so they arrive at the popup). (2) **One prior selector instead of two**: a radio row (Box/Gaussian/Log-normal/Custom…) sat above a "Distribution" combo listing seven families, so Gaussian and Log-normal were shown in both and five families were reachable only through "Custom…". Now a single `Prior:` combo lists Box plus all seven; a prior this editor cannot express (callback, product) is reported through a read-only "Other" entry that is added and removed on demand and never overwrites the attached prior. Popup width **414 -> 282 px**. Folded the two default-seeding paths into one — a Gaussian's σ now defaults to a tenth of the parameter's magnitude rather than a flat 1.0, which the radio path did and the combo path did not. (3) Link/unlink buttons carry 🔗/✂️ icons and tooltips. Tests: `test/gui/test_parameter_prior_widget.py` grew from 12 to 25 — the family combo (all eight reachable, seeding, switching, "other" not clobbering a script prior) and five dismissal tests, including the child-focus regression. The two focus tests assert on the deferred check rather than on a second window stealing focus, because an offscreen `Qt::Popup` holds the keyboard grab and a real hand-off is not reproducible headlessly. `test_popup_edit_repaints_the_row_without_dispatching` still fails — **pre-existing test-order pollution**, passes alone, unrelated.
* **Parameter detail popup made compact: 650x520 -> 414x120 px.** `FittingParameterDetailPopup` laid every control on its own full-width row, so a popup meant to sit next to the parameter it edits covered most of the fit window. Rows are now merged where they read together — name + link state + Link…/Unlink in the header, Value + Fixed on one row, Enable-bounds + Low + High on one row — the `Prior` group box became an inline `Prior:` label + radio row (one less frame and two less rows of padding), the redundant "Click outside to close" hint is gone (the popup is a `Qt.Popup`; that is its documented behaviour), spin boxes are width-capped, and spacing dropped 4 -> 2 px. A long "Linked to: name (group / local)" string used to stretch the popup arbitrarily; the label now shows `→ name` with the full origin in its tooltip, matching the short-label/tooltip rule. Verified by an offscreen `QWidget.grab()` render. No behaviour change — every widget attribute the handlers and the existing tests use is unchanged (`test_parameter_prior_widget.py` + `test_parameter_table_actions.py`: 24 passed; the one failure, `test_popup_edit_repaints_the_row_without_dispatching`, is **pre-existing test-order pollution** — it passes alone and fails identically with this change stashed).
* **`Lifetime.lifetimes` getter no longer writes on every read (23% faster in isolation, no end-to-end effect).** The getter computed `math.sqrt(x.value ** 2)` per lifetime and then wrote every value back to its parameter — the same getter-performs-writes anti-pattern fixed earlier in `Parameter.value`. Now reads each parameter once, takes `abs()`, and writes back only where the sign actually flipped (normally never, since lifetimes are positive). Sign-flip semantics verified preserved: a negative lifetime still reads back positive *and* is persisted. Controlled best-of-3: `L.lifetimes` **6.918 -> 5.354 us**. **But `update_model` is unchanged (0.0787 -> 0.0785 ms, best-of-5)** — 1.5 us saved in an ~79 us evaluation is below this harness's resolution. Kept as cleanup, not claimed as a speedup. A first attempt guarded the write with `if p.value != v`, which re-read the parameter and made it *slower* (7.14 -> 9.39 us) now that writes are cheap; restructuring to read once fixed that. **Assessment: the fit path is at diminishing returns.** Of a ~117 us `get_wres`, the remaining items are convolve 26 us (already the C SIMD kernel), `anisotropy.get_decay` ~11 us, cached `irf` 11 us, `convolve.scale` 11 us, `lifetime_spectrum` 13 us and `parameter_values` 12 us — no single dominant target left, and the last several attempts have landed at or below measurement noise.
* **TCSPC periodic convolution switched from the numba reimplementation to tttrlib's C kernel.** Safe now that the two agree to 1e-15 (see the off-by-one fixes above). One-identifier change at the call site; the tttrlib wrapper already existed with an identical signature. Verified end-to-end: chi2r **1.012636 before and after**, the model curve agrees to **8.3e-16**, and the fixture still recovers tau 4.0087/1.2003. Gains: the **~110 ms numba JIT compile on first `update_model()` is gone** (165.7 -> 54.4 ms) and a model evaluation is **~5% faster** (0.0824 -> 0.0779 ms). **Not the 2.2-2.7x I had implied** — that was the convolution kernel measured in isolation, but it is only ~8 us of an ~80 us model evaluation, so a 2.2x there is worth ~5% of the whole. Iteration counts shift chaotically because a 1e-15 model change perturbs the finite-difference Jacobian and the amplitude parameterisation is near-degenerate: on the single convergence fixture tttrlib took 405 evals vs numba's 230 (looking 59% *slower*), but across six starting points it was **875 evals / 135.2 ms vs 1050 / 166.8 ms** — the single-fixture result was path luck, and neither number is a property of the kernel. Both always reach the same chi2r. No regressions across decay conv/fit, convolution reference, TCSPC convergence, fit, models, IRF and priors.
* **Import hygiene: `scipy.stats` removed from the TCSPC import graph — but NO measurable speedup.** I predicted a large win here and did not get one; recording the negative result. `-X importtime` attributed ~0.9 s to `scipy.stats` when importing a lifetime model, reached three ways: a **dead** `import scipy.stats` in `models/tcspc/nusiance.py` (the file uses scipy nowhere), another **dead** one in `core/fitting/fit.py` (only `scipy.linalg` is used), `core/math/rand` via eager submodule imports in `core/math/__init__.py`, and `core/fluorescence/tcspc/__init__.py` eagerly importing `irf_estimation` -> `scipy.signal` -> `scipy.stats`. Removed both dead imports, deferred `scipy.stats` in `math/signal.py` to its single call site, made `core.math` submodules lazy (PEP 562, matching the existing `fio`/`fluorescence` treatment), and made `IRFEstimator`/`irf_estimation` lazy. Verified: `scipy.stats` and `scipy.signal` are no longer imported at all, and all four changes are needed — reverting the lazy `core.math` alone brings `scipy.stats` back. **But best-of-5 import time is unchanged (1891 ms vs 1864 ms baseline).** The reason: `import scipy.stats` costs 688 ms *standalone*, but almost all of that is scipy's shared core, and ChiSurf loads `scipy.optimize` (406 ms) and `scipy.linalg` (227 ms) anyway for the optimiser — so only a thin top layer was skipped. `-X importtime`'s cumulative column credits shared subtrees to whichever importer reaches them first, which is what made the 0.9 s look addressable. Kept as dependency hygiene (two dead imports gone, a rare module no longer loaded by every model, smaller graph), explicitly **not** as a performance change. numba (290 ms) remains and is genuinely required by the convolution kernels.
* **Two off-by-one bugs in the periodic TCSPC convolution — the model function itself was wrong.** Chasing why ChiSurf's numba periodic convolution disagreed with tttrlib's C reference by 1e-5 turned up two errors, both from translating C `for (i = ...; i <= stop; i++)` loops into Python `range()`. (1) The convolution loop started at `i = 0` and read `irf[i - 1]` — which in Python **wraps to the last IRF sample** instead of being out of range — and also added a second contribution to `decay[0]` on top of the explicit line above it. (2) The periodic tail loop used `range(stop)` where the reference uses `i <= stop`, so the final channel never received its tail term; invisible for a couple of lifetimes, 1e-5 relative for a **128-exponential FRET spectrum** where tail terms accumulate. The sibling *non-periodic* kernel in the same file already used the correct `range(1, ...)`, which is what made the first one recognisable. After both fixes the two implementations agree to **1e-15 across numexp 1→128**, so the C version was right all along. This changes fitted values slightly — it is a correctness fix, not a regression — and no existing test changed result. Tests: `test/test_periodic_convolution_reference.py` (9) pin agreement with the C reference at rounding level, plus channel-0 double-counting, the final-channel tail, and an IRF-wrap probe; all 9 fail without the fix. **Also found, not yet acted on:** the model uses this numba kernel rather than tttrlib, so it is 2.2–2.7x slower than the now-SIMD C path and costs a **338 ms JIT compile** on first `update_model()`; and importing a TCSPC lifetime model takes **3.3 s**, pulling in `chisurf.core.experiments` (1.77 s) and the whole FCS reader stack (0.90 s) via `core.curve` → `core.math`.
* **Two plot-construction defects fixed; fit-window setup 431 -> 323 ms.** (1) `ResidualPlot` iterated its `fit` argument, so a plain `Fit` (rather than a `FitGroup`) raised `TypeError: 'Fit' object is not iterable` and the plot could not be built at all — new `_member_fits()` treats a lone fit as a group of one. (2) pyqtgraph >= 0.14 moved `autoRangeEnabled` from `PlotWidget` to `ViewBox`; chisurf already ships a compat shim but applied it only from `get_win()`, so anything constructing a plot outside full GUI startup (tests, scripts, headless harnesses) hit a swallowed `AttributeError` and took a degraded path. The shim is idempotent, so it is now also applied at `gui_tweaks` import. **Correction to the previous entry**, which listed that AttributeError as a GUI bug: it was an artifact of a harness that never called `get_win()` — the shipped GUI was never affected. Removing the exception path did cut LinePlot construction 271 -> 189 ms. Remaining plot-construction cost is pyqtgraph itself (~32 ms per LinePlot warm, which builds three `PlotWidget`s); the 189 ms first build is one-time module import and Qt font setup, and the fit subwindow already instantiates plots lazily per tab. Tests: `test/gui/test_plot_construction.py` (3).
* **Opening a fit window stalled ~230 ms JIT-compiling a three-line statistic.** Built the first headless harness that constructs a fit's real plot widgets and measured them, which reframed the GUI question entirely: steady-state plot updates are **2.6 ms** for all five plots — trivial against a 60 ms fit — so there is no per-fit GUI cost worth chasing. The cost is **one-time**, when a fit window first renders: 391 ms of Qt/pyqtgraph widget construction plus 238 ms of first-update, of which **LinePlot alone was 229.5 ms**. Profiling that showed it was entirely `_build_metrics_overlay_text` -> `fit.durbin_watson` -> **numba `_compile_for_args`**: the Durbin-Watson statistic was `@nb.jit(nopython=True)` *without* `cache=True`, so an O(n) sum-of-squared-differences compiled on the GUI thread the first time any plot drew its metrics overlay, once per process. Replaced with plain NumPy (3.3 us per call, no compile): LinePlot first update **229.5 -> 2.43 ms (94x)**, total first-update **238.2 -> 11.9 ms (20x)**, steady state unchanged. Tests: `test/math/test_durbin_watson.py` (13) pin the arithmetic against the original loop, the three limiting cases (white noise ~2, random walk <0.5, alternating 4(n-1)/n), degenerate inputs, and a guard asserting it is not numba-compiled again. Still open: the 391 ms of widget *construction*, which is Qt/pyqtgraph and a different problem; `ResidualPlot` fails to construct outside the fit subwindow ('Fit' object is not iterable); and a swallowed `AttributeError: autoRangeEnabled` fires from pyqtgraph on every LinePlot build.
* **Fitting 4.1x faster cumulatively: the convolution wrapper stopped rebuilding a cached IRF.** `Convolve.convolve` resized the IRF to the data length and renormalised it on every model evaluation — a resize copy, **two** full `sum()` passes and a dividing copy — even though `irf` is itself served from a cache and `_process_irf` has already normalised it. That was **~19 us of a ~40 us call**. Now cached against the curve's identity and the target shape (the upstream cache returns the identical object while nothing changes). `c.convolve()` **40.55 -> 29.01 us**; fit **63.4 -> 60.7 ms**, cumulative **246.3 -> 60.7 ms (4.06x)**, chi2r unchanged at 1.0126, zero regressions. Also measured the post-fit path a GUI user pays on every click: `update_error_estimates()` 1.24 ms, `finalize()` 0.96 ms, `__getstate__()` 0.20 ms, `update()` 0.24 ms — together ~4% of a fit, so not worth attacking. **GUI plot rendering remains unmeasured**: the headless fixture attaches no plots, so the redraw cost a user actually perceives after a fit still needs a real GUI harness.
* **Fitting 3.9x faster cumulatively: uniform priors no longer instantiated per evaluation, shifted IRF cached.** Two more from direct timing of a `get_wres` call. (1) **`_prior_residuals` cost 20.3 us per evaluation to contribute nothing.** A *bounded* parameter reports a `UniformPrior` — bounds and priors are deliberately one concept — and `UniformPrior.residuals()` is empty by construction because the box is enforced as a hard optimiser bound instead. So every bounded parameter was constructing a throwaway prior object on every residual evaluation. Now skipped, but the check must consult **both** stores: a distribution prior mirrors a serialisable spec onto the port, while a *callback* prior is runtime-only and deliberately leaves `port.prior` as None with the live object on the parameter. My first attempt checked only the port and silently dropped callback priors from the objective — caught by `test_callback_prior_enters_map_objective`. 20.3 -> 6.2 us. (2) The **shifted** IRF is now cached alongside the processed one: the timeshift is one of several free parameters, so during a finite-difference Jacobian sweep it is unchanged for all but one column and most evaluations re-shift the identical curve by the identical amount. Measured best-of-7: fit **84.0 -> 63.4 ms**, cumulative **246.3 -> 63.4 ms (3.88x)**, model evaluation **0.4196 -> 0.0884 ms (4.7x)**, chi2r unchanged at 1.0126. Zero regressions (per-file results byte-identical). Remaining hot spots, all measured not profiled: `anisotropy.get_decay` ~10.5 us, `lifetime_spectrum` 14.2 us, `convolve.scale` 10.4 us, cached `irf` 10.2 us, `corrections.pileup` 5.8 us.
* **Fitting ~1.5x faster again (cumulative 2.9x): the parameter *write* path, a stat() in the hot loop, and a wasted roll.** Follow-up to the read-path/IRF-cache work. cProfile pointed at `Port.value`'s **getter** (0.040 s self over 4434 calls) — but direct timing showed it costs **0.156 us**, i.e. that figure was almost entirely profiler overhead. Measuring instead of profiling relocated the cost: of a ~210 us `get_wres` call, `update_model` is 130 us and the **`parameter_values` setter is 70 us** — 9.57 us to write a single float, because chinet's `Port.value` setter runs `atleast_1d` + three allocating `np.where` NaN/inf passes + `astype` + `clip` for every scalar assignment. Added a fast path for writing a finite Python float into a scalar float port (bounds still applied; NaN/±inf deliberately fall through to the sanitising path): **9.57 -> 0.81 us per write**. Two smaller ones: `Curve.__init__` ran `pathlib.Path(filename).is_file()` — a filesystem **stat** — on every construction including the in-memory curves built per model evaluation (now skipped when the filename is empty), and `shift_array` did two `np.roll` allocations even for whole-sample shifts (second roll skipped when the fractional part is 0; identical for finite input). Measured best-of-7: fit **122.8 -> 84.0 ms** this round, **246.3 -> 84.0 ms (2.93x)** cumulative, model evaluation **0.4196 -> 0.0931 ms (4.5x)**, chi2r unchanged at 1.0126. Zero regressions — per-file results across models/decay/priors/fit/grouping are byte-identical with and without. Tests: `test_parameter_bounds_api.py` (17) now also pins the setter fast path (round-trip, bounds, NaN/inf sanitisation, int/vector writes, fixed ports, link propagation).
* **Filter-Calculator residuals fixed: fFCS filters now solved over the fit-range window + scatter uses the auto-fit IRF.** The reconstruction had a systematic antisymmetric spike at the prompt and a ~+6σ offset across the tail even though the auto-fit itself was good (χ²ᵣ≈1.05, flat). Two root causes, both consistency bugs between the auto-fit and the *filter* reconstruction the user actually sees. (1) **Scatter/IRF mismatch:** the filter's synthetic scatter was an *independent* coarse re-fit (`optimize_synthetic_scatter_pattern`, ±3-bin grid) whose prompt didn't line up with the auto-fit's precisely fitted IRF — `_nuisance_patterns` now uses the detector's own IRF (`_detector_irf`, carrying the fitted width/skew/shift) as the scatter shape (label `…, detector-IRF`), so scatter, the component convolution and the auto-fit share one IRF (prompt residual 22→~11σ). (2) **Full-range vs windowed fFCS:** the filters/g-matrix were solved over the whole decay (including the excluded pre-prompt and far-tail bins) and only the *output* was zeroed to the range, biasing the reconstruction. New `_ranged_filters` solves `calc_ffcs_filters` on the `[start, stop]` slice and embeds the result back (in-range filled, reconstruction == total outside ⇒ residual 0), used by the single- and multi-detector paths (tail offset 6.3→0.16σ, rms 1.53 on the real BS/green `bh_spc132_sm_dna` data). Because the filters now genuinely depend on the window, a range change re-fits (`_on_fit_range_committed` → recompute) rather than re-zeroing from cache. The remaining ~10σ at the prompt is the synthetic-Gaussian-vs-real-IRF shape limit (needs a measured IRF). Tests: filter-calc `test_widgets.py::test_filters_computed_over_fit_range_window`; the scatter-label assertion updated to `detector-IRF`. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Filter-Calculator: Instrument dock (AutoForm), periodic convolution, Info-dock layout fix.** (1) **New Instrument dock** — an AutoForm (`instrument_options.py` + `.view.json`, a `scalar_table` + toggle + `help`) exposing the calibration/instrument constants **α, β, γ, δ, G-factor, l₁, l₂, R₀, laser period** and a periodic toggle; the values are **pre-populated from the selected setup** (`_prepopulate_instrument_from_setup`, refreshed on setup change) and consumed by the auto-fit and FRET-species seeds. (2) **Periodic (laser-period) convolution** is now threaded through the fitter: `fit_lifetime_components`/`_basis` gained a `period` argument (→ `synthetic_decay(period=…)`, the existing `convolve_lifetime_spectrum_periodic_nb` kernel), and the auto-fit reads it from the Instrument dock (`_fit_period_ns`, 0 ⇒ full micro-time window) — verified to fit a wrap-around decay exactly (χ² 0.53→0.00) where the aperiodic fit leaves a residual ramp; the generated components carry `period_ns` so they stay periodic. (3) **Info-dock vertical stretch fixed** — the per-detector range table was `Expanding` and ballooned to a big empty box; it now hugs its content (`_size_range_table`, `QSizePolicy.Fixed`) so the Information text takes the remaining space. Tests: `test/test_decay_fit.py::test_periodic_convolution_fits_wraparound_decay`; filter-calc `test_widgets.py::test_instrument_dock_autoform_and_period` (dock count 7→8). *Still open (user-requested):* when **Pol** is on, the auto-fit should fit anisotropy (ρ, r₀, G-factor) and the lifetime/FRET spectrum **simultaneously** over the parallel/perpendicular channels — the Instrument dock now supplies G/l₁/l₂ for it. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **`Parameter.lb`/`ub` existed as constructor kwargs but not as properties — assignment was a silent no-op.** `Parameter.__init__` takes `lb`/`ub` (158 call sites pass them) yet there were no accessors, so `p.lb` raised `AttributeError` and `p.lb = 0.01` quietly created a dead instance attribute while the bound was never applied — which is exactly how a fixture ended up with lifetime bounds that looked set and were not. Added `lb`/`ub` properties over the port's stored bounds. They read back the way `__init__` spells an absent bound (`±inf`) and round-trip even while `bounds_on` is False, because `Port.bounds` reports `(None, None)` whenever enforcement is off although the values *are* stored — enforcement stays `bounds_on`'s job. Tests: `test/fitting/test_parameter_bounds_api.py` (7).
* **Fitting is ~1.8x faster: parameter reads no longer write, and the processed IRF is cached.** Profiling a converging TCSPC fit (7 free params, 234 model evaluations) showed the numerics were not the cost. Two defects dominated. (1) `Parameter.value`'s **getter wrote back to the port on every read** — running chinet's expensive setter (`atleast_1d` + three `np.where` sanitisation passes + `astype` + `clip`) and calling `update_attached_node()`, so every parameter *read* invalidated the node graph. Reads outnumber writes by orders of magnitude and the value is unchanged unless a bound clamped it, so the write-back is now conditional on `v != raw`. (2) `Convolve._process_irf` was **42% of total fit wall time**, rebuilt identically on all 234 evaluations (702 `Curve` constructions, 468 `Curve.__sub__` copies) although it only depends on the source IRF, background, window and width/shape parameters — none of which move during a fit. It is now memoised, with the timeshift (a genuine fit parameter) applied after the cache since `__lshift__` copies. The cache keys on `hash(y.tobytes())` rather than a summary statistic: `sum()` is blind to in-place reordering, so an `np.roll` on the IRF would have silently served a stale curve — covered by a test. Measured best-of-7 on the new headless fixture: fit **246.3 → 134.4 ms (1.83x)**, single model evaluation **0.4196 → 0.1291 ms (3.25x)**, chi2r identical at 1.0126. No regressions — models/decay/IRF suites are 8 failed + 65 passed with and without, and `test/fitting` per-file results are unchanged. Bounds-clamping on read still works (regression test added). Note `Parameter` exposes `bounds=(lo,hi)`; there are **no `lb`/`ub` setters**, so `p.lb = ...` silently creates a dead attribute — that bit the fixture and is now documented in the tests.
* **Filter-Calculator UX batch: fraction display, Info dock, per-detector fit range, range-zeroed filters, live range updates.** Several linked improvements. (1) **Fitted fractions are shown persistently** — each auto-fit component's list label carries its fraction (`τ=1.20 ns (58%)`), the status line + `cs.logging.info` record the full breakdown including `scatter_fraction`/`background_fraction`. (2) **Auto-fit clears the component list before adding** the freshly-fitted set (was appending). (3) **New Info dock** (`_build_info_panel`) gathers all state — mixed-decay source/files, bins/bin-width/µ-time binning, global fit range, per-detector IRF (width/skew/shift/measured), components, last auto-fit, and per-result filter counts/mode — refreshed on every `_update_plots`. (4) **Per-detector fit range**: the Info dock hosts an editable Detector/Start/Stop table (`_detector_fit_ranges` overrides; blank ⇒ global region). (5) **Filters are zeroed outside the fit range** (`_zero_filters_outside`/`_apply_range_to_result`) in every compute path (single/multi/stacked/anisotropy), using each detector's range; residuals are masked per-detector. (6) **Changing the range auto-updates filters + residual plots without a refit** — the fFCS filters are computed over the full decay and only zeroed to the range, so `_apply_range_to_result` caches the un-zeroed filters and a region drag-release / spin edit / Info-table edit just re-zeros from the cache and replots (`_on_fit_range_committed`), so narrowing then widening correctly recovers columns. (7) **Correlator→Filter-Calc re-sync**: `showEvent` re-adopts the Correlator's current files (when the total is correlator-sourced, `_total_from_correlator`) and refreshes the plots on every visit, not just first show. Tests: filter-calc `test_widgets.py` — `test_filters_zeroed_outside_per_detector_fit_range`, `test_fit_range_change_auto_updates_filters_without_refit`, `test_info_dock_gathers_state_and_per_detector_range_editable` (dock count 6→7). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Fitting: unbounded parameters were silently NaN inside the optimiser.** `leastsqbound` optimises in internal coordinates via a sin/arcsin transform and detected "unconstrained" by testing `bound is None` — but `Model.parameter_bounds` reports an absent bound as `±inf`. An `(-inf, inf)` parameter therefore took the *bounded* branch, `arcsin((x-lower)/(upper-lower) - 1)` → `arcsin(inf/inf - 1)` → **nan**, poisoning every free parameter. Symptom: a TCSPC fit started AT the true parameters (chi2r 1.55) walked away to NaN. Fixed with an `_is_unbounded()` helper treating non-finite bounds as free; a two-exponential fit from a poor start (τ 8.0/0.4) now recovers τ 4.009/1.200 and amplitudes 0.695/0.305 at chi2r 1.01. No regressions — the fitting suite's pre-existing failures are byte-identical before and after. Added `test/fitting/test_leastsqbound_infinite_bounds.py` (10 tests, 6 of which fail without the fix) and `test/fitting/test_tcspc_fit_convergence.py`, the repo's first **working headless TCSPC fit fixture** — the existing `test_reference_models.py` uses `model.data` and a writable `lifetime_spectrum`, both long gone. Driving a `LifetimeModel` headlessly needs four settings that each fail *silently* into a flat background: `convolve.stop` is in TIME units and defaults to 0; `convolve.dt` is its own parameter defaulting to 1.0; the IRF must be in counts because `_process_irf` subtracts `lamp_background` then clips; and `irf_stop` defaults to 1 with a **broken setter** that wraps the value in `np.array([v])`, which the parameter rejects. Two further defects are documented but NOT fixed: (a) `scatter` defaults to exactly its lower bound 0.0, where the sin-transform has zero derivative, so the optimiser jumps (chi2r 1.55 → 1765) — captured as a strict xfail; (b) the amplitude parameterisation `|a|/Σ|a|` is exactly scale-invariant, so n amplitudes carry n−1 DOF (Jacobian rank 1 of 2, lifetime block full rank as control) and the redundant direction lets amplitudes collapse to 0/0. Pinning one amplitude removes the NaN but measurably *worsens* the fit (chi2r 2.98 vs 1.01), so it was not adopted.
* **Project ACLs were written and checked under two different object types — archived projects could never be restored.** `record_operation` seeds the owner ACL with object type `"operation"` (the logical name, matching `artifact`/`sample`/`experiment`), but the project browser authorized against `"mmfdb_operation"` — the *table* name — in all 12 of its ACL sites (`require_access`, `filter_readable`, `chmod`, and the direct `mmfdb_object_acl`/`mmfdb_acl_entry` queries). Since the ACL object type is an opaque key, the two spellings never met: the owner's ACL row was invisible to every check, so archive succeeded and `project_browser.restore` raised `Permission denied` for every non-admin user, and project visibility always read back as `private`. Aligned the project browser to the canonical logical name. Also fixed a second FK-integrity defect in the same area: `save_pipeline`/`record_pipeline_run` stamp `created_by_user_id` with the configured default user but never seeded that user row, so a `default_user_id` that was never provisioned failed the foreign key and rolled the whole save back — added `_ensure_user`, mirroring the guard `result_registry` already applies on its write path. Tests: `test/fio` is green (250 passed); `test_project_actions_archive_and_restore` and the two `test_pipeline` round-trips now cover both. See [subsystems/project-persistence.md](/subsystems/project-persistence.md), [subsystems/pipeline.md](/subsystems/pipeline.md).
* **`test/fio` suite realigned with the PRD-37 fail-closed auth boundary.** Eight failures were tests asserting pre-hardening behavior: two encoded "anonymous callers may browse/open datasets when a `default_user_id` is configured", which [PRD-37](/prds/prd-37.md) deliberately reversed — authorization is default-deny and, per `_require_auth`, "independent of database contents", so a configured default user stamps ownership on write but must never stand in for a verified principal on a read. Rewrote those as `test_datasets_open_rejects_anonymous` plus a positive `test_datasets_open_with_session_token`, and gave the audit/archive/browser tests real `create_session` tokens instead of weakening the boundary. Two others bypassed the real registration path (raw `db.register_artifact`, direct repository writes) and so never got the owner ACL that registration seeds — fixed the fixtures to match what production actually writes. No production auth code was relaxed. See [prds/prd-37.md](/prds/prd-37.md).
* **Filter-Calculator auto-fit now fits scatter + background fractions and honors the fit region.** The residuals were systematically bad because the lifetime fit had to absorb the prompt (scatter) and baseline (afterpulse/background), railing a component at the τ upper bound. `fit_lifetime_components` gained `include_scatter` (an IRF-shaped column — the current fitted or measured IRF) and `include_background` (a constant/afterpulse column); their non-negative amplitudes are solved by NNLS alongside the lifetimes and returned as `scatter_fraction`/`background_fraction` (+ absolute amplitudes), so the reconstruction and χ² reflect how well those nuisance terms are captured. The Filter-Calculator auto-fit drives them from the **AP** / **IRF** input toggles, reports the fractions in the status line, and now uses the **draggable fit region as the fit window** (`fit_lo = start`, no longer forced to 0) so the fit respects the user's range; the fitted IRF shift write-back accounts for that window offset (`fit_lo·dt + center − 2·FWHM`) and components are convolved from the absolute prompt (`comp_start = 0`, `apply_irf` always on). Validated on the real BS/green example files (`bh_spc132_sm_dna/m00*.spc`, channels 0/1, range 599–3274): χ²ᵣ dropped 1.48→1.05 and the spurious 8 ns tail component was replaced by an explicit ~22% background + ~25% scatter. Tests: `test/test_decay_fit.py::test_fits_scatter_and_background_fractions`; the idealized `test_auto_fit_adds_lifetime_components` now disables the nuisance toggles for its pure-lifetime check. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Filter-Calculator auto-fit crash on correlator/file data fixed (IRF built at the real bin count).** After the joint-IRF-fit + shift work, clicking **From correlator** then **Auto-fit** raised `ValueError: irf must contain a positive value inside the decay window` (`scattered_light_decay_pattern` inside `synthetic_decay`). Root cause: `_detector_irf` defaulted to **256 bins** whenever `_total_vector` is `None` — which it always is for file-/correlator-backed totals — so the synthetic IRF was far shorter than the real micro-time axis, and the fitted shift (valid within the real window) pushed the prompt off the end, leaving an all-zero IRF. New `_current_n_bins()` derives the length from the active total (`_total_decay()` when `_total_vector` is `None`); `_detector_irf` takes an explicit `n_bins` (auto-fit passes the real one) and falls back to the unshifted IRF if a large shift/skew still empties the window; `_add_autofit_species` drops to no-IRF convolution rather than passing a degenerate one; `_commit_fret_component` uses `_current_n_bins()` too. Tests: filter-calc `test_widgets.py::test_auto_fit_on_file_backed_total_with_nondefault_bins` (2048-bin file-backed decay). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Filter-Calculator auto-fit now optimizes IRF width, shift AND skew; example components clear on data load.** (1) **Joint IRF fit extended:** `fit_lifetime_components(fit_irf=True)` previously fitted only the FWHM (center tied to `2·FWHM`, skew fixed). It now fits the **center/shift** (`irf_center0`/`irf_center_bounds`, default initial = detected rising edge) and **skew** (`irf_skew`/`irf_skew_bounds`) as free parameters too, returning `irf_center` + `irf_skew` alongside `irf_fwhm` (dof accounts for 3 IRF params). The Filter-Calculator auto-fit passes the detector's current width/skew as starting values and writes all three back to detectors lacking a measured IRF — `set_width`, new `set_skew`, and `set_shift` (shift = fitted center − nominal `2·FWHM`, consumed by `_detector_irf`→`_shift_irf`); the status line reports FWHM / shift / skew. Verified end-to-end: recovers width 0.30, center 0.90, skew 0.40 (χ²≈0) from a synthetic IRF-convolved bi-exponential. (2) **Example components are eye-candy:** the two seeded example species are tagged `example: True` and dropped by `_clear_example_components()` the moment measured data is loaded (`_set_total_paths`, so Load / drag-drop / From-correlator all clear them); user-added components survive. The fit-range region now also re-seeds from a **file-backed** total that has no computed result yet (`_update_plots` falls back to `_total_decay()`), since clearing the example can leave zero species. Tests: `test/test_decay_fit.py::test_fits_irf_width_shift_and_skew_jointly`; filter-calc `test_widgets.py::test_example_components_cleared_when_data_loaded`. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Filter-Calculator: global (stacked) multi-detector filters use FRET-constrained inter-detector scaling.** The FRET species patterns already carried the correct inter-detector brightness ratios (`fret_species_patterns(normalize=True)` normalizes *jointly* across channels), but the independent per-detector filter path (`_compute_filters_multi_detector`) column-normalized each detector on its own and so *discarded* that ratio. New **Global (stacked) multi-detector filters** toggle (`stacked_mode_cb`, below the Detectors table) adds `_compute_filters_stacked`: each detector's total decay and per-species patterns are concatenated onto one axis (`[green | red | yellow]`, joint scaling intact), one filter set is solved jointly (`compute_filters` over the stacked axis, per-detector nuisance stacked by index), and the global result is split back into per-detector `FilterResult` slices stored in `_result_multi_detector` — so the existing multi-detector plotting/export path renders each segment unchanged and each detector's `to_channel_filters()` returns its slice of the global filters. Because the stacked species patterns keep their relative brightness, a species that is bright in green and dim in red constrains the unmix (verified: stacked filters ≠ independent filters for an asymmetric FRET species). Independent per-detector filters remain the default (toggle off); the mode persists in the project `ui_state`. Tests: filter-calc `test_widgets.py` — `test_stacked_mode_computes_global_filters_split_per_detector`, `test_stacked_mode_uses_fret_interdetector_scaling`. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Filter-Calculator: residuals masked to the fit range + per-detector IRF time shift.** Two refinements from live use. (1) **Weighted-residuals are now only drawn inside the selected fit range** — a new `_mask_to_fit_range()` sets residual bins outside `[start, stop]` to NaN and every residual trace (single / multi-detector / anisotropy ×2) is plotted with `connect="finite"`, so the excluded region simply isn't shown (the ±3σ/0 guide lines still span the axis). (2) **New per-detector IRF "Shift" column** in the `DetectorIrfTableWidget` (ns, between Skew and the IRF load button) applied to **both** the measured and the synthetic/computed IRF via `_shift_irf()` (sub-bin linear interpolation, zero-filled edges; positive = later) inside `_detector_irf` and the measured-scatter branch of `_nuisance_patterns`. Shift is always editable (unlike Width/Skew, which lock when a measured IRF is loaded) and is persisted in the detector `export_state`/`import_state` (`set_shift`/`shift` accessors). Tests: filter-calc `test_widgets.py` — `test_residuals_masked_to_fit_range`, `test_detector_irf_shift_moves_prompt_and_persists`. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Filter-Calculator auto-fit: draggable fit region survives replots; auto-fit fits the IRF when none is loaded.** Two GUI regressions/gaps from live use. (1) **The fit-range region item vanished on the first recompute.** Every plot update calls `PlotWidget.clear()`, which removes *all* items — including the `LinearRegionItem` selector added once in `__init__` — so the region disappeared as soon as filters computed and never came back (the user saw "no fit range in the plot"). A new `_clear_recon_plot()` clears then re-adds `self._fit_region`; all four reconstruction-plot update paths (single / multi-detector / anisotropy ×2) route through it. (2) **Auto-fit now fits the synthetic IRF when the primary detector has no measured IRF.** Previously it did a tail fit with no IRF; now, when `_measured_irf_vector(primary)` is `None`, it fits with `fit_irf=True` over a window extended down to the prompt (`fit_lo=0`, so the rising edge makes the FWHM identifiable), writes the fitted FWHM back to every selected detector lacking a measured IRF (`DetectorIrfTableWidget.set_width`), and builds each species' per-detector pattern convolved with that detector's IRF from `start_bin=0` (`_add_autofit_species(..., apply_irf=True)`). With a measured IRF the proven tail fit/alignment is kept. The status line reports the fitted IRF FWHM. (3) Verified the **detector on/off checkboxes do drive recompute** (`selectionChanged → _on_detector_selection_changed → _compute_filters`, results change 3→2 detectors) — the earlier "no effect" was the multi-detector crash (now fixed) plus the wiped region making the replot look static. Tests: filter-calc `test_widgets.py` — `test_fit_region_survives_replots`, `test_auto_fit_fits_irf_when_no_measured_irf`, `test_auto_fit_joint_irf_fit_resolves_distinct_components` (was the tail-range test, updated for the joint-IRF behavior). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Filter-Calculator multi-detector FRET compute no longer crashes on zero-emission species.** In multi-detector (per-channel) mode a species can legitimately emit *nothing* in a given detector — e.g. a FRET-sensitized acceptor pattern is all-zero in the donor/green channel — so its per-detector pattern sums to zero. `_nuisance_patterns` fed those patterns straight into `optimize_synthetic_scatter_pattern` (the synthetic-scatter/IRF unmix helper), which rejects zero/degenerate basis vectors and raised `component_decays[1] must match total_decay, be non-negative, and be non-zero`, aborting the whole compute (`Multi-detector computation error`). The scatter fit uses the species patterns only as an unmix basis, so `_nuisance_patterns` now **sanitizes** that basis — dropping any pattern that is length-mismatched, non-finite, negative, or zero-sum — before calling the helper (all four compute paths route through `_nuisance_patterns`, so single/multi/anisotropy are fixed at once). The *filter* computation itself was already safe: `calc_ffcs_filters` guards zero column-sums and falls back to `pinv` on the singular normal matrix, yielding a clean finite **zero filter** for a non-emitting species — the correct result, since it keeps species indexing consistent across detectors for cross-fFCS. Regression test `test_nuisance_scatter_fit_ignores_zero_emission_species` in filter-calc `test_widgets.py`. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Table-rendered parameters get a controller again; a named-but-unknown `fit_uid` no longer retargets another fit.** Two problems surfaced by running the GUI after the `parameter_group_table` work.
