(1) **`parameter.controller` was never set by the table.** The per-parameter row widgets do `fitting_parameter.controller = widget` (`make_fitting_parameter_widget`), and both `FittingParameter.update()` and `FittingParameterGroup.finalize()` drive the display *through* that attribute — so for every table-rendered parameter those calls did nothing, logging `Parameter 'sc' has no controller to finalize.` once per parameter (the visible symptom; the real cost is a row that does not repaint when the model changes a value). `ParameterGroupTableWidget` now claims each parameter's `controller` with the `FittingParameterProxyController` it already builds for the link menu / detail popup, and releases the back-reference on `destroyed` so a dead table never leaves a deleted proxy behind. **This closes a feedback loop that had to be broken at the same time:** `finalize()` is called *during* a recompute, and the table's `on_change` is `AutoForm._dispatch_fit_update` (a `fit.update` action), so a repaint that dispatched would request the recompute that caused it. A proxy's `finalize()` is now a display refresh of its own row only (matching the row widget's `finalize` semantics), and `sync()`/`refresh` — which runs *after* a fit — no longer dispatches either (it emitted `dataChanged` over the whole table, which `_on_data_changed` could not distinguish from a user edit; a `_suppress_change` guard now separates programmatic repaints from real edits). Genuine cell edits still dispatch. (2) **`_resolve_fit` fell back to `fit_index` when a supplied `fit_uid` matched nothing** — so a client naming a specific fit could have the operation silently applied to a *different* one. Observed as `RemoteError: parameter 'dt' not found` (the fallback landed on fit 0, which has no `dt`); confirmed by test that the old code returned `ok: True` and wrote the value to the wrong fit. A non-empty unmatched uid is now `NOT_FOUND`; empty/absent still means "unspecified" and uses the index. **Follow-up (the GUI then reported `fit not found` for `fit.range.auto`/`fit.set_fit_range`, which the fallback had been masking): the uid lookup only ever matched top-level fits.** `state.fits` holds `FitGroup`s whose member `Fit`s carry *different* uids, and the fit controller addresses the member it shows — so **every** member-uid lookup missed and silently landed on `fit_index` 0 instead (with a Global-Fit at index 0 that is also the likely source of the `parameter 'dt' not found` above). `_resolve_fit` now matches group members too, returning the member paired with its group's index. The client half: `_parameter_context` yields `""` when it cannot locate a parameter's fit and the 26 `fitting_client` methods only skipped `None`, so `FittingClient._try_rpc` now drops a falsy `fit_uid` instead of forwarding an unmatchable one. Tests: `test/gui/test_parameter_table_actions.py` (15, incl. the real `model.finalize()` warning symptom and the loop guards), `test/server/test_service_robustness.py` (+2), `test/test_fitting_client.py` (+2). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md), [architecture/server.md](/architecture/server.md).
* **H2MM surrogate in C++ (PRD-60)**: The amortised neural estimator now has a C++ implementation in the photon-processing library — a reusable, domain-agnostic `NeuralNet` (train + infer, Adam/backprop, JSON models) plus an `H2mmSurrogate` adapter, so future surrogates supply only a feature extractor and decoder. ChiSurf side: `SurrogateModel.export_json()` writes a language-neutral JSON schema, `core/surrogate_tttrlib.py` executes it, and `core/engines.py` routes JSON surrogates there while pickled models stay on the scikit-learn path; 8 cross-engine tests prove identical estimates either way. Reaching that took matching NumPy's histogram bin-edge algorithm exactly and disabling FMA contraction (33/200 photons differed otherwise). Benchmarked rather than assumed: ~10x faster per estimate than EM, trains in seconds, and accuracy splits by state separation (surrogate ~4x better where the MLE is unidentifiable, EM better on well-separated states) — so no averaged accuracy claim. Also benchmarked forward-mode AD for the fitting path (1.6x-9.8x on gradient cost, scaling with parameter count) without converting any fitting code; see the library's PRD-010.
* **`parameter_group_table` rows regain parameter linking and the detail popup.** Swapping the verbose `parameter_group` sections for the compact `parameter_group_table` (see the 2026-07-19 entry) silently dropped the two per-parameter actions the row widgets carried: the **right-click link menu** (link/unlink a parameter across fits) and the **detail popup opened by clicking the parameter's label**. Rather than duplicate that logic in the table, the parameter-centric actions were extracted from `FittingParameterWidget` into a `ParameterActionsMixin` (`_locate_parameter*`, `_parameter_indices`, `_parameter_context`, `_trace_operation`, `make_linkcall*`, `build_link_menu`, `_update_linked_parameters`) and a new `FittingParameterProxyController` mixes it into a bare hidden `QWidget` that wraps one `FittingParameter` and forwards `finalize()` to an `on_change` callback — the controller contract `FittingParameterDetailPopup` and the link menu are written against, minus the row editors (the popup now falls back to default decimals via `_controller_decimals` instead of reading `controller.widget_value.opts`). `ParameterGroupTableWidget` builds one proxy per row lazily: right-click prepends a `🔗 Link <name> to` submenu + `⛓️‍💥 Unlink` (enabled only when linked, routed through the fitting client with a `parameter_unlink` provenance trace) above copy/paste, and a left-click on the **Name** column opens the same popup positioned under the cell; both refresh the row and fire the section's `on_change`. **Follow-up fix (found by running the GUI):** editing the value in the popup crashed with `AttributeError: 'FittingParameterProxyController' object has no attribute '_trigger_model_update'` — that method (recompute the fit + refresh visible output-parameter widgets) had been left behind on `FittingParameterWidget` although it only needs `_parameter_context` + `self.window()`, so it moved into the mixin with the rest. The class of bug is now guarded: a test parses `FittingParameterDetailPopup` for every `self.controller.X` it touches and asserts the proxy has them all, so a controller call added for the row widget fails in tests instead of at runtime. Tests: `test/gui/test_parameter_table_actions.py` (8). Pre-existing, unrelated: the full `test/gui` run segfaults and `test_every_configured_tcspc_model_resolves_with_a_name` fails on the untouched tree too. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Filter-Calculator auto-fit + reusable decay-mixture fitter.** New `chisurf/core/fluorescence/decay_fit.py` — a general, dependency-light decay fitter built on the canonical `synthetic_decay`: `fit_lifetime_components` (discrete multi-exponential, bounded log-space lifetimes via `scipy.least_squares`, non-negative amplitudes by NNLS each step, Poisson-weighted residuals, optional **joint IRF-FWHM fit** via `synthetic_irf`) and `fit_component_amplitudes` (weighted NNLS unmixing). Wired into the FCS Filter Calculator as a `🎯 Auto-fit` toolbar action: prompts for `N`, fits the measured mixed decay, appends one synthetic species per component (each with per-detector IRF-convolved `patterns_by_detector`), and auto-computes the filters. **Fit/filter range:** a draggable `pg.LinearRegionItem` on the reconstruction plot defines the fit window (defaults just past the prompt → near the end); the auto-fit does a robust **tail fit** over it (`fit_lifetime_components` with no IRF, components aligned to the range start via `start_bin`) — fitting the *whole* trace with an unanchored fitted IRF collapsed both lifetimes to one and diverged on real BH-SPC data, whereas the tail range resolves distinct components. `DetectorIrfTableWidget.set_width` updates the spinbox **display** (not just the backing `_width` dict) for manual IRF edits; the status line reports the range, per-component fractions and χ²ᵣ. (The core `fit_lifetime_components` retains an optional joint IRF-FWHM fit for other callers.) The fit range is exposed **both** as the plot region and **start/stop spinboxes** (bidirectionally synced) and is **re-defaulted for each newly-loaded dataset** — a stale range left over from the built-in example (fitting pre-prompt bins) was collapsing real BH-SPC fits to one component. **FRET auto-fit:** the Auto-fit dialog offers *Lifetime species* / *FRET species*; the FRET path takes the longest fitted donor lifetime as τ_D0 and turns each fitted lifetime into a FRET species with E=1−τ/τ_D0 (τ_D0→donor-only), expanded to per-detector coupled patterns — a starting FRET set. Bulk species addition suspends the per-add recompute (`_suspend_compute`) and computes once at the end (multi-detector recompute per add was very slow). The transient input popups were replaced by a persistent **Auto-fit dock** (`_build_autofit_panel`, mirrored into `_auto_fit_settings` by `_sync_autofit_settings`) — component type (lifetime / FRET), number of components/states, the lifetime min/max bounds, a live "Fit range: a–b TAC bins" readout kept in sync with the plot region/spinboxes, and a `🎯 Fit + generate filters` button with an inline status line — so the settings stay visible beside the decay instead of vanishing with a modal, and the collapse-to-one-component (from defaulting N=1) is user-controllable (default N=2; the core fitter resolves distinct components on BH-SPC-like data). (An interim modal `_show_auto_fit_settings_dialog` was superseded by this dock.) The fit region is now drawn prominently on the decay plot (brighter brush + hover, `zValue` above the traces so the handles are grabbable). Also added the mixed-decay `📂 Load…`/`📡 From correlator` controls and the data-derived micro-time axis (bin width from the TTTR header + optional `microtime_binning` from the shared `FcsWorkflowContext`). Tests: `test/test_decay_fit.py` (6), filter-calc `test_widgets.py` (+auto-fit, +correlator-total, +micro-time-axis). **Next (user-requested):** migrate the auto-fit onto the real TCSPC `LifetimeModel`/`FRETModel` so it uses `FittingParameter`s cross-linkable to existing fits, and so FRET-species auto-fitting reuses the FRET model. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **GUI startup ~2× faster (splash phase 10.8s → 5.8s) by removing discarded work.** Profiling (new per-stage timing in `AppStartupServiceManager._start_service`, exposed as `.timings`, slow stages logged at INFO) showed the splash phase was dominated by work that was thrown away, not by unavoidable cost. Five fixes, each measured: (1) **`load_tools` 3.00s → 0.80s** — the ribbon/toolbar/menu builders imported *every* plugin module just to read an optional `icon` module attribute, even though `icon_utils.create_plugin_icon_with_fallback` resolves the manifest icon and on-disk `icon.png`/`icon.svg` *first*; it now accepts a **callable module provider** so the import happens only for the handful of plugins that actually need it (4 call sites: `ribbon_plugins.py`, `ribbon_categories.py`, `gui/main.py`, `gui/__init__.py`). Verified equivalent: across all 111 plugins the lazy and eager paths resolve **identical** icons, importing 29 modules instead of 111. The worst case alone (`intensity_trace`) cost ~1s because a module-level `from hmmlearn.hmm import GaussianHMM` pulled in scikit-learn — now function-local. (2) **`iter_plugins()` memoized** (`chisurf/plugins/__init__.py`) — it re-walked the whole plugin tree and AST-parsed ~200 `__init__.py`/`manifest.json` files on each of its 4 startup calls; new `invalidate_plugin_cache()` is called from the plugin-manager rescan and the plugin-check refresh. Its redundant legacy AST parse is now skipped when the manifest already supplies description + CLI entrypoint. (3) **`setup_style` 1.39s → 0.45s** — the identical 31 kB stylesheet was applied twice (once in `get_app()` before any widgets exist, once after the window was built); `_apply_stylesheet` now early-returns on an unchanged sheet, since Qt rebuilds `QStyleSheetStyle` and re-polishes the whole widget tree on every `setStyleSheet`. (4) **`init_setups` 1.81s → 0.87s** — `_setup_experiment` eagerly constructed a controller widget for every reader of all 8 experiments (18 widgets) though `_refresh_setup_ui` only ever shows **one**; `ExperimentReader.controller` is now a property backed by `set_controller_factory`, built on first access (`Experiment.add_reader` no longer clobbers a pending factory with `None`). (5) **`gui_imports` 3.56s → ~2.9s** — `chisurf/core/fio/__init__.py` and `chisurf/core/fluorescence/__init__.py` eagerly imported all their sub-packages, so importing a small reader (`fio.ascii`, `fluorescence.fcs`) paid for the entire fluorescence/pandas/scipy stack; both now expose sub-modules via a PEP 562 module `__getattr__`. New concept [architecture/gui-startup.md](/architecture/gui-startup.md) records the staged-startup design and the four laziness invariants so these regressions are recognizable. Verified against baseline: core suites (`fio`/`fluorescence`/`startup`/`experiments`/`models`) are **26 failed / 405 passed both with and without the changes** — all 26 pre-existing — and a boot-and-select harness confirms all 20 reader controllers still build and render.

## 2026-07-19
* **Central table formatting, unified FCS component editor, `scalar_table` section.** Three consistency fixes from a screenshot review. (1) **Central table formatting:** `chisurf.gui.widgets.general.table_font` is now the single source of truth and defaults to the system **monospace** font (+ Monospace style hint) when no `gui.table.font_family` is set, so the log table, `parameter_group_table`, and AutoForm record `table` sections all match the log/console and numeric columns align (configurable via `gui.table`). (2) **Unified add-component path:** the FCS Filter Calculator's two separate "Add synthetic/fit…" and "Add FRET species…" context-menu entries + dialogs are merged into one **➕ Add component…** dialog with a **Type** selector (Lifetime spectrum / FRET species) that swaps the embedded editor; commit logic factored into `_commit_synthetic_component` / `_commit_fret_component`, double-click edit reuses it. (3) **New reusable `scalar_table` AutoForm section** (`chisurf/gui/autoform/sections/scalar_table_section.py`) — a compact name/value table for plain `float`/`int`/`bool` model attributes (HTML labels, central monospace format), adopted in the FRET-species editor's FRET / Crosstalk / Anisotropy / Histogram groups to shrink the tall one-field-per-row layout. Tests: `test/gui/test_scalar_table_section.py` (4), `test_parameter_table_widget.py` (20), Filter-Calc `test_widgets.py` (15), FRET editor (9). Follow-up polish: AutoForm record `table` sections now **size to their content** (header + rows, no vertical expansion) instead of leaving a large empty area below few-row spectrum tables (the `height` option becomes a fixed/scroll cap when set); and the FRET-species editor regained **convolution** controls — a Laser period (periodic convolution, threaded through `fret_species_detector_patterns` → `_apply_irf` as FFT circular convolution) and a colour shift — since the per-detector IRF is applied from the Detector settings but the laser-period/colour-shift are per-component. The synthetic-decay and FRET-species editors now lay out as a **`dock_area` (`split: horizontal`)** — parameters in a scrollable left dock, the live decay preview in a right dock (rearrangeable/floatable) — instead of the plot stacked below a tall form; the AutoForm dock area is now configured like the rest of ChiSurf (no plain "+" tab button, ChiSurf context menu). **Fix — table edits recompute the preview even inside a dock:** a dock can reparent/float the plot out of the AutoForm's child tree, so `refresh_plots()` (which used `findChildren`) stopped reaching it and edits stopped redrawing the curves. AutoForm now also tracks every `PlotWidget` and `AUTOFORM_REFRESH` widget it builds in a `_refresh_targets` registry and refreshes those too, so a floated/reparented plot still updates; `persist` was dropped from the two modal editors so they open deterministically. Regression test in `test_fret_species_editor.py`. Also: the FRET-species anisotropy is now a **spectrum** `r(t)=Σᵢ bᵢ·e^(−t/ρᵢ)+r∞` (multi-exponential rotation) — `Anisotropy.donor_spectrum`/`acceptor_spectrum` lists of `{amplitude, rho}` (single `r0`/`ρ` kept as the fallback), edited as donor/acceptor rotational-component tables in the editor. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Adopted the compact `parameter_group_table` section across all model editors.** The AutoForm already had a `parameter_group_table` section (`ParameterGroupTableWidget`, a `QTableView` with a name/value/fixed/bounds/error model + checkbox delegates) but it was **unused** — it lacked the refresh markers, so it never participated in the form's post-fit refresh. Made it a true drop-in: added `AUTOFORM_REFRESH` + a `refresh` alias, and broadened `AutoForm.sync_fields()` to also sync/refresh `AUTOFORM_REFRESH` widgets (not just `is_form_field` ones) — so its displayed values update after a fit exactly like the per-parameter widgets. Then swapped every verbose `{"type":"parameter_group"}` → `{"type":"parameter_group_table"}` across **17 model view.json** (all TCSPC/DEER/RICS/PDA model editors, 50 sections) for a much more compact, parameter-dense layout (same `target`/`collapsible`/`exclude_source`). Updated the model-editor tests (deer/pda/rics/integration/auto_model_widget) to count table rows alongside `parameter_widgets`. **Polish** (from a screenshot review): the Name column now renders parameter `label_text` **HTML** (sub/superscripts — `n<sub>0</sub>`→n₀, `&tau;<sub>0</sub>`→τ₀) via a `_RichTextDelegate`; the table is **sized to its content** (Name column stretches, numeric/checkbox columns hug contents, `stretchLastSection` off, vertical scrollbar off, fixed height = header + rows) so it no longer wastes vertical space or leaves a wide empty Bounds column; and a right-click **context menu + Ctrl+C/Ctrl+V** copy/paste of cell values (scalar fills all selected editable cells; a tab/newline block pastes from the top-left). The table now uses the shared configurable **table font** (`gui/widgets/general.py::table_font` + `table_row_height`/`table_header_height`, from the `gui.table` settings) and defaults to a **monospace** face (`QFontDatabase.FixedFont` + Monospace style hint) so it matches the log/console and numeric columns align. NB: an earlier duplicate `parameter_table_section.py` I had drafted was removed in favour of this canonical section (do-not-duplicate). Pre-existing unrelated failure noted: `EtModelFreeWidget` missing `.name` (untouched `et.py`). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Coupled per-detector FRET-species decay components (Filter Calculator).** A component can now be a whole smFRET labeling state with *different, physically-coupled* channel decays instead of one decay shared across detectors. New core `chisurf/core/fluorescence/fret/species_decay.py::fret_species_patterns` for states donor-only / DA / acceptor-only: green = donor (FRET-quenched in DA), red = FRET-sensitized acceptor (donor-decay-shaped rise via the reused `fret/acceptor.py::da_a0_to_ad` — whose latent spectrum-indexing bug `[i]`→`[2i]` I fixed) + donor leakage (α) + direct excitation (δ), yellow = acceptor's own decay; optionally split ∥/⊥ by a per-chromophore anisotropy `r(t)=(r0−r∞)e^(−t/ρ)+r∞`. **Channel amplitudes come from the full excitation `[laser,chromophore]` + emission `[chromophore,detector]` matrices** (so relative weights are physically consistent), with the familiar Hellenkamp **α/β/γ/δ kept as the user-facing knobs** that induce those matrices. FRET given as efficiency E or a **distributed distance** — Gaussian components (mean/σ/fraction) reusing the TCSPC-fit distance grid + `distance_to_fret_rate_constant` — plus R0/κ² and a donor-only fraction `x(D-only)`. Decays are generated **ideal (no IRF)**; each detector's own IRF is applied at compute time from the Detector settings and **normalized to unit sum** (`normalize_irf`). Fractions are NOT inputs — each state is one component, unmixed by the fFCS filters. New GUI editor `chisurf/gui/widgets/fret_species_editor.py` (+ `.view.json`, AutoForm tables for spectra + distance distribution), opened from the Components context menu as *🔬 Add FRET species…* (double-click to edit); previews the ideal green/red/yellow(∥/⊥) decays live and seeds α/β/γ/δ/R0 from the selected detector-setup calibration; on accept expands to `patterns_by_detector` via `fret_species_detector_patterns` (detector colour → channel, applying that detector's normalized IRF). Tests: `test/test_fret_species_decay.py` (12), `test/gui/test_fret_species_editor.py` (9), filter-calc `test_widgets.py` +1. *Follow-up:* the legacy hand-built TCSPC FRET-fit input widgets are not yet AutoForm, so the FRET-parameter/distance-distribution *input UI* is not yet shared between the fits and this editor (the core physics already is). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Reusable `?` help-modal section, Filter-Calculator Components context menu, editor de-clutter.** (1) **General UI rule + mechanism:** a new reusable AutoForm `help` custom section (`chisurf/gui/autoform/sections/help_section.py`) renders a small `?` `QToolButton` that opens a modal `QTextBrowser` (Markdown/HTML from `text`/`resource`) — the standing rule is to put *longer* explanations behind a `?` modal rather than inline paragraphs (short labels + tooltips remain the default). Added to the shared synthetic-decay editor. (2) **Components list context menu:** right-click the FCS Filter Calculator Components list for Add measured / Add synthetic-or-fit / **Edit…** (reopens the shared editor prefilled via `SyntheticDecayEditorModel.load_component`, replacing the item in place through `SpeciesListWidget.replace_synthetic_source`; double-click also edits) / Remove; the `➖ Remove` toolbar button was removed (removal now lives on the context menu + Delete). (3) The shared editor's redundant `status_text` info line was dropped. Tests: `test_widgets.py` +3 (no-remove-button, edit-replaces-in-place, help-section-registered). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md), [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Synthetic-decay editor gains skewed-Gaussian IRF, colour shift, and periodic (laser-period) convolution.** The shared editor's synthetic IRF now routes through the canonical `tcspc/irf.py::synthetic_irf` (generalized-normal) with a **skew** field (0 = symmetric); an **experimental IRF** file still overrides it. New `synthetic_decay` params: `time_shift` (a wrapped sub-bin IRF colour shift via the new reusable `tcspc/convolve.py::periodic_shift`) and `period` (routes to the `fconv_per` periodic kernel `convolve_lifetime_spectrum_periodic_nb`, adding the geometric inter-pulse tail `1/(1-exp(-period/τ))` so the unrelaxed decay of earlier pulses wraps into the window). `synthetic_component_decay` threads the persisted `period_ns`/`time_shift_ns`/`irf_skew` so a Filter-Calculator reconstruction matches the editor preview. Editor view adds an IRF panel (experimental file / Gaussian FWHM / skew / colour shift) and a Convolution panel (laser period). Tests: `test/gui/test_synthetic_decay_editor.py` +4 (periodic_shift wrap, skewed-IRF asymmetry, time-shift delays peak, periodic tail lift). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **One shared synthetic-decay editor across the FCS Filter Calculator and the acquisition simulator.** The two synthetic-decay editors had diverged into different widgets ("why so different? makes no sense — unify"). New reusable `chisurf/gui/widgets/synthetic_decay_editor.py::SyntheticDecayEditorModel` + `synthetic_decay_editor.view.json` — a single AutoForm editor with the amplitude/lifetime spectrum table (➕/➖), IRF (file or synthetic Gaussian FWHM), Poisson shot-noise (photons+seed), histogram size, a measured-pattern override (📂 Pattern… / ✖ Clear), 📥 Fit… (Read from Fit), and a live decay preview (all through the canonical `synthetic_decay`). The Filter Calculator's `_add_synthetic_dialog` now embeds it and reads `editor_model.component()` for the persisted source dict; the acquisition simulator's `DecaySettingsDialog` wraps it with a per-species selector (push/pull the shared editor's `spectrum_rows`/`pattern_path`/`irf_fwhm_ns` per species). Removed the now-dead plugin-local `SyntheticSpectrumViewModel` / `synthetic_editor.view.json` (Filter Calc) and the hand-built τ-table/IRF/preview widgets (acq). **Framework:** editable AutoForm tables now refresh the hosting form on cell edit (walk-up to `sync_fields`+`refresh_plots`, mirroring value/toggle/button sections), so any table-driven preview/derived field stays live. Tests: `test/gui/test_synthetic_decay_editor.py` (7) + updated acq `test_setup_widgets.py` (species-switch preserves edits) + Filter-Calc `test_widgets.py` (Fit-button label). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md), [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **AutoForm `call` auto-refreshes dependent widgets; acq simulator regains per-species brightness table.** Two fixes to the acquisition-simulator settings after a UX review against the pre-AutoForm dialog (`67a614ff` migrated the 4068-line hand-built dialog to AutoForm and lost per-species brightness + species-count reactivity). (1) **Framework:** a field's `call` (`ValueSection`/`Toggle`/`Choice`) now walks up to the hosting AutoForm and refreshes its `AUTOFORM_REFRESH` widgets, so a control that changes shared state (e.g. the species count) updates dependent widgets whether the form is standalone or embedded in a settings page — previously the species field only worked when the dialog manually wired `_refresh_widgets`, so in the embedded ChiSurf-Settings page "changing the number of species did nothing." (2) **Per-species brightness:** the single-species Channels table (q replicated across species) is replaced by a `_SpeciesTable` (`acq_species` custom section) — one row per species (grows/shrinks with the Species count) plus a Background row, columns M / D / per-enabled-colour ∥/⊥ brightness q; new model fields `species_M`/`species_D`/`species_q` (with legacy-`q` expansion in `from_parameters`), so each species has its own molecules, diffusion and per-detector brightness again. The Decay modal already edits amplitude+lifetime pairs. Tests updated: `test_setup_widgets.py` (per-species + resize + colour-enable). Remaining vs. the old UX: derived τD/Nfcs/S-total cells and the dark-state (k_bd/k_bb/k_db) kinetics grid are not yet re-added. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Reusable AutoForm `rate_matrix` section + `button_row` popup-menu mode; acq kinetics + amp/τ decay.** New `chisurf/gui/autoform/sections/rate_matrix_section.py` — a reusable editable N×N transition-rate grid (off-diagonal i→j rates; diagonal fixed 0) that tracks a `size_attr` (e.g. the species count) and writes a flat row-major list back to the model; intended for kinetic/interconversion matrices throughout ChiSurf. `ButtonRowSection` gained a `menu` field: a non-empty value collapses the button row into one popup `QToolButton` (emoji labels as inline icons) — space-efficient tool actions. Applied both in the acquisition simulator setup dialog: a collapsible **Kinetics** panel with `k_rad`/`k_nrad` rate matrices that resize when the **Species** count changes (`n_species` field `call="species_changed"` → refresh), and the bottom `🛠 Tools` menu (Decay / Load / Save / View JSON). The per-species **Decay** modal now edits **amplitude + lifetime** pairs (was lifetime-only) — `_species_decay` accepts `[amp, τ]` pairs (or bare τ) and feeds `synthetic_decay(taus, amplitudes=amps)`. Tests: `test/gui/test_rate_matrix.py` (2, general) + acq `core/test/test_setup_widgets.py` (kinetics-tracks-species, amp/τ round-trip). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).

## 2026-07-18
* **Acquisition simulator settings: a modal Decay editor (with load-existing-pattern) + compact Channels table.** The simulation setup dialog (`plugins/core/acq/tcspc_devices/simulation/setup_dialog.py`) had no way to define the fluorescence decay. New modal `DecaySettingsDialog` (opened by a `🧬 Decay…` button): a per-species lifetime (τ) table + Add/Remove, a shared Gaussian IRF FWHM, a `📂 Load pattern…` button to use an existing measured/saved decay histogram (text/.npy) instead of lifetimes, and a live decay preview (built through the canonical `synthetic_decay`). It writes back per-species `decay_lifetimes` / `decay_pattern_files` / `irf_fwhm_ns`, which `to_parameters` now emits and `build_engine` consumes (a loaded pattern → `SimDecay.from_pattern`; else lifetimes → `synthetic_decay`). Separately, the cramped **Channels** section (~14 flat q/background fields) is replaced by a compact custom `acq_channels` table — one row per colour (Green/Red/Yellow) with an enable checkbox and its `q ∥ / q ⊥ / bg ∥ / bg ⊥` columns bound to the model. Tests: `.../simulation/core/test/test_setup_widgets.py` (3: decay round-trip, load-pattern, channels binding) + the engine `test_decay.py` (3). See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Wired the canonical decay generator into the photon-stream simulators.** The acquisition TCSPC simulator (`plugins/core/acq/tcspc_devices/simulation/core/algorithms.py::build_engine`) previously ignored its `decay_lifetimes` param and never set `SimSpecies.decay`, so simulated photons had no lifetime structure — the synthetic decay generator was "missing in simulator/acq". It now builds each species' `SimDecay` from `decay_lifetimes[i]` (+ optional shared Gaussian IRF via `irf_fwhm_ns`) through the canonical `synthetic_decay` → `tttrlib.SimDecay.from_pattern`. The CLSM imaging simulator (`core/fluorescence/imaging/simulate.py::_lifetime_species`) was a second local duplicate (`np.convolve(np.exp(-t/τ), irf)`) — now also routed through `synthetic_decay`. Verified: longer lifetime shifts the simulated mean micro-time later (1 ns → bin ~31, 6 ns → ~149), IRF gives a prompt offset. Tests: new guarded `.../simulation/core/test/test_decay.py` (3, skip without tttrlib SimEngine); imaging-sim suite (6) still green. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **One canonical synthetic-decay generator + a full-stack `synthetic_decay` plugin; filter-sweep TODO.** Consolidated decay generation: `chisurf/core/fluorescence/decay.py` gained `synthetic_decay` / `synthetic_component_decay` (built on the shared `calculate_fluorescence_decay` + `tcspc/convolve.py` IRF kernels + `sample_decay_shot_noise`), replacing the FCS Filter Calculator's duplicate `api.py::synthetic_decay` (a local exponential + `np.convolve` reimplementation, now a thin re-export). New full-stack plugin `chisurf/plugins/fluorescence_decay/synthetic_decay/` (**Spectroscopy:Fluorescence Decay:Synthetic Decay Generator**): transport-agnostic `core/algorithms.py`, Click CLI (`synth-decay generate|component`), RPC services (`synthetic_decay.compute[_component]`), and an AutoForm GUI (editable lifetime-spectrum table + histogram/IRF/noise + live decay plot). The acquisition `core/experiments/tcspc/simulator.py` intentionally stays on the lower-level `calculate_fluorescence_decay` (rise terms / zero-lifetime components that the strict wrapper rejects). Tests: plugin `test_core.py` (7: core/CLI/RPC + a "uses core, not a duplicate" guard) + `test_widgets.py` (3); Filter-Calc suite still green. Also filed a **TODO (autoresearch)** in [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md): a fast auto-optimizing filter sweep maximizing species-filter contrast (→ anti-correlation contrast), with the `flc_2d` decay-unmixing machinery as a reuse candidate to drive/seed fFCS filter computation.
* **FCS Filter Calculator: unified Detectors + per-detector IRF table.** The detector-selection checkbox list and the confusing hidden per-detector IRF editor (a "Detector" combo + shared File/Width/Skew fields, where switching the combo changed nothing visible) are merged into one `DetectorIrfTableWidget` (`gui_parts/widgets.py`): a row per detector with a select checkbox, name, editable synthetic-IRF **Width** (FWHM ns) and **Skew**, and an **IRF** column whose `…` button loads a measured IRF (then becomes `✕` to unload). While an IRF is loaded, Width/Skew are grayed and auto-populated from the measured IRF (`irf_width_skew_ns` estimates FWHM + skewness). **Pol** on splits each detector into parallel/perpendicular rows with independent IRF/width/skew. Width/skew are now genuinely per-detector (previously global); `_nuisance_patterns` reads them (and the per-detector IRF path) from the table via `irf_path/width/skew(detector, role)`. Removed the `display_detector`/`irf_paths_by_detector`/`synthetic_irf_*`/`irf_path`/`set_detectors`/`detector_changed` surface from `CalculatorOptionsViewModel` and the Detector/File/Width/Skew rows from `calculator_options.view.json` (only the Pol/AP/IRF toggles remain); project save/load now persists `detector_irf` via `export_state`/`import_state`. 29 filter-calc tests pass; screenshot-verified (loaded-IRF row grays width/skew + shows ✕; Pol splits into ∥/⊥). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Tooltip fix (show + fold) and removal of the redundant FCS "Nuisance" toggle.** (1) The app-wide tooltip filter previously rewrote a widget's tooltip on `QEvent.ToolTip` and passed the event through, which *suppressed* tooltip display. `_TooltipWrapFilter.eventFilter` now displays the folded tooltip itself via `QToolTip.showText(...)` and consumes the event (only when the widget has a tooltip, so parent→child inheritance still works) — tooltips show again and fold to `gui.tooltip.wrap_width`. (2) Removed the FCS Filter Calculator's `🚫 Nuisance` (`nuisance_rejection`) toggle: it was redundant with the AP/IRF toggles (which model afterpulse/scatter) and its only effect — excluding those rows from the correlation-facing output — is now **unconditional** (`reject_nuisance=True` hardwired at all compute sites; `api.py` keeps the param, default already True). The AP/IRF toggles stay; the Inputs row is now Pol / AP / IRF. Dropped the field from `CalculatorOptionsViewModel`, the toggle from `calculator_options.view.json`, and the project save/load persistence; tests updated. Verified: 29 filter-calc tests pass, Inputs shows 3 toggles, `to_channel_filters()` excludes nuisance (2 of 4 rows). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **App-wide auto-folding tooltips, yaml-configurable UI font size, and a settings deep-merge.** Long tooltips rendered as one screen-wide line. New `chisurf/gui/tooltip.py`: `wrap_tooltip()` folds tooltip text to a configurable width, and `install_tooltip_wrapping(app)` (called from `get_app`) installs an application event filter that folds *every* widget tooltip on first hover — so any `setToolTip` in ChiSurf is folded without touching the call site. AutoForm now folds section descriptions through the same helper (previously the form's label column kept the raw unwrapped text). Config: `gui.tooltip.{enabled,wrap_width}` (default 72) in `settings_chisurf.yaml`. Separately, the hardcoded macOS global font point size is now `gui.application_font_size` (default 13; 0 = platform default). **Root-cause fix enabling all this:** `get_chisurf_settings` now deep-merges the packaged default YAML *underneath* the user's settings file, so keys added in newer releases resolve with their defaults on existing installs (the loader previously used the user file verbatim, so new keys were invisible) while any user-set value still wins. Tests: `test/gui/test_tooltip_wrap.py` (5, incl. the deep-merge). See [workflows/build-and-env.md](/workflows/build-and-env.md), [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Shared compact `SetupSelector` widget + FCS Filter Calculator UI polish.** New reusable `chisurf/gui/widgets/setup_selector.py::SetupSelector` — a compact detector-setup chooser (combo + `↻` reload + optional muted detector summary) that emits `setupChanged(str)` and is backed by `load_detector_setups` (injectable loader for tests/RPC). It replaces the copy-pasted `QLabel("Setup:") + QComboBox + reload` pattern the survey found in ~8 sites. Adopted in the FCS Filter Calculator (drops the "Definitions: Detector Def" / "Setup:" labels for a single tight row) and in `burst_fcs_correlator` (removed its hand-rolled combo + `load_detector_setups` plumbing). Remaining hand-rolled sites to migrate: the acquisition-control experiment widgets (`gui/widgets/experiments/{tcspc,pch,rics,pda}` — richer setup+detector+routine controls), `fcs_channel_preset` (the setup *editor*), `burst_bva` (mirror combo), `burst_fcs_correlator/wizard.py` (legacy). Filter Calculator also: the cryptic `⌫ Reject` toggle is now `🚫 Nuisance` with a clearer tooltip (keep AP/scatter filters to absorb nuisance photons but drop those rows from the correlation-facing output); and the Reconstruction/decay plot now overlays each detector's IRF/scatter pattern (cyan dotted, rescaled to the decay peak, near-zero tail masked so the log axis stays sane). Tests: `test/gui/test_setup_selector.py` (3) + the Filter Calculator suite (29) pass. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md), [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).

## 2026-07-17
* **pixi now always uses the freshly-built local tttrlib (fixes missing `SimEngine`).** The Lifetime-FCS Sim crashed with `module 'tttrlib' has no attribute 'SimEngine'` because `pixi run chisurf` used the conda `tttrlib` (bioconda 0.26.2 — its files had also been clobbered by an old 0.0.0 manual install), and `SimEngine` (the PRD-005 photon simulator) is in the *unreleased* 0.27.0 source, 78 commits past the v0.26.2 tag. New `build_tools/build_tttrlib.py` + `build-tttrlib` pixi task build the local tttrlib source *over* the conda package; it is wired into `build-extensions` (and `build-extensions` is now a `depends-on` of the `chisurf` launch task), so pixi always uses the most recent build. It no-ops when the developer-local, gitignored `modules/tttrlib` symlink (→ the tttrlib checkout, added like `modules/imp-tricks`) is absent, so CI/other machines keep the conda package. macOS specifics: links the env `libomp` into the SWIG module (else it loads with a flat-namespace `___kmpc_barrier` error) and builds in an isolated gitignored `build/tttrlib` tree (reusing tttrlib's in-source build dir across flag changes regenerates a stale SWIG wrapper that fails to compile). Verified: clean+idempotent builds install tttrlib 0.27.0 with `SimEngine`, and `simulate_lifetime_fcs` runs in the pixi test env. See [subsystems/compiled-modules.md](/subsystems/compiled-modules.md), [workflows/build-and-env.md](/workflows/build-and-env.md).
* **FCS Correlator workflow: dropped the detector-setup step, leads with Channel Definitions; wired lifetime filters into the Correlator step.** (1) **Reordered/pruned the workflow** — removed the separate `Detector Setup` step; the correlator workflow is now `1. Channel Definitions → 2. Files & Steps → 3. Photon/Burst Filter → 4. Correlator → 5. FCS Merger` and opens on the file-drop step. Step 1 is the FCS channel-definition editor (`FCSChannelWidget`, formerly a standalone Tools entry, now removed from Tools): `FcsCorrelatorTool._refresh_context` derives `setup_name`/`detectors`/`channel_defs` from its selected setup (`build_channels_from_setup`, the same mapping the old detector step exposed) and leaves `tttr_reading` empty so the container type is auto-detected per file. Panel row indices are unchanged (channel-def replaces detector at row 0), so the existing carry-over test still holds. (2) **Lifetime-filter (FLCS) wiring in `4. Correlator`** — `set_lifetime_filters` was previously dead code; added a `lifetime_filter_controls` AutoForm section (`🧬 Load filters… / ✖ Unload`) that loads an fFCS `FilterResult` JSON (`to_channel_filters()`, nuisance excluded) or a `.npy`/`.npz` matrix via `CorrelatorSettingsModel.load_lifetime_filter_file`. In *species mode* the `channel_combos` A/B selectors list filter species (labels from metadata or `Species N`) and set `_species_a`/`_species_b`; `_correlate_lifetime_filtered` now correlates only the selected pair (ACF when A=B, CCF when A≠B) by subsetting the filter rows (`_subset_species`, handles 2-D/3-D/`{channel: 2-D}`). Unload restores detector-channel correlation. Tests: `fcs_correlator/test/test_widgets.py` +3 (workflow leads with channel-def & opens on files; filter load→species→unload; combos switch to species). 10 FCS-umbrella GUI tests + 90 FCS-suite tests pass (arm64, offscreen), both changes screenshot-verified. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Merged the FCS Correlator and FCS Tools plugins into a single `FCS` plugin.** The FCS submenu previously exposed two overlapping entries — *Correlator* (`fcs_correlator`, the detector→files→filter→correlate→merge workflow) and *FCS Tools* (`fcs_toolbox`, a rail of standalone tools). They are now one menu entry, **`FCS`** (`fcs_toolbox`, display `Spectroscopy:Fluorescence Correlation Spectroscopy:FCS`): the correlator steps come first under a `Correlator` group header, then a `Tools` separator groups the optional, independent tools (2D-FLCS, Lifetime-FCS Sim, Burst-wise FCS, diffusion/volume calculator, fFCS filter calculator, correlation-channel presets). Implementation: `FcsCorrelatorTool.__init__` now accepts a custom `panels`/`title`/`initial_role` (defaults preserve the standalone workflow and its tests); the merged `FcsTool` (in `fcs_toolbox/tool.py`, `FcsToolboxTool` kept as an alias) subclasses it and passes `CORRELATOR_PANELS` + `Tools` separator + the tool panels, so the correlator's inter-panel context wiring (which keys off panel `role`s) is reused unchanged and the extra tool roles are ignored by it. `fcs_correlator` is now `menu_hidden` (a reusable building block, not its own entry); the redundant standalone Detector-Def and FCS-Merger tool panels were dropped since the workflow already owns those steps. Tests: rewrote `fcs_toolbox/test/test_widgets.py` (merged nav = correlator + `Tools` sep + tools, opens on Files & Steps, lazy tool load, `fcs_correlator` now menu-hidden); `fcs_correlator/test/test_widgets.py` unchanged (standalone workflow still valid). 4 GUI tests pass (arm64, offscreen). See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).

## 2026-07-16
* **CLSM Generator plugin + map-based photon simulator.** New AutoForm plugin `chisurf/plugins/microscopy/clsm_generator` (**Imaging:Simulate:CLSM Generator**) turning an intensity image + one lifetime map per detector (TIFF/`.npy`/`.npz`) into a synthetic CLSM photon image whose per-pixel intensity and lifetime match the inputs — ground-truth test data. Core `core/fluorescence/imaging/simulate.py::simulate_clsm_from_maps` places per-pixel emitters (lifetime×intensity quantised into `SimSpecies`, one set per detector channel) and raster-scans them; `load_image_map` reads tif/npy/npz. GUI: Qt-free `ClsmGeneratorViewModel` + `generator.view.json` + threaded `tool.py` (file picker + `path_list` inputs, `🧪 Generate`, shared `image_browser` over input maps + reconstruction, `💾 Save` photon stream `.npz`/`.ptu`/`.spc`/`.ht3` + intensity TIFF). Verified recon-intensity corr≈0.95, longer-τ photons arrive later; tests `test/fluorescence/test_imaging_simulate.py` + `clsm_generator/test/test_widgets.py` (11). See [plugins/imaging.md](/plugins/imaging.md).
* **Molecule-wise MLE tool: synthetic generator + browse/preview/reload/calibration/export/batch.** Reusable Qt-free `simulate_clsm_molecules(molecules,…)→SimulatedImage` (tttrlib `SimEngine`/`SimScanner`; replaces the inline test helper). Five `sm_image_mle` enhancements: reopen a saved analysis from disk (`run` saves `intensity.npy`; `load_analysis`/`results_tsv`), live segmentation preview (`preview_segmentation`), imaging-pipeline hooks (`apply_pipeline_context`/`apply_calibration`), per-molecule decay+fit plot, and result export — Preview/Run/Export + Open-results in `molecule_mle.view.json`. Core `fit_molecules` batch-fits via `Fit2x.fit_many` when `keep_curves=False` (bit-identical; landed with the vv_vh rename). Tests: `test_imaging_simulate.py` (6) + `sm_image_mle` core/widget (14). See [plugins/imaging.md](/plugins/imaging.md).
* **Calibration: per-pixel / FLIM application (continuation 4).** Closed the last-remaining "later phase" for the FRET-calibration work — a per-pixel imaging path. New Qt-free `chisurf/core/fluorescence/fret/pixel.py`: `corrected_es_image(intensity, excitation, emission, *, unmix, ridge, pairs, min_counts)` applies the general crosstalk-matrix correction to an `(n_laser, n_detector, H, W)` FLIM/PIE/ALEX image tensor → per-pixel accurate FRET-efficiency maps, masking photon-starved pixels (`total < min_counts` → `NaN`); `pixel_source_photons(counts, emission, *, unmix="shuffle"|"stable", seed)` returns integer, Poisson-preserving (or continuous NNLS) per-source photon images. Both are thin wrappers over the array-safe burst cores (`corrected_es_general`, `photon_shuffle_unmix`, `invert_mixing`), which already broadcast over trailing axes — so no new correction maths, just imaging ergonomics + masking. Headless test `test/fitting/test_pixel_fret.py` (4): synthetic two-colour FLIM E-gradient recovered exactly, `min_counts` masks a zeroed border while the rest stays exact, stable un-mixing stays in-range on a noisy ill-conditioned 3-colour image where naive spills out of [0,1], and per-pixel shuffle is integer + per-pixel count-preserving. 63 calibration/crosstalk tests pass, ruff clean (arm64). Docs `docs/FRET calibration.md` gains a "Per-pixel / FLIM imaging" section; OKF [references/fret-calibration.md](/references/fret-calibration.md) moves per-pixel/FLIM from "missing" to a done bullet (only the one-click ndx toolbar button remains as a pure-Qt convenience).
* **Pixel-wise FLIM MLE GUI converted to AutoForm; the `imgmle.py` monolith retired.** Replaced the ~1900-line `LifetimeMleAnalysisWizard(QMainWindow)` (loading `imgmle.ui`) with the proven AutoForm pattern (mirrors `sm_image_mle`): a Qt-free view-model `chisurf/plugins/microscopy/img_pixel_mle/gui/view_model.py` (`PixelMleViewModel`), an authored view `gui/pixel_mle.view.json`, and a thin `gui/tool.py` (`ImgPixelMleTool` is now a plain `QWidget` hosting `AutoForm`, not a `LifetimeMleAnalysisWizard` subclass; the class name is unchanged so `manifest.entrypoints.gui` still resolves). `imgmle.py` + `imgmle.ui` deleted; `__init__.py` gained a lazy-Qt `__getattr__` gate + a `run()` that opens the AutoForm tool; the `api/pixel_mle.py` header no longer references the wizard. The view-model holds the transport-agnostic `api.models.PixelMleSettings` (the same dataclass the RPC backend + CLI consume) and, in a background-thread `run()`, builds the prepared VV/VH IRF via `backend.services._build_irf_vv_vh`, assembles a per-file core `PixelMleSettings` exactly as the backend does, fits through the Qt-free `core.pixel_mle`, writes `<stem>_pixel_mle.csv`, and displays the fitted τ map through the shared `image` AutoForm section (`ImageMapWidget`: inferno colormap, per-file in-plot channel selector, frame z-slider). **Added the `engine` (auto/fast/loop) + `n_workers` thread controls** to the GUI *and* to `api.models.PixelMleSettings`, threaded through `backend.services._handle_analyze` so the RPC/CLI honour them too. Kept the Imaging-Tools aggregator hooks (`apply_setup_settings`/`apply_pipeline_context`/`apply_calibration`). Fixed a TTTR object-lifetime bug in `_period_ns` (`tttrlib.TTTR(path).header` dangles once the temporary TTTR is collected → C++ `nlohmann::json` abort; keep the TTTR referenced). Headless end-to-end verified on `tttr-data/imaging/pq/Microtime200_HH400/beads.ptu` (418/2500 px fit, τ map + CSV, screenshot-inspected). Tests: new `test/test_widgets.py` (5: Qt-free bindings, `can_run` guards, `apply_calibration` carry-over, view-spec load, tool build); `test/test_pixel_mle_core.py` unchanged (6). 11 pass, ruff clean (arm64). See [plugins/imaging.md](/plugins/imaging.md), [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md).
* **Renamed the historic "jordi" format to descriptive `vv_vh`.** The stacked polarization-resolved decay format (parallel/VV + perpendicular/VH, optional magic-angle/VM channels concatenated into one column) is no longer called "jordi" anywhere in code. Repo-wide rename: module `chisurf/core/fio/jordi.py` → `vv_vh.py`; API `read_jordi`/`write_jordi` → `read_vv_vh`/`write_vv_vh`; `assemble_jordi` → `assemble_vv_vh`; reader flag `is_jordi` → `is_vv_vh`; plugins `jordi_g_factor` → `vv_vh_g_factor` and `jordi_anisotropy` → `vv_vh_anisotropy` (dirs, class names `VvVh*`, RPC namespace `vv_vh_g_factor.*`, CLI `vv-vh-g-factor`, `state_namespace`, display names → "VV/VH …"); plus tests, docs (`docs/manual/vv_vh_format.rst`), settings anchors (`tcspc-vv_vh`), and the example notebook. Raw sample-data dirs under `test/data/tcspc/Jordi*` intentionally keep their provenance names. The historic "jordi" term is preserved for discoverability only in the new OKF concept [references/vv-vh-decay-format.md](/references/vv-vh-decay-format.md). No logic changed — purely nominal. See [subsystems/data-io.md](/subsystems/data-io.md).
* **Fixed pre-existing failures surfaced during the VV/VH rename.** (1) `vv_vh_g_factor` backend `archive_g_factor_handler` never bound the resolved MMFDB into the execution context, so every `register_*` call fell through to "no MMFDB available" and archival failed silently — it now opens the DB once and wraps the raw/derived/calibration registrations in `database_context(db)`. (2) Registering an ASCII VV/VH decay hit the closed `data_format` vocabulary (`.txt`/`.dat` unseeded) — fixed at the root in the mmfdb support lib (committed there): `data_format` is derived from arbitrary file suffixes, so `register_artifact` now `ensure_extensible_vocab`s it (records new open-ended formats instead of rejecting; controlled vocabs like `artifact_kind` stay strict). (3) `vv_vh_anisotropy` built its window `QIcon` at module top-level, aborting headless test collection — deferred to a cached `_plugin_icon()`. (4) `test/tcspc/test_tcspc_reader.py` pointed at a stale `chisurf/fio/...` path (missing `core/`). All previously-failing reader/provenance/widget tests now pass (38 green in the affected suites).
* **Burst IRF & Background GUI + burst-MLE handoff.** New AutoForm + JSON plugin `chisurf/plugins/burst/burst_irf_bg/` (`IrfBackgroundViewModel` Qt-free view-model, `gui/irf_bg.view.json`, custom `irf_bg_channels`/`irf_bg_run`/`irf_bg_results` sections; mirrors `tttr_count_rate_analysis`) exposing the non-burst IRF/background extraction: shared channel-definition page + drop file list + burst-search/dark-count-floor/micro-time-binning parameters → per-detector IRF plot + results table (screenshot-verified). Added core helper `extract_mle_irf_background` (in `irf_bg.py`) that builds the vv_vh-stacked `[parallel, perpendicular]` (`chs[::2]`/`chs[1::2]`) micro-time IRF+background patterns the burst MLE consumes (background = raw non-burst histogram, IRF = floor-subtracted). Wired into the `burst_analysis` navigation shell as the "IRF & Background" step (inherits channel settings + raw files via the workflow context); its "Send to MLE" action calls the new shell method `apply_irf_background_to_mle`, which writes each detector's `irf_np`/`bg_np` directly and refreshes the decay/fit without reloading files — so non-burst scatter/background becomes the MLE-Lifetime IRF/background with no separate acquisition. Tests: `chisurf/plugins/burst/burst_irf_bg/test/test_widgets.py` (3) + `test/plugins/burst/test_irf_bg_workflow.py` (4). See [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **IRF + background from the non-burst photons of a single-molecule measurement.** New Qt-free core `chisurf/core/fluorescence/burst/irf_bg.py` (`extract_irf_background`, `non_burst_mask`, `DetectorIrfBackground`, re-exported from `chisurf.core.fluorescence.burst`). A confocal sm measurement is mostly empty focus, so the photons a sliding-window burst search *rejects* are the built-in scatter/background: `extract_irf_background` inverts the burst search and, per detector, (a) fits the non-burst interphoton-time tail for the background rate (kHz, reusing `background.estimate_background_from_interphoton_times`) and (b) histograms the non-burst micro-times over the full laser period, subtracts a robust flat dark-count floor (low quantile) and normalises the remainder to a scatter-derived IRF. IRF is per detector (each colour has its own response; PIE micro-time gating is applied to the background but *not* the IRF, which would clip the prompt). Removes the need for a separate scatter/buffer acquisition. Integrated into the guided facade `burst_analysis/api/workflow.py`: new `IrfBackground` result (`.background_khz`, `.table`, `.irf(name)`, `.plot()`), a `Bursts.irf_background()` method (reuses the selection's burst-search params, pools non-burst photons across all loaded files — `Bursts` now carries `_search`), and a one-line `BurstWorkflow.estimate_irf_background(dataset, setup=…)`. Tests: `test/plugins/burst/test_irf_background.py` (6, on real BH-SPC sm-DNA data + demo-MMFDB workflow step). Example: `examples/burst_irf_background.py`. See [subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md](/subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md), [plugins/burst.md](/plugins/burst.md).
* **Lifetime-FCS (FLCS) simulator: end-to-end simulate → filter → correlate, wired into the FCS toolbox.** Added a Qt-free lifetime-FCS simulation core `chisurf/core/fluorescence/fcs/simulate.py` (`simulate_lifetime_fcs`) that drives the photon simulator to generate a confocal stream of several **diffusing** species with distinct fluorescence lifetimes and an optional symmetric interconversion rate matrix. It reconstructs the absolute macro-time from the engine window index + within-window arrival offset and reads native micro-times (a simpler/faster path than the `to_tttr` SPC round-trip, keeping the decay on the exact simulated TAC axis; `to_tttr` is itself faithful and gives equally well-conditioned filters, cond ~3.3). Feeding `filtered.py::calc_ffcs_filters` + `species_filtered_correlation` recovers the physics: static species separate by diffusion time, interconverting states reveal a cross-correlation exchange peak. New AutoForm GUI tool `chisurf/plugins/fcs/fcs_lfcs_sim/` (`LifetimeFcsSimWidget`, `lfcs_sim.view.json`, `manifest.json`, experimental) — a parameter form + species-correlation plot with a "Simulate + Correlate" button — registered as a panel in the `fcs_toolbox` meta-tool. Worked examples: `examples/lifetime_fcs.py` (chisurf) and `examples/correlation/plot_lifetime_fcs.py` (photon-simulator gallery). Tests: `test/fitting/test_lifetime_fcs_sim.py` (4: labelled stream, well-conditioned filters, static diffusion-time separation, interconversion cross-peak) + `fcs_lfcs_sim/test/test_widgets.py` (3, headless, screenshot-verified) — 17 pass with the existing FLCS suites. See [plugins/fcs.md](/plugins/fcs.md).
* **Reusable `image_browser` AutoForm section + adopted by the molecule-MLE tool and the TTTR image browser.** Factored the "pick one of many, show its image" workspace shape into a shared custom section `chisurf/gui/autoform/sections/image_browser_section.py` (`ImageBrowserWidget`): a left entry list (from an `entries_source`; each entry a string or `{id,label,badge,rating}`) with an optional text filter, editable star-rating row (`rating_call`), read-only info panel (`info_source`) and editable per-entry note (`note_source`/`note_call`), plus optional drag-drop (`on_drop`) and single/multi-select — beside the existing `image` canvas (`ImageMapWidget`), which it composes so browsers inherit colormap/z-slider/movie/markers/ROI/pixel-pick for free. Also extended `ImageMapWidget` with `labels_source` (on-image text overlays, e.g. mosaic tile names). Registered under key `image_browser`; the section `target` is the current entry's image and selection routes through one `_commit` that fires `select_call` only on a real change. **Adopted in two tools.** (1) `sm_image_mle` GUI now browses molecules with it (list of `Mol N` + τ badge, canvas marks the selected molecule via `current_molecule_marker`, info panel shows the Fit23 result) — replacing the earlier single image + table. (2) **Refactored `tttr_image_browser` onto AutoForm**: its ~825-line hand-built `QWidget` (file `QTableWidget` with star cells + pyqtgraph mosaic + annotation) became a Qt-free `gui/view_model.py::ImageBrowserViewModel` + `gui/browser.view.json` driving the shared section — file list with size + ★ badges, the per-file intensity mosaic as the canvas with per-tile channel/micro-time labels (`labels_source`), editable annotations (`note_call`→metadata.json), a rating-filter `choice`, and folder drag-drop — over the *unchanged* Qt-free core + RPC client; the toolbar shell (Open/Clear/Caches/Export/TIFF/DOCX/Next), detector-setup page and coordinator/pipeline handoff are preserved (selection now flows via a view-model observer instead of the old table signal). Tests: `test/gui/test_autoform_image_browser.py` (5: populate/select, canvas update, filter, rating write-back, refresh), rewritten `sm_image_mle`/`tttr_image_browser` widget tests — 21 pass, ruff clean (arm64), both tools headless-screenshot-verified. See [subsystems/gui-autoform.md](/subsystems/gui-autoform.md), [plugins/imaging.md](/plugins/imaging.md), [plugins/tttr.md](/plugins/tttr.md).
* **Pixel-wise FLIM MLE: C++ batch fit + threaded dispatch makes fitting scale (in-process, ~4×).** Superseded the earlier fork-multiprocessing approach after finding the real bottleneck. Per-fit threading did not scale (flat 1.0× on 2–8 threads) even with the GIL released around `DecayFit23::fit`: each fit releases the GIL for <1 ms, and the **per-fit GIL-handoff convoy** serialises the threads. Ruled out the macOS nano allocator (`MallocNanoZone=0` unchanged) and `thread_local`/TLS (plain `static` unchanged); a BLAS `np.dot` control thread-scales 4× in the same process, so it was the fit, not the environment. Fix: a new tttrlib batch entry point **`DecayFit23::fit_matrix`** (committed in tttrlib `1650cf67`) fits a whole `(n_rows × 2·n_ch)` matrix in one C++ call with the GIL released for the *entire* loop; rebuild the arm64 editable install per [[tttrlib-editable-rebuild]] (note the SWIG-regen gotcha recorded there — `touch ext/python/tttrlib.i` after editing an included `.i`). ChiSurf side: `Fit2x.fit_many` wraps it, and `chisurf/core/fluorescence/mle/parallel.py::fit_matrix_threaded` splits rows across a `ThreadPoolExecutor` (each thread its own `Fit2x`) — **no multiprocessing**, so no fork/spawn fragility and safe inside the Qt GUI. `img_pixel_mle/core/pixel_mle.py` now dispatches through it (`n_workers` control, `_MIN_ROWS_FOR_THREADS` gate). Measured: batch threading 2t 1.95× / 4t 3.74× / 8t 4.63×, bit-identical to single-fit; end-to-end A/B on `beads.ptu` baseline→+fast 1.15×→+threaded(7w) **4.08×**, results identical. Tests updated (`test_threaded_matches_serial`); benchmark `test/benchmarks/benchmark_pixel_mle.py`. Replaces the fork-MP `fit_matrix_parallel`. See [plugins/imaging.md](/plugins/imaging.md) and the `fit2x` harness in [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **fit2x fit itself ~5× faster (Brent 1-D + objective hoist), bit-close.** Two tttrlib changes to the fit23 estimator (commits `1650cf67`/`f133e231`/`96924906` there). (1) **Objective hoist** — `targetf` recomputed the data-only signal integrals (`Sp/Ss/B`) on every objective evaluation, but they are constant during a fit (only the soft-BIFL `Bexpected` depends on parameters); recompute only when soft-BIFL is active → ~10% faster, **max\|Δτ\|=0** over 120 real decays vs the unmodified build. (2) **Brent 1-D minimiser** — when only τ is free (γ/r0/ρ fixed: the pixel-wise FLIM / burst-lifetime case) the fit is 1-D, so a bounded derivative-free Brent minimiser replaces the general BFGS-with-numerical-gradients engine (~5× fewer objective evaluations). Verified on real FLIM decays: same MLE minimum to 6 sig-figs (max\|Δτ\|=5e-5, rel 6e-6 — far below lifetime measurement precision), **0.88 → 0.18 ms/fit (~4.9×)**; BFGS unchanged for all other free-parameter combinations. (3) **NEON 2-channel convolution** (commit `eee0eebf`) — the fit23 model convolves parallel/perpendicular channels that share lifetimes; the per-channel recurrence is latency-bound and un-vectorisable *within* a channel, but the two channels run together in `float64x2` lanes (`fconv_per_cs_2ch`, scalar two-call fallback off-ARM) → another **~1.5×** (0.18 → 0.12 ms), numerically identical (median τ matches the scalar path to full precision). So the fit itself is **~7.3×** (0.88 → 0.12 ms) and, with the batch/threaded dispatch, **≈33× throughput** on the pixel-MLE fit vs the original per-fit BFGS+scalar-convolution path. Tried but dropped: `-ffast-math -mcpu=native` gave only ~6% (the recurrence dominates and can't reassociate) — not worth global FP-semantics changes. float32 would help little here (working set is L1-resident and the 2 channels already fill the NEON register), so not pursued.
* **IRF scatter is now fitted per detector and the first-run example uses the full forward model.** The Filter Calculator's AutoForm selects an independent measured IRF for each detector. Missing entries invoke the shared TCSPC synthetic-IRF path: prompt position and FWHM are searched against that detector's mixed decay while molecular, constant-afterpulse, and IRF-scatter amplitudes are constrained non-negative; parallel/perpendicular decays receive separate fitted patterns. The preloaded fast/slow patterns are now convolved with detector-distinct synthetic IRFs, and each 100,000-photon detector mixture includes both IRF scatter and constant afterpulse counts. Per-detector IRF paths, synthetic starting parameters, and inline example decays persist in projects. Verification: 33 focused tests pass, including synthetic-IRF recovery and distinct detector patterns/mixtures.
* **FCS Filter Calculator gained explicit afterpulse and IRF-scatter nuisance rejection.** Added shared Qt-free nuisance bases in `chisurf.core.fluorescence.decay`: a normalized constant microtime pattern for afterpulsing/dark counts and a validated normalized IRF pattern for scattered excitation. Filter calculation and non-negative unmixing now include enabled nuisance components, report their fitted counts independently, draw stable diagnostic filter colors, and preserve them in projects/results. Correlation-facing exports slice nuisance rows when rejection is enabled, so nuisance photons are absorbed without becoming molecular correlation species. AutoForm exposes compact `▤ Afterpulse`, `⚡ Scatter`, and `⌫ Nuisance` controls plus the scatter-IRF file field. Verification: 31 focused tests pass, including exact species/nuisance recovery, serialization, and GUI species-only export coverage.
* **FCS Filter Calculator residuals now sit directly above the decay.** Reordered the default ChiSurf dock stack to Lifetime filters → Weighted residuals → Reconstruction/decay and versioned its persistence key so previously saved layouts do not mask the corrected first-run arrangement. The panels remain user-rearrangeable. Added a dock-order regression and reran visual verification.
* **FCS Filter Calculator controls are compact, AutoForm-backed, and color-stable.** Shortened the visible toolbar actions to emoji-led `📂 Mixed…`, `➕ Add`, `➖ Remove`, `🧩 Unmix`, and `💾 Project` tool buttons while retaining complete explanatory tooltips and descriptive popup-menu items. Shortened the detector panel/dock copy (`Setup`, `Inputs`, `Detectors`, `Mixed decay`, `Components`) and moved polarization/background toggles into a Qt-free `CalculatorOptionsViewModel` rendered by AutoForm. Replaced index-dependent pyqtgraph colors with identity-stable colors: semantic green/red/yellow/etc. detector colors plus a deterministic digest palette for arbitrary detector and component names; total/reconstruction pairs share color and differ by line style. Verification: 28 focused tests pass, including a survivor-color toggle regression; critical Ruff and compileall pass; visual verdict 97/100.
* **Synthetic decays gained central, reproducible Poisson shot noise.** Added `chisurf.core.fluorescence.decay.sample_decay_shot_noise` as the shared finite-photon observation step over an expected detector decay. `synthetic_decay` and persisted synthetic/FRET component definitions now accept a photon budget and seed; fit-derived detector patterns use the same sampler with detector-specific seed offsets when noise is requested. The Filter Calculator's first-run 70/30 mixture is now a reproducible 100,000-photon observation, producing realistic filter, reconstruction, and weighted-residual traces while retaining ideal component bases. Its AutoForm editor exposes a Poisson toggle, photon count, and seed. Verification: 27 focused tests pass, critical Ruff and compileall pass, visual verdict 96/100.
* **FCS Filter Calculator now uses AutoForm tables, ChiSurf docks, and a useful first-run state.** Extended the shared AutoForm `TableSection` with opt-in editable-cell dispatch (`editable` + `update_call`) and moved the synthetic amplitude/lifetime spectrum editor to a Qt-free view-model plus authored `synthetic_editor.view.json`; add/remove/import controls are AutoForm tool buttons. Replaced the calculator's fixed `QTabWidget`/splitter/sidebar buttons with one persistent ChiSurf `DockArea` (decay sources, detector setup, lifetime filters, reconstruction, weighted residuals) and a compact toolbar/menu surface for measured input, components, unmixing, projects, and export. The GUI starts with a replaceable 70/30 mixture of 1.2 ns and 4.0 ns components and immediately computes detector-aware filters. Focused verification: 20 FCS tests plus shared AutoForm editable-table coverage pass; compileall clean; visual verdict recorded separately.
* **Central intensity/decay computation is now an explicit design objective; FCS Filter Calculator begins the convergence.** Documented the north-star in [Fluorescence Domain](/subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md): detector-resolved intensities, TCSPC decays, and reusable patterns should have one Qt-free computation path owning IRF, polarization, pile-up, background/scatter, detector response, excitation/emission mixing, and crosstalk; fits, simulators, FCS/FLCS, imaging, and exporters consume it instead of duplicating forward models. Added `chisurf/core/fluorescence/decay.py` as an incremental adapter over the established fit/model stack (`lifetime_spectrum_from_model`, `compute_model_decay`, `compute_detector_patterns_from_fit`). The FCS Filter Calculator now edits component lifetime spectra in an amplitude/lifetime table, reads effective spectra from existing lifetime/FRET/distance-distribution fits, asks every grouped detector model to recompute its corrected pattern, snapshots detector/routing patterns for project persistence, and feeds them into non-negative unmixing, lifetime filters, and existing correlation weights. Plugin-local ideal/distribution generation remains a headless fallback, reusing ChiSurf's existing FRET distance/rate functions. Focused verification: 18 tests pass; critical Ruff checks, compileall, diff checks, and table-dialog visual verdict pass.
* **Calibration: general excitation/emission crosstalk-matrix correction (Hellenkamp is the 2-colour special case) (continuation 3).** Per the steer that the scalar α/β/γ/δ are only a special case and the general excitation/emission crosstalk matrices must still work, added the general matrix correction. New `es.py::corrected_es_general(intensity, excitation, emission)` corrects straight from the two light-path matrices — **excitation** `X[l,k]` (laser→chromophore) and **emission** `D[k,m]` (chromophore→detector) — in three steps: (1) un-mix emission `e[l,:] = I[l,:] @ D⁻¹` (removes *all* spectral crosstalk incl. acceptor↔acceptor bleed, and folds the detection/QY ratio into the `D` diagonal so **no explicit γ** appears), (2) subtract direct excitation `F[l,k] = e[l,k] − (X[l,k]/X[k,k])·e[k,k]`, (3) coupled donor budget `E[l,k] = F[l,k]/(e[l,l] + Σ_a F[l,a])`. It reduces **algebraically** to `corrected_es` for two colours (`D=[[1,α],[0,γ]]`, `X=[[1,δ],[0,1]]`) and recovers exact E for ≥3 chromophores with inter-acceptor leakage the scalar path cannot express. `calibration.py::crosstalk_matrices_from_lightpath` builds `X`,`D` from a `get_crosstalk_matrices()` payload (folding QY/detection efficiency into `D`), and `general_correction_from_lightpath` wraps the whole path. This resolves the previously-deferred "multi-acceptor cross-leakage" item; `corrected_es_matrix` stays as the scalar fast path when there is no inter-acceptor bleed. Tests: `test/fitting/test_general_correction.py` (8: 2-colour reduction == `corrected_es`, 3-colour with acceptor↔acceptor bleed exact, scalar-path-is-biased contrast, light-path-payload path) — 43 calibration/crosstalk tests pass, ruff clean (arm64). Docs: `docs/FRET calibration.md` gains a "The general case: crosstalk matrices" section (scalar factors reframed as the two-colour reduction); OKF [references/fret-calibration.md](/references/fret-calibration.md) updated (general-form bullet; cross-leakage moved from "missing" to done). **Stable un-mixing follow-up:** the emission un-mix (step 1, `I[l,:] @ D⁻¹`) is the numerically delicate part — for strong spectral overlap `D` is ill-conditioned, so the plain pseudo-inverse amplifies shot noise and returns unphysical **negative** emissions. Added a selectable `unmix` argument to `corrected_es_general`/`general_correction_from_lightpath`: `"naive"` (fast vectorized pinv, default) vs `"stable"` (**non-negative least squares** per burst — enforces `e≥0`, robust to overlap), plus an optional `ridge` (Tikhonov) damping usable with either. Both share `crosstalk.invert_mixing`, which was extended with a `ridge` Tikhonov parameter (closed form for the linear solve, augmented-system for NNLS) — also available to the phasor-FLIM unmix. Method choice follows the standard fluorescence-microscopy unmixing literature (NNLS/fully-constrained LU for positivity; Tikhonov to fix ill-conditioning). Tests grew to 13 (naive==stable on clean data; NNLS non-negative where pinv goes negative on ill-conditioned noisy data; ridge lowers burst-to-burst E variance; invalid-method guard) — 48 calibration/crosstalk tests pass, ruff clean (arm64). Docs gain a "Naive vs. stable un-mixing" subsection. **Photon-statistics + ndx follow-up:** (checked the user-supplied `nishio-laboratory/lambda_split` repo — it is an unrelated LLM/diffusion inference-splitting project, not the fluorescence "λSplit" spectral-unmixing paper; removed from junk.) (1) **Integer, statistics-preserving un-mixing** `crosstalk.photon_shuffle_unmix(counts, matrix, *, abundances, seed)` — reassigns each detected photon to a source by a multinomial draw (`P(k|m) ∝ a_k·B[k,m]`, `B`=row-normalised emission spectral shape, `a_k`=NNLS abundance prior), giving a non-negative **integer** per-source photon stream that preserves the total count exactly and the Poisson shot-noise (multinomial thinning of Poisson is Poisson) — for BVA/MLE/burst-variance downstream; expectation == the continuous Richardson–Lucy/EM unmix (unbiased). Vectorised over bursts by a binomial chain. `test_photon_shuffle.py` (5). (2) **ndx stable/shuffle injection** — ndx corrects FRET with scalar string-equations and exposes **no compute hook** (confirmed by exploring the in-tree `modules/ndxplorer` submodule), so `push_unmixed_columns_to_ndx(ndx, *, emission, channel_columns, source_labels, unmix="stable"|"shuffle", seed, rate_columns)` reads the measured per-channel count columns, un-mixes them (NNLS default / integer shuffle) with the light-path emission matrix, and injects leakage-free per-source photon+rate columns back as **new** columns (native ndx columns/equations untouched → no double-correction). Per user steer, **no hard-coded detector names** — caller supplies the emission matrix + column names + source labels (from `crosstalk_matrices_from_lightpath`), general to any N-colour setup. `test_calibration_ndx_bridge.py` grew +3. Total 56 calibration/crosstalk tests pass, ruff clean (arm64). Docs gain "Preserving photon-counting statistics" + "ndxplorer: stable / shuffle un-mixing" sections; OKF [references/fret-calibration.md](/references/fret-calibration.md) updated. **Headless real-engine ndx test (per steer "make it possible to drive tests headless"):** the stub-only ndx coverage is now complemented by `test/fio/test_ndxplorer_unmix_headless.py`, which drives ndx's **actual** `DataSource` + real `mfd.equations.yaml`/`mfd.constants.json` with **no Qt window / no display** — it self-adds the in-tree `modules/ndxplorer` submodule to `sys.path`, sets `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen`, `importorskip`s when absent (mirrors the existing `test/fio/test_ndxplorer_rpc_bridge.py` pattern), and asserts (a) a pushed `gamma` genuinely changes the engine-computed `FRET efficiency` column, (b) injected unmixed columns are computable by ndx's own equation engine, (c) shuffle columns are integer + count-preserving through the real `DataSource`. 3 headless tests pass (arm64, offscreen).
* **Pixel-wise FLIM MLE throughput: vectorised extraction + multiprocessing fits + GIL release (A/B benchmarked).** Two optimisations to `img_pixel_mle/core/pixel_mle.py`, both A/B-verified to give **identical** per-pixel lifetimes. (1) **Vectorised extraction** — replaced the per-pixel Python `tttr_indices`+`np.bincount` loop with one `CLSMImage.get_fluorescence_decay` C++ call building the whole per-pixel histogram stack (matches bincount exactly; the returned uint8 is guarded — saturation auto-falls-back to the exact bincount engine). An `engine` switch (`"auto"`/`"fast"`/`"loop"`) selects it. (2) **Multiprocessing fits** — the per-pixel `Fit23` calls (the dominant cost) now run across worker processes over a shared-memory copy of the histogram matrix, via a new reusable, Qt-free helper `chisurf/core/fluorescence/mle/parallel.py::fit_matrix_parallel` (built on the `fit2x` harness). It uses the **fork** start method so workers inherit the parent's imports — avoiding a hang where `spawn` re-imports the deep plugin/GUI package chain in a subprocess — with a serial fallback on any failure and an `n_workers` control. **Investigation & tttrlib change:** per the "let it release GIL" steer, added GIL release around `DecayFit{23,24,25,26}::fit` in tttrlib (`ext/python/DecayFit*.i`, committed there `bebd7bb9`; rebuild the arm64 editable install per [[tttrlib-editable-rebuild]]). Threaded fits are now **bit-identical** to serial (the fit's per-call state is `static thread_local`), but in-process threading still does **not scale** on macOS (the fit serialises on an internal per-thread-state access path; a BLAS `np.dot` control thread-scales 4× in the same env, so it is the fit, not the environment) — hence multiprocessing for throughput. The GIL release is kept as the correct, safe precondition (helps latency/GUI-responsiveness; enables future threading if the C++ serialiser is removed). **A/B benchmark** `test/benchmarks/benchmark_pixel_mle.py` on real FLIM `tttr-data/imaging/pq/Microtime200_HH400/beads.ptu`: baseline (loop, serial) → +fast extraction 1.13× → +parallel (7 workers) **2.58×**, results identical. Tests: `img_pixel_mle/test/test_pixel_mle_core.py` gains fast-vs-loop and MP-vs-serial equivalence (6 pass); benchmark has a `slow` equivalence+speedup test. See [plugins/imaging.md](/plugins/imaging.md) and the `fit2x` harness in [subsystems/fitting.md](/subsystems/fitting.md).
* **ALEX analysis of Shimon Weiss lab `.sm` files: auto window-split + end-to-end test/example (chisurf and tttrlib).** Shimon Weiss lab single-molecule `.sm` files (tttrlib `SM` container, record type 11) store only a macro-time and a routing channel — no TCSPC micro-time. Micro-second ALEX encodes the green/red laser alternation in the macro-time clock, so `TTTR.alex_to_microtime(period, shift)` folds it into a synthetic micro-time (`micro = (macro − shift) % period`) that recovers the excitation window by gating, exactly like PIE. Added **automatic green/red window detection** to the ALEX plugin core (`chisurf/plugins/tttr/ptu_alex_creator/core`): `auto_alex_windows(...)` histograms the folded phase, finds the two laser-on plateaus as the two largest occupied runs (circular `_contiguous_runs`), **guard-bands each edge** (default 6%) to drop the laser rise/fall photons (some photon loss is intended — ~88% retention), and labels the donor-brighter window "green"; `alex_stream_masks(...)` returns the DD/DA/AA/AD boolean masks. It raises `ValueError` on continuous-wave data (single fully-occupied period). Verified the full smFRET path works with `.sm` input: load → `apply_alex` → auto-split → all-photon burst search → per-burst DD/DA/AA counting → apparent/corrected E and S via [`burst/es.py`](/subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md) (`apparent_es`/`corrected_es`). Because the shipped reference `tttr-data/sm/data.sm` is a continuous-wave, donor-dominated file (60 s, ch0≫ch1, no alternation — confirmed by FFT/fold-contrast analysis showing no laser-alternation frequency), tests use a **synthetic ground-truth ALEX stream with realistic laser windows (interior plateaus + rise/fall gaps + edge smear) round-tripped through the `.sm` container** (set `MeasDesc_GlobalResolution` tag so the written file reads back at 12.5 ns) to check window recovery + E/S recovery, and additionally load the real file to prove reading + burst search. Auto-split recovers windows inside the true laser bounds and two injected populations (600 bursts, E≈0.20/0.80, S≈0.55). New: `test/fitting/test_alex_sm.py` (7), `examples/alex_sm_es.py`; and in tttrlib `test/python/burstfilter/test_alex_sm.py` (7, with a standalone copy of the auto-split helper) + `examples/single_molecule/plot_alex_sm.py` (sphinx-gallery, phase histogram with detected windows + E-S 2D histogram). Only new core code is the `auto_alex_windows`/`alex_stream_masks` helpers; the ALEX/.sm folding + es capability already existed.
* **Molecule-wise MLE (`sm_image_mle`): reworked onto a Qt-free core + AutoForm GUI, verified against tttrlib-simulated CLSM images.** The plugin's real algorithm (segment single molecules from a CLSM intensity image, build a per-molecule VV/VH histogram, fit each by Fit23 Poisson MLE) lived in a ~790-line monolithic `click` script `sm_image_mle.py`, and the "API layer" (`api/molecule_mle.py`) shelled out to that script **as a subprocess per PTU file** — so every consumer re-parsed CLI args, and the GUI was an old-style `QMainWindow` (drag-drop `FileListWidget` + `Worker(QThread)` running the subprocess + a `BrowserWidget` reading PNGs off disk).
