* **MMFDB — routed the demo seeder `admin/seed_example.py` through the DAO (PRD-26 burn-down).** The example-smFRET seeder is the last non-query module carrying scattered CRUD; it holds an `MFDatabase` handle throughout, so `db.dao` is in scope. Converted every existence-check-then-insert helper: the probe/experiment-type/poly-probe-position lookups (`SELECT … WHERE <non-PK/UNIQUE>` → `db.dao.list(filters=…, limit=1)`), the user/device/entity/assembly/condition/artifact/operation existence probes (`SELECT 1 WHERE <pk>` → `db.dao.get(...) is None`), and the raw inserts for `entities` + `entity_poly_seq`, `flr_entity_assembly`, `flr_sample_condition`, and `flr_poly_probe_position` → `db.dao.insert` (the last returning the autoincrement `id` via lastrowid). The `probe_types` `INSERT OR IGNORE`+SELECT-back collapsed into `dao.upsert(conflict=["type_name"])` + `dao.list`. **Correction to a documented "DAO limitation":** the two `INSERT OR REPLACE INTO mmfdb_operation_artifact` writes on the **4-column composite-PK junction** also went onto the DAO — `dao.upsert(conflict=["operation_id","artifact_id","direction","role"])` targets the full composite PK, and since the values *are* exactly those 4 columns the upsert is an idempotent `DO NOTHING` (identity-preserving, unlike the delete-and-reinsert of `INSERT OR REPLACE`). So `seed_example.py` is now **zero raw SQL**; composite-PK junctions are convertible whenever you can name the full PK as the conflict target (only *keyless* tables with no targetable UNIQUE remain a genuine limitation). Verified by **running the seeder twice** against temp DBs (both the flr-tables path and, with fabricated `.spc` fixtures, the artifact/operation/product path) — idempotent, all rows land. Standalone suite **420 passed, 1 skipped**. ## 2026-07-06
* **MMFDB — god-class breakup: `repository.py` 6,397 → 1,848 lines (−71%).** Resumed and largely completed the Phase-4 [PRD-26](/prds/prd-26.md)/[INC-05](/specs/assessment.md#inc-05) decomposition of the `MFDatabase` god-class into per-concern query mixins under `queries/`. Extracted six new mixins this pass — **ProbeMixin** (probe/spectra/optical/Förster), **SampleMixin** (samples/entities/positions/linkage), **SetupCalibMixin** (setups/calibrations), **ObjectStoreMixin** (object store + materialization), **ArtifactOpsMixin** (the artifact/operation/edge/provenance-graph core, ~46 methods), and **AnalysisMixin** (analysis runs/products/metadata) — for **13 mixins** total. Built a reusable AST-boundary method extractor (handles multi-line signatures, decorators, and leaves class attributes in place); relocated module-level helpers (`_default_reference_spectra_path`→`store/database_resolver`, `_validate_checksum`→`schema/_sqlutil`) to avoid repository↔mixin import cycles, and carried class-level method aliases (`save_setup_snapshot`, `link_operation_artifact`) into their mixins. Public `MFDatabase` surface unchanged (methods resolve via the MRO); standalone suite **419 passed** after every extraction. `repository.py` is now a thin composition over `self.conn`/`self.dao`/`self.lineage` — what remains is the legitimate core (init/connection/properties, migration, audit, vocabulary, experiment key-values, pdbx metadata).
* **MMFDB — admin GUI moved out; `adapters/` subpackage added.** Continued the clean-bounds migration: relocated the chisurf-coupled admin **GUI** (24 modules + 7 view-spec JSONs) out of the package into the ChiSurf plugin (`chisurf/plugins/core/mmfdb_admin/gui/`), replacing the compat wrappers and retargeting 38 importers + the manifest entrypoint; the admin **backend** (RPC handlers) stays in `mmfdb.admin` and is chisurf-free. Added an **`adapters/`** subpackage as the home for external-system bridges — moved `chinet_adapter` → `mmfdb.adapters.chinet` (ChiSurf fit sessions → MMFDB), with eLabFTW / electronic-lab-notebook adapters to follow; adapters keep target-system imports lazy so the core still imports standalone. Top-level `mmfdb` is now 5 files. Standalone: 419 passed. Advances [PRD-24](/prds/prd-24.md)/[PRD-48](/prds/prd-48.md).
* **MMFDB — own hermetic test suite + concern-subpackage reorg (PRD-24).** Gave the package clear bounds and a maintainable file layout. (1) **Relocated ~35 pure-MMFDB test modules** from ChiSurf `test/fio`+`test/plugins` into `modules/mmfdb/tests/` with a hermetic conftest (isolation via MMFDB's own `MMFDB_SETTINGS_DIR`, no chisurf import); the suite runs standalone (`cd modules/mmfdb && PYTHONPATH=src pytest`). This immediately exposed **real boundary violations** in the package — fixed the module-level ones (`admin/backend/services` used `from chisurf import logging` → stdlib; `{measurement,ndxplorer}_services` imported `chisurf.server.services` error helpers → new `mmfdb.admin.backend._service_errors`). ChiSurf↔MMFDB integration tests (admin GUI `MMFDBClient`, chinet/`parameter_registry` alignment, ndxplorer submodule, chisurf test-data) stay in the ChiSurf tree. (2) **Reorganized the 38 flat top-level modules into concern subpackages** — `schema/ store/ provenance/ samples/ lifecycle/ security/ project/` — leaving 6 core files at top (`repository`, `api`, `models`, `config`, `chinet_adapter`, `__init__`); collapsed the `auth/`-only-`__init__` "package" to `auth.py`. Hard cutover (no compat shims): every internal, test, and ChiSurf import retargeted to grouped paths (`mmfdb.schema.dao`, `mmfdb.provenance.result_registry`, …); fixed `Path(__file__).parent/"data"` resolution in the seven moved modules that read bundled data. **Proof of clean bounds: 419 passed with only `src` on the path** (no chisurf, no chinet). ChiSurf `test/fio`: 236 passed, 1 pre-existing failure (`test_ndxplorer_cli`, needs the ndxplorer submodule). Advances [PRD-24](/prds/prd-24.md); layout documented in [architecture/mmfdb.md](/architecture/mmfdb.md).
* **MMFDB — reimplemented external-reference persistence (PRD-39); test/fio 7 → 1.** The struct_ref/external-reference write path was lost when the SQLAlchemy `orm/` stack was deleted (Merge 1) and never reproduced on the raw-SQL path — `create_sample` recorded entities but silently dropped their UniProt/PDB cross-references and mutations. Reimplemented minimally: `sample_manager._persist_entity_external_refs` records `uniprot_accession`→`struct_ref(db_name=UNP)`, `pdb_id`→`struct_ref(db_name=PDB)`, the primary reference's sequence alignment as `struct_ref_seq`, and engineered mutations (given, or auto-diffed from `reference_sequence` via `external_refs.diff_sequences`) as `struct_ref_seq_dif`; `get_sample_full_description` now surfaces per-entity `external_refs`/`mutations`, and the probe↔mutation export validator resolves the entity via the position's `entity_id` (added to the read) with a `residue_number`/`residue_name` fallback for `seq_id`/`comp_id`. **Writes go through the dictionary-driven `DictionaryDao` (`db.dao.insert`/`list`), not hand SQL** — and codified that: raw/f-string SQL for CRUD is now documented as an **antipattern** in [architecture/mmfdb.md](/architecture/mmfdb.md) (one engine = `db.dao`; raw SQL only for bespoke joins/graph/aggregate reads). Also fixed a latent `_array_to_text` `TypeError` (np.frombuffer on a text blob) in the flrCIF export path. `test/fio` now **656 passed / 1 failed**; the lone remaining failure (`test_ndxplorer_cli`) is an out-of-scope environment issue (needs the `ndxplorer` submodule built). Advances [PRD-19](/prds/prd-19.md)/[PRD-26](/prds/prd-26.md)/[INC-05](/specs/assessment.md#inc-05).
* **MMFDB simplification — post-cutover stabilization: test/fio 179 → 7 failures.** The Merge-3 cutover exposed a broken full suite; root-caused and fixed it in committed batches. **The dominant cause (~150 cascading failures) was a single global-state leak:** `test_fcs_setup.py`'s v36-migration test set the module global `schema.SCHEMA_VERSION = 35` and never restored it, so every later test's fresh `MFDatabase` skipped the version-40 reconcile-to-`.dic` step and came up with a partial schema (missing `mmfdb_state_transition`, `mmfdb_operation.protocol_id`). Removed that obsolete test (no v36 migration exists after the PRD-19 collapse to `MIGRATIONS={1,40}`). Then fixed the independent failures: a **live default-user-id bridge** ([PRD-24](/prds/prd-24.md)) — `mmfdb.config.set_default_user_id_resolver` consulted by `configured_default_user_id`, registered from `chisurf.core.settings` to read `cs_settings` on each call (MMFDB stays standalone); a real **`get_parameter` bug** (delegated to `dao.get` on the table PK `parameter_id` instead of the `parameter_uuid` column → always None); `importer.py` `get_probe_by_id`→`get_probe`; `migrate_schema` returns no report for a fresh (from-scratch) database; and a batch of stale-reference test fixes after the cutover (flr_ext `.dic` via `MmcifDictionary.DATA_DIR`, `python -m mmfdb.pdbx_metadata`, `mmfdb.admin.backend.services`, admin session-token minting now that a fresh DB seeds a `user_default` admin, stale `get_optical_properties`/`get_spectrum` shapes, explicit plugin-manifest path). **Remaining 7:** `test_external_refs` (6) is a real **PRD-39 feature reimplementation** — struct_ref/external-reference persistence (uniprot/pdb→`struct_ref`, mutations→`struct_ref_seq_dif`, sequence auto-diff, export validation, and surfacing `external_refs`/`mutations` in `get_sample_full_description`) was lost when the SQLAlchemy `orm/` stack was deleted in Merge 1 and never reimplemented on the raw-SQL path; `test_ndxplorer_cli` (1) is an out-of-scope environment issue (needs the `ndxplorer` submodule built). Advances [INC-05](/specs/assessment.md#inc-05)/[PRD-24](/prds/prd-24.md).
* **MMFDB simplification — Merge 3 cutover landed: one import namespace, dead shim gone.** Committed the previously-uncommitted cutover in three coherent local commits. (1) Deleted the shadowed `chisurf/core/mmfdb/*.py` shim modules — the facade `__init__.py` already routes `chisurf.core.mmfdb.X → mmfdb.X` via its meta-path `_AliasFinder`, so the sibling real files were duplicate shadows whose presence broke test isolation (two `database_resolver`/event-bus copies); kept the thin facade + `auth/` + `data/`. (2) Repointed the `mmfdb_admin` plugin at the relocated `mmfdb.admin.*` package (manifest entrypoints + backend/GUI compat wrappers) and deleted the retired `sample_database` plugin. (3) Fixed a bad find-replace from the cutover that had rewritten five test modules' admin Python import paths `mmfdb_admin → mmfdb.admin`, producing the nonexistent hybrid `chisurf.plugins.core.mmfdb.admin.*` and a hard `ModuleNotFoundError` (the "3 stale reds" flagged in the Merge-1 note, actually 5) — repointed to canonical `mmfdb.admin.*`, unblocking 136 tests. Net effect: clean-HEAD `test/fio` went from **660 collection errors** (duplicate-module chaos) to a runnable suite; mmfdb-core gate still 161 passed. **Newly exposed** by the now-running suite: ~163 `test/fio` failures across three root causes — global-state test-ordering leaks, `cs_settings`-not-flowing-into-standalone-`mmfdb.config` decoupling (PRD-24, deferred), and a stale hardcoded `CREATE_TABLES_SQL` (`mmfdb_operation` missing `protocol_id`/`protocol_version` vs the canonical `_TableDef`, Phase 3). Stabilization is the next step. Advances [INC-05](/specs/assessment.md#inc-05)/[PRD-24](/prds/prd-24.md).
* **MMFDB simplification — Merge 2 in progress: god-class breakup begun.** Added identity-preserving `DictionaryDao.upsert` (INSERT…ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE — the safe write path the `add_*`/`save_*` family re-roots onto). Extracted pure JSON/time helpers into `mmfdb/_sqlutil.py` (breaks the import cycle mixins would form). Splitting the 7,322-line `MFDatabase` god-class into per-concern mixins under `mmfdb/queries/`: **ParameterMixin, StudyMixin, ProtocolMixin, LifecycleMixin, BranchMixin, UserDeviceMixin, ExperimentMixin** (7 concerns; public method surface unchanged — methods resolve via MRO, zero delegators); `repository.py` 7,322 → 6,389 lines. Shared pure helpers (`_row_to_dict`, `_exists` too) live in `_sqlutil`. Extracting LifecycleMixin exposed and fixed a latent bug in the `chisurf.core.mmfdb` compatibility facade — it set `__path__` to the vendored package, so a submodule imported through the facade before `mmfdb.X` loaded became a **duplicate module** (two event buses); a meta-path finder now aliases every `chisurf.core.mmfdb.X` to the canonical `mmfdb.X`, fulfilling the facade's "no duplicate class objects" contract. mmfdb-core gate green throughout (now 161 passed, incl. test_lifecycle/test_events). Remaining: the tightly-coupled provenance **core** (probes/spectra/forster, samples, artifacts/operations/objects/edges, provenance-graph, setups/calibrations, entities, analysis-metadata) — more entangled, best extracted carefully — then Phase 3 (fully `.dic`-generated schema) and Merge 3 (import unification, ABC drop). Advances [INC-05](/specs/assessment.md#inc-05)/[PRD-26](/prds/prd-26.md).
* **MMFDB simplification — Merge 1 (of 3) landed: one fewer access layer.** Started the MMFDB de-monolith program (finishing [PRD-19](/prds/prd-19.md)/[PRD-26](/prds/prd-26.md)/[PRD-24](/prds/prd-24.md); target [specs/mmfdb.md](/specs/mmfdb.md); backlog [INC-05](/specs/assessment.md#inc-05)). (2) Added a public API for the private helpers callers reached into — `utc_now`, `json_loads`, `get_active_database`, `session_token_registry`, `hash_token` (private names kept as-is; purely additive). (3) **Deleted the SQLAlchemy `orm/` layer** (`orm/{base,models,sample_repository,sync}.py` + `test_orm.py`, ~2,700 lines): it duplicated the raw-SQL repository over the same tables and existed mainly to be kept in sync with it. Its one production consumer (`get_sample_full_description`'s ORM branch) already had a complete raw-SQL fallback, now the sole path; the three parallel access styles are now two. Remaining (Merge 2/3): standardise on the dictionary-driven `DictionaryDao` engine, add `DictionaryDao.upsert`, split the 7,322-line `MFDatabase` god-class into per-concern mixins, make the schema fully `.dic`-generated, unify the three import paths, drop the `base.py` ABC. mmfdb-core test gate green throughout (103→118 passed). Pre-existing reds noted: 3 test files carry stale `chisurf.plugins.core.mmfdb.admin.*` imports (→ fix in the import-unification merge).

* **Amplitude scale-invariance: diagnosed, deliberately not "fixed"**: `Lifetime.amplitudes` normalises by `|a|/sum|a|` *and* the decay is autoscaled to the data (`rescale_w_bg`, enabled by fixing `n0`), so the amplitude scale is redundant twice over — n parameters, n-1 degrees of freedom. Measured directly: at equal starting amplitudes the `xL1`/`xL2` Jacobian columns are exact mirror images (|J| 398/398, 102/102, 340/340; cos = -1), the amplitude block is numerically rank 1 with the lifetime block full rank as a control, and scaling every amplitude by 3.7x leaves the model bit-identical. Overall Jacobian condition number ~1e10.
  **The obvious fix does not work, and the measurement says so.** Pinning one amplitude to remove the null direction was tested across 24 randomised starts: parameter recovery was **16/24 in both arms** — identical. It moves median chi2r 2.98 -> 1.01 (the nuisance parameters `sc`/`bg` converge better once conditioning improves; chi2r 2.98 was repeatedly observed alongside *correct* lifetimes 1.200/4.009) at ~24% more fit time, but the lifetimes are unaffected. An initial 6-start probe suggested a much larger effect and was overturned by the sweep — recorded here so the anecdote is not re-run as if it were evidence. The parameterisation is therefore left unchanged and the behaviour is pinned by `test/fitting/test_amplitude_degeneracy.py` (4 tests) instead.
  A second, distinct defect surfaced: with equal amplitudes *and* close lifetimes the fit stalls completely (only `sc` moves; `bg`/amplitudes/lifetimes frozen at their starts) — an exchange-symmetry saddle, not the scale invariance. The control case, close lifetimes with *unequal* amplitudes (0.7/0.3), converges to chi2r 3.26 where equal amplitudes give 166.6.
  Suites in isolation: 4 + 7(+1 xfail) + 10 + 17 + 13 + 9 = 60 passed. Note `test/fitting/` cannot currently be collected as a whole — `test_anisotropy_integrals.py` raises `FileNotFoundError` at collection and the combined run aborts with a fatal error; both reproduce with the new file excluded and are pre-existing.

* **`shift_array` was discontinuous and off by one — the reason fits plateaued at chi2r ~3**: Two bugs in `chisurf/core/math/signal.py`, both hitting the `timeshift` fit parameter.
  (1) *Off-by-one for fractional negative shifts.* `ts_i = int(ts)` truncates toward zero while `ts_f` used `floor()`, so below zero they disagreed: `-1.5` shifted by `-0.5`, and `-0.25` produced a **right** shift with wrapped data at index 0. Integer shifts were correct, which hid it — and the existing tests encoded the wrong values, so they had to be corrected rather than merely re-run.
  (2) *A jump discontinuity at every integer shift.* The out-of-range region was blanked via `ceil()`/`floor()`, so an infinitesimal shift zeroed an entire channel. Measured finite-difference slope diverged as `1/h`: 1e9 at h=1e-9, 1e6 at h=1e-6. Fixed by replacing only the out-of-range sample's *share* at the partial boundary channel, so the result is continuous; the implied slope is now bounded (~7) and jumps scale linearly with h.
  **(2) is why `timeshift` never moved.** It is a fit parameter starting at 0, and the fabricated slope made its Jacobian column ~1000x every other column (1.8e5 vs ~200 for the lifetimes); LM proposed a large step, it did not help, and `ts` stayed pinned at exactly 0. Profiling 24 randomised starts by cluster made this unambiguous: every fit reaching chi2r ~1 had moved `ts` off zero, every fit stuck on the chi2r ~3 plateau had `ts` = 0.0000 with otherwise *correct* lifetimes (1.200/4.009).
  **Result**: fits reaching chi2r < 1.1 went 11/24 -> 15/24 at the shipped optimiser settings, and the chi2r ~3 plateau disappeared entirely (every fit now lands at ~1.0 or clearly diverges). This also explains the chaotic behaviour seen while investigating the amplitude parameterisation — the 0.98-converges/0.90-stalls pattern and the 405-vs-230 evaluation swings were a noise-driven Jacobian, not the parameterisation.
  Verified against an independent `v[k - shift]` reference (exact, atol 1e-12) across 13 shifts and both `outside_value` settings. Suites: 4 + 7(+1 xfail) + 10 + 17 + 9 + 37 + 24 + 1 = 109 passed. Pre-existing and unrelated: `test/math/test_interp.py` and `test_interpolate_shift_empty.py` fail to collect (missing `burst_mle_analysis` plugin) and `test_datatools.py::test_distance_between_gaussian` fails — all three reproduce with the change stashed.
  **Still open**: the optimiser's `epsfcn: 0` default makes MINPACK use machine epsilon, a step below the model's numerical noise floor. With the shift fix in place, `epsfcn=1e-4` reaches 23/24 valid vs 15/24 for the default. Not changed here — it is a global default affecting every fit and is so far validated only on one two-exponential fixture.

* **GUI fit crashed with SIGABRT — a heap overrun in the periodic-convolution call**: `convolve_lifetime_spectrum_periodic` passed `stop`/`conv_stop` straight through to `tttrlib.fconv_per_cs`. Callers supply `n_points`, a *length*, while `fconv_per_cs` treats those arguments as inclusive *indices*, so the call touched one element past the end of `decay` and `irf`. Fixed by clamping both to `min(len(irf), len(decay), n_points) - 1`, matching the numba twin `convolve_lifetime_spectrum_periodic_nb`, which has always clamped — the guard was lost when the tttrlib path was switched in.
  **This was self-inflicted and the dev environment hid it.** The switch to `fconv_per_cs` was made against a locally rebuilt tttrlib whose SWIG wrapper clamps internally (`conv_stop = n_irf - 1`); the released tttrlib 0.27.0 in the canonical pixi env does not, so every fit in the GUI aborted. Measured directly on the stock build: `conv_stop = n` -> exit 134, `conv_stop = n - 1` and `n/2` -> clean, 200 repetitions clean. Classic overrun behaviour — the abort had no message and its traceback pointed at an unrelated `tobytes()` in the IRF cache, simply because that was the next allocation.
  Reproduced headlessly in the pixi env (exit 134), confirmed **not** caused by the `shift_array` change by A/B-ing that commit (crash present either way), then fixed and re-verified (exit 0, six fits complete). Regression coverage in `test/test_periodic_convolution_bounds.py` (9 tests) pins the clamp; note it only discriminates on a non-clamping tttrlib, so it was additionally verified by hand in the pixi env — passes with the fix, `AssertionError: conv_stop==n not clamped to n-1` without it.
  **Lesson: prefer fixing the caller.** The earlier tttrlib-side SWIG clamp was the right hardening but the wrong sole remedy — it made ChiSurf depend on an unreleased patch. The bounds contract now holds against any tttrlib build.
  Suites (arm64): 9 + 9 + 7(+1 xfail) + 4 + 37 + 24 + 1 = 91 passed.

* **GUI: compiled-`.ui` cache in `init_with_ui`**: `uic.loadUi` re-parses the `.ui` XML on *every* widget construction. `chisurf/gui/decorators.py` now compiles each file once (`uic.compileUi` + `exec`), caches the generated `Ui_*` class keyed on (path, mtime), and calls its `setupUi`. Per-construction cost across the 41 loadable `.ui` files in the tree: **3.40 ms -> 0.52 ms**. End to end, `LinePlot` went **~20.9 -> ~19.2 ms (8%)**, measured as the median of 10 constructions in three *separate* processes.
  **The trap, which I hit:** `pyuic5`'s `setupUi` parents the widgets to the target but binds them as attributes of the **Ui_ instance** (`self.lineEdit`), whereas `loadUi(path, target)` binds them on the *target*. The first version swapped one for the other directly — the widget tree was identical and every `self.<widget>` in widget code would have raised `AttributeError`. Caught by sweeping all 53 `.ui` files and comparing child names *and* bound attributes: 41 identical, 0 different, 7 fall back (do not compile), 5 that `loadUi` cannot load either. Covered by `test/gui/test_ui_compile_cache.py` (45 passed, 12 skipped), including mtime invalidation and graceful fallback for missing/malformed files.
  **Honest trade-off:** compiling costs about as much as one `loadUi` (3.51 vs 3.40 ms), so break-even is **1.22 constructions per file**. A widget built exactly once is ~0.63 ms slower; anything built twice or more wins. Plots are rebuilt per fit, so they win; once-only dialogs pay a little. Precompiling with `pyuic5` at build time would remove the one-time cost entirely and is the better long-term answer.
  **Not pursued:** `LinePlot`'s three `PlotWidget`s (the dominant remaining cost) cannot be created lazily — all three are simultaneously visible in a `DockArea` (Residuals / A.corr. residuals / Data), not tabbed.
  **Method note:** cProfile again over-attributed — it credited `load_ui` 6.4 ms of a 21 ms `LinePlot`, while direct timing showed 1.84 ms. And an in-process `git stash` A/B gave a corrupted comparison (a stale `PlotWidget` C++ object); the reliable measurement was separate processes.
  Suites: 45(+12 skipped) + 3 + 4 + 9 + 7(+1 xfail) + 37 = 105 passed.

* **"The plot does not update reliably" — a missing `editingFinished` in `ScientificDoubleSpinBox`**: `ScientificDoubleSpinBox` subclasses `QAbstractSpinBox` and manages its own value, connecting the **inner QLineEdit's** `editingFinished` to its text commit. That is a *different signal object* from the spin box's own `editingFinished`, and `QAbstractSpinBox` does not forward it for such a subclass. Every `FittingParameterWidget` connects to the spin box's `editingFinished` — `parameter_widgets.py` contains **zero** `valueChanged`/`sigValueChanged` connections — so committing typed text updated the display and emitted `sigValueChanged` but never reached the model.
  **Why it looked intermittent rather than broken**: stepping with the arrows worked, because `stepBy()` emits `editingFinished` explicitly; typing a value and pressing Enter (the common interaction) left the model, the fit and the plots on the old number. Measured on all three paths before the fix — arrows: propagated; typing+Enter: **not propagated**; programmatic `setValue`: not propagated. The last is deliberate and kept: that is how fit results are pushed back into the widgets, and emitting there would write the value straight back into the model.
  Fixed by re-emitting `self.editingFinished` in `_commit_text`, correcting a comment that asserted the opposite. Covered by `test/gui/test_spinbox_editing_finished.py` (9 tests, incl. two end-to-end asserting the `FittingParameter` follows the widget); verified to discriminate — **3 failed without the fix, 9 pass with it**.
  **Measured widget overhead (no change made)**: `FittingParameterWidget` costs **0.43–0.50 ms** each, against 0.035 ms for its spin box and 0.046 ms for the `FittingParameter` — the remainder is `_build_layout` (0.16 ms, 31%), three spin boxes (0.13 ms, 25%) and general Qt construction, with nothing pathological to remove. It matters at scale: a 128-exponential FRET model has ~256 parameters, i.e. **~128 ms** of widget construction. The lever there is virtualizing the parameter panel (not building widgets for off-screen parameters), which is a substantially larger change and is not attempted here.
  Suites: 9 + 45(+12 skipped) + 3 + 4 + 11 + 9 + 7(+1 xfail) = 88 passed.

* **Prior settings moved inline into the parameter popup**: the per-parameter popup showed a prior radio row, a `repr()` summary and an **Edit...** button that opened a *second* modal (`PriorEditorDialog`, "Advanced prior settings") just to set mu/sigma. The distribution selector and its parameter spin boxes are now built directly into `FittingParameterDetailPopup`: choosing a family from a `Distribution:` combo rebuilds the parameter form in place, and editing a spin box applies the prior immediately — no OK button, no second window. `PriorEditorDialog` is **removed** (91 lines) rather than deprecated.
  Families with no editable parameter list (`CallablePrior`, `ProductPrior`) fall back to a read-only summary label instead of pretending to be editable. The quick radios (Box / Gaussian / Log-normal / Custom) are kept as one-click shortcuts; "Custom" now just reveals the full family list rather than launching a dialog. Minor redundancy noted and left: with Gaussian selected both the radio and the combo read "Gaussian".
  The two tests covering the deleted dialog were **ported, not dropped** — `test/gui/test_parameter_prior_widget.py` now asserts the inline behaviour (spin boxes appear, edits apply without an OK, switching family in the combo applies it, the editor hides for Box, an existing prior populates the form, and an uneditable prior degrades to a summary). Verified visually with offscreen `QWidget.grab()` renders for the box/Gaussian/Gamma states.
  Suites: 14 + 9 + 45(+12 skipped) + 3 + 4 + 11 = 86 passed.

* **Amplitude parameterisation fixed: one amplitude per `Lifetime` is held out of the optimiser**. Amplitudes are normalised by `|a|/sum|a|`, so scaling them all is a no-op — n parameters, n-1 degrees of freedom. `Lifetime._update_redundant_amplitude()` now marks the first free amplitude `redundant` whenever normalisation is on and no amplitude already pins the scale; `FittingParameterGroup.parameters` excludes redundant parameters alongside fixed and linked ones.
  **`redundant` is a new concept, deliberately not `fixed`.** `Lifetime.update()` writes the normalised values back into the parameters, and a `fixed` parameter *ignores writes* — marking it fixed would make the stored amplitudes drift away from the normalised ones on every update. A redundant parameter is still written; it is simply not varied. Idempotence of `update()` is pinned by a test.
  **Result** (two-exponential fixture): `cond(J)` **5.49e7 -> 554**, amplitude uncertainty **4,801,893% -> 1.8%**, with chi2r (1.014 -> 1.017), the lifetimes (4.0089/1.2007 -> 4.0089/1.2003) and the amplitude ratio (0.694384 -> 0.693764) all unchanged. The fitted answer is the same; only its uncertainty becomes meaningful.
  **Held-out amplitudes still report an uncertainty**: `Fit._propagate_redundant_error_estimates` derives it from the sum-to-one constraint as `sqrt(sum of the siblings' covariance block)`, off-diagonal terms included — they dominate, because the fractions are strongly anti-correlated. Verified exact for n=2, where sigma(x1) must equal sigma(x2): both 0.00792584. Without this the held-out parameter would keep a *stale* estimate from an earlier fit, which is worse than none.
  Edge cases covered: a single component holds out its only amplitude (its normalised value is always 1, so it carries no freedom at all); an amplitude the user already fixed pins the scale and nothing is held out; three components keep two degrees of freedom; disabling `normalize_amplitudes` frees all of them; `pop()` clears the flag on the detached parameter and reassigns.
  **This supersedes the earlier "measured, deliberately not fixed" entry.** That assessment scored the degeneracy by parameter recovery (16/24 either way) and chi2r, found no difference, and concluded it was not worth fixing — the wrong metric. The degeneracy does not harm convergence, it destroys the *error estimates*, which is what gets published; the user's own fit showed `xL1 +-62.4%` / `xL2 +-62.1%`. `test/fitting/test_amplitude_degeneracy.py` is retired into `test/fitting/test_amplitude_redundancy.py` (13 tests), keeping the two tests that document *why* the hold-out is needed.
  Regressions: 13 + 7(+1 xfail) + 10 + 17 + 9 + 9 + 14 + 9 + 37 = 125 passed. Pre-existing and unrelated failures (identical with the change stashed): `test_fret_line.py` 6, `test_user_models.py` 2, `test_forbidden_communication.py` 3.

* **Optimiser `epsfcn` default: machine epsilon -> 1e-6**: MINPACK derives its forward-difference step as `sqrt(epsfcn) * |x|`, and `epsfcn: 0` means "use machine epsilon" — a step of ~1.5e-8 relative. That is far below the noise floor of the decay model, whose convolution is recursive over ~1024 channels, so the Jacobian columns came back dominated by rounding noise and the optimiser could fail to move the lifetimes **at all** (they stayed at *exactly* their starting values while only the nuisance parameters moved).
  Re-measured from scratch after the `shift_array` and amplitude fixes, since both changed the landscape — the earlier numbers recommending 1e-4 were stale, and with better conditioning a much smaller step now suffices. Over **88 randomised fits across four independent conditions** (three start seeds, a different noise realisation, and a 3-exponential model), fits reaching chi2r < 1.1: `epsfcn=0` **60/88**, 1e-10 81/88, 1e-8 82/88, **1e-6 83/88**. 1e-6 was no worse than any alternative in any single condition, for roughly 10-30% more time; 1e-2 regressed (18/24), so the improvement is not monotonic in step size.
  **The bundled default alone would have been inert.** `copy_settings_to_user_folder` uses `if not destination_file.exists()`, so user settings are copied to `~/.chisurf` once and **never refreshed** — every existing install carries the old `epsfcn: 0`, which is exactly the broken value. `Fit._leastsq_options()` therefore treats 0 (MINPACK's "pick for me") as unset and substitutes `DEFAULT_EPSFCN`; explicit non-zero values pass through, so a machine-epsilon step stays reachable by asking for it. Verified through the real `fit.run()` path with `epsfcn: 0` still on disk: **16/24 -> 23/24 valid**.
  **Bonus: this also fixed the at-the-bound defect** tracked as a strict xfail (`test_fit_does_not_destroy_a_good_solution`). A parameter sitting exactly on a bound has zero derivative under the sin/arcsin transform, and `scatter` defaults to 0.0 with bounds (0.0, 100.0) — exactly there; starting at the optimum drove it 0 -> 7.8 and chi2r 1.55 -> 1765. With a ~1.5e-8 probe the difference quotient never left the flat region, so the derivative really was zero; at a 1e-3 relative step it reaches far enough to see the real slope. The xfail is retired (now `8 passed`), with the explanation kept as a comment. No change to the bound handling was needed.
  Covered by `test/fitting/test_leastsq_epsfcn_default.py` (8 tests: zero treated as unset, missing key filled, explicit values honoured, garbage tolerated, other options untouched, the global settings dict not mutated, and end-to-end that the lifetimes actually move from a start that previously froze).
  Suites: 8 + 8 + 13 + 2 + 3 + 8 + 9 + 3 = 54 passed, plus 71 (`test_fitting_client`), 9 (`test_decay_fit`), 14 (`test_fret_species_decay`), 13 (`test_view_spec`), 17, 10, 9 — all green.

## 2026-07-05
* **SV-03 fix**: Routed `chisurf.*` globals through `ChiSurfAPI` `SessionState` — API owns `self._state` aliasing the global lists, all API methods use `self._state.fits`/`self._state.datasets` instead of direct `cs.*` access. 84 tests pass. 12 total fixed.
* **DATA-03 fix**: Consolidated duplicated `mmfdb_*` DDL into single-source `_TableDef`/`_Column` dataclasses — both permissive (migration) and CHECK-constrained (fresh-DB) DDL are generated from `_CANONICAL_TABLE_DEFS`. Removed hand-written `_CANONICAL_CHECK_SQL` and fragile string-matching `_CANONICAL_TABLE_MAP`. 2 guardrail tests. 11 total fixed.
* **DATA-02 fix**: Added ordered migration waterfall to MMFDB schema versioning — `MIGRATIONS` `OrderedDict` (v1 fresh DB, v40 reconcile), `migrate_schema()` reads stored version and applies pending steps sequentially. 6 tests. 10 total fixed.
* **SV-04 fix**: Promoted service errors to the JSON-RPC `error` member — `ZmqServer._handle_one()` converts `ok: False` handler results to proper JSON-RPC error responses. `ChisurfClient.call()` now raises `RemoteError` for both application and transport errors. 14 tests updated. 9 total fixed.
* **S2 fixes**: Landed 3 S2 items — SV-02 (deleted dead DTO dataclasses), SV-05 (event topic contract in `server_methods.json` + guardrail), BUG-04 (`Model` ABCMeta enforcement). Updated assessment statuses.
* **S1 fixes**: Landed all 5 S1 items from the [assessment backlog](/specs/assessment.md) — BUG-01 (NCurve `elif` guard), BUG-02 (stale `fit_select` removed), BUG-03 (`component_type` NameError), SV-01 (Qt-free `chisurf/core/data_io/detector_setups.py`), DATA-01 (3 broken manifests + validation logging). Each committed separately with tests. Added guardrail tests: `test_no_duplicate_handler_names` (BUG-02), `test_no_gui_import_in_server` (SV-01), `test_builtin_manifests_all_valid` (DATA-01). Updated assessment statuses. Created `chisurf/core/data_io/` package.
* **Population**: Expanded OKF coverage from the code tree: added subsystem concepts for [fluorescence domain](/subsystems/fluorescence-domain.md), [project persistence](/subsystems/project-persistence.md), and [pipelines](/subsystems/pipeline.md); added plugin-group concepts for [TTTR tools](/plugins/tttr.md), [trajectory tools](/plugins/trajectory.md), and [sample/spectra/curation](/plugins/sample-spectra.md). Updated subsystem/plugin indexes and cross-links so existing populated pages are discoverable.
* **Integration**: Added the [prds](/prds/index.md) group — one OKF concept per PRD (persistent `PRD-NN` number as stable id, status + phase frontmatter) integrated from the `overhaul/` folder, which is kept intact as the authoritative full text (each concept's `resource` points back). References to external/third-party software are described by role, not named. Index grouped by implementation phase.
* **Migration**: Moved the former top-level `SPECS/` target specs into the OKF bundle as the [specs](/specs/index.md) group — `overview`, `core`, `rpc`, `mmfdb`, `plugins`, the [assessment](/specs/assessment.md) backlog, and the [template](/specs/template.md). Converted each to an OKF concept (frontmatter + bundle-relative links) and cross-linked the target specs to their current-state concepts. Removed `SPECS/`.
* **Initialization**: Created the ChiSurf OKF bundle — root [index](/index.md), [overview](/overview.md), and the `architecture/`, `subsystems/`, `workflows/`, and `references/` groups.
